# Roadmap: theta chains on definite unitary groups

**Summary.** This roadmap builds a chain of theta lifts between definite unitary groups over an abelian CM field, together with every object the chain is made from. The host `E` is a finite abelian extension of `ℚ` with an imaginary quadratic subfield `K = ℚ(√−d)`, `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)` a prime, maximal totally real subfield `F = E⁺` and the CM type `Φ = Gal(E/K)` induced from `K`; every prime other than `d` that ramifies in `E` splits in `K`, and the inertia group at `d` has order `2` and does not contain complex conjugation, so `E/F` is unramified at every finite place. The chain is built on the cyclic hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, whose auxiliary pair has `q ≡ 3 (mod 8)` and `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)` and whose layer is `n ≥ 2`. The column spaces `X_j = E^{N_j}`, `N_j = k + j(t + 1)` (`0 ≤ j ≤ m`), carry the standard Hermitian form, and step `j` of the chain is the theta lift along the homomorphism pair `Hom_E(X_{j−1}, X_j)` with the skew element `δ_T = −1/√−d`, a normal form in which the Harish-Chandra parameters of the earlier steps persist.

The headline theorems are these.

1. *Hecke characters with prescribed data* (2.4 (a)). Let `E/F` be a CM extension unramified at every finite place, `η = η_{E/F}`, `n_η ∈ {0, 1}`, `S_θ` a finite set of finite places of `F` split in `E`, with a place `w_v | v` and a continuous character `θ_v` of `𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^×` for each `v ∈ S_θ`, a CM type `Φ` of `E`, and integers `m_τ ≡ n_η (mod 2)` for `τ ∈ Φ`. A unitary Hecke character of `E` that restricts to `η^{n_η}` on `𝔸_F^×`, is unramified outside the places above `S_θ`, restricts to `θ_v` and `θ_v^{−1}` on the units at `w_v` and `w̄_v`, and has type `(z/|z|)^{m_τ}` at `τ`, exists if and only if an explicit product of these data equals `1` at every `x ∈ E^×` whose ideal is generated by a fractional ideal of `F`. The criterion in this generality, with its "only if" direction, is this roadmap's own formulation; [FormalProof] establishes the existence of the characters of 2.4 (b) for the hosts of the chain, which is the case of the "if" direction used later.
2. *The step is a theta lift* (3.3, 3.4). The step operator `K_j(ξ)`, a class sum of jets of theta functions of CM abelian varieties, maps algebraic modular forms of weight `Λ_{j−1}` on `U(X_{j−1})` to forms of weight `Λ_j` on `U(X_j)`. Up to a nonzero constant it is the class-set theta lift of the pair `(X_{j−1}^∨, δ_TX_j)` with the splitting characters `(μ_j(ξ), μ_j(ξ))`, and at a split place of odd residue characteristic where all data are unramified it sends a Hecke eigenform of Satake parameter `α` to one whose parameter is `α` together with the segment of the step, without inversion.
3. *Integrality* (3.6, 3.10). The least common multiple `D_Γ` of the orders of the stabilisers of all classes divides `minkowskiBound(2fN_m)`. If at a finite place `𝔭` the kernel of every step maps integral values to integral values, then `D_Γ^m` times the chain lift maps integral forms of level `0` to integral forms of level `m` at `𝔭`; and the top period of a form of level `m` with values in the Weyl lattices against a form on `U(X^h)` with values in the dual Weyl lattices has valuation at least `−e_𝔭·v_{p(𝔭)}(D_Γ)` at every finite place `𝔭` for the base tuple, and, for a tuple `ξ` of twisting characters, at every finite place `𝔭` whose residue characteristic lies below no twist place of `ξ`.
4. *Source lattices* (3.8). If `2fk + 1 < g₀` for the type prime `g₀`, every class of the column level of a word `u` has trivial stabiliser; the lattice `M_u` of integral new forms has rank `d*_u·(T(u)^k − 1)^f·((T(u) − 2)/(T(u) − 1))^f`, where `T(u)` is the level prime of the word, and its slope is that of the lattice of all integral forms minus `f·log(T(u) − 1)/(T(u) − 2)`.

The hosts and their predicates come from [FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums](../FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums/README.md); the classification of Hermitian spaces, class sets and masses from [HermitianFormsAndLattices](../HermitianFormsAndLattices/README.md); the dictionary of the theta lift of a definite pair from [ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups](../ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups/README.md), and the global Weil representation of a unitary pair from [ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs](../ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs/README.md); integral branching from [IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups](../IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups/README.md); slopes from [SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles](../SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles/README.md). The later roadmaps of the chain in the family [RiemannHypothesisForDirichletLFunctions](../README.md), from [LocalGainOfThetaChains](../LocalGainOfThetaChains/README.md) to [ThetaChainsAndFrobeniusPrimeSums](../ThetaChainsAndFrobeniusPrimeSums/README.md), work with these data, the chain lift, its source lattices and its top period.

Layer 2 constructs the setting: the ranks, scales and named size predicates (2.1); the column, row and model spaces (2.2) and the normal form of the pairs they form, with its conjugate datum and the conventions for lifts and descents (2.2a); weight data and words, adapted and separated data and their existence (2.3–2.3c); the Hecke characters of the chain and the twisting characters (2.4, 2.5); the coefficient field `𝕂`, explicit and of polynomial size, and the field of places `𝕃` (2.6); the type and auxiliary primes and the level prime of each word, chosen so that the sources nearly have the dimension of the target (2.7, 2.8); and the level structures at the unramified places, the type primes, the level primes and the twist places (2.9–2.9c). Layer 3 builds the column chain: chain forms with their integral structures (3.1), a family of theta functions of CM abelian varieties with its integrality at every prime (3.1a–3.1d), the step operator and its identification with a class-set theta lift (3.2–3.4), the chain lift and its integrality (3.5, 3.6), the operators at the level prime (3.7), the source lattices (3.8), and the top period with its integrality (3.9, 3.10).

## Conventions

**Groups and indices.** Galois groups are written multiplicatively: for `g ∈ G` and subsets `S, S′ ⊂ G`,
`gS = {gs : s ∈ S}` and `SS′ = {ss′ : s ∈ S, s′ ∈ S′}`. Places of `Φ` and tops are written `τ`; the layer of the cyclotomic
`ℤ₂`-tower of a cyclic host is `n`; the steps of the chain are `j = 1, …, m`; `k` is the base rank and `t` the rank step (2.1).

**Fields.** `E` is the host (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1): finite abelian over `ℚ` with an imaginary quadratic subfield `K`; `F = E⁺` its
maximal totally real subfield, `f = [F:ℚ]`; `c ∈ G` complex conjugation; `χ_K` the quadratic character of `K`; `f_E` the
conductor of `E` (the conductor of a primitive character `χ` is `f_χ`); `h_E` the class number of `E`, `μ_E` its
group of roots of unity and `rd_E` its root discriminant. Under `SplitRamification d` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3) `K` is ramified only at the prime `d`; on the cyclic hosts
(FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4) `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)`, `K = ℚ(√−d)`, the layer is `n ≥ 2`, and a second imaginary quadratic field
`ℚ(√−q) ⊂ E`, `q ≡ 3 (mod 8)`, is used; `(q, d)` is the *auxiliary pair*. `HostSplitting E` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3)
contains `4 | f`, so `f ≥ 4` for the hosts that satisfy it. The fields
`𝕂 ⊂ 𝕃` are the two fields of 2.6: the *coefficient field* `𝕂`, an explicit number field containing `E`, and the *field of
places* `𝕃`, a finite Galois extension of `ℚ` inside `ℂ`, stable under complex conjugation and containing `𝕂`; `σ₀ : 𝕃 ↪ ℂ` is
the inclusion (on `E` it is `ι₀`) and `c_𝕃` the restriction of complex conjugation. The finite places of `𝕃` are written `𝔭`,
with residue characteristic `p(𝔭)`, ramification index `e_𝔭` and weight `ϱ(𝔭) = log N𝔭/[𝕃:ℚ]` (so
`Σ_{𝔭|p} ϱ(𝔭)e_𝔭 = log p`). Embeddings `𝕃 ↪ ℂ` are written `σ`.

**CM type.** An embedding `ι₀ : E ↪ ℂ` is fixed; `Φ = Gal(E/K)` is identified with the CM type `{ι₀∘g : g ∈ Φ}` induced
from `K` (`|Φ| = f`). Elements of `Φ` are called places. On a cyclic host `G = A × ⟨c⟩ × ⟨σ_d⟩` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 (iv)). When
`K = ℚ(√−d)`, `√−d ∈ K` denotes the root with `Im ι₀(√−d) > 0`, so that `Im τ(√−d) > 0` for every `τ ∈ Φ`.

**Primes of the construction.** The *type primes* `g₀, g₁` (2.7) carry the depth-zero types; the *auxiliary primes*
`p_δ, p′_δ` carry the characters `δ, δ′` (2.4, 2.7); the primes `q, d` form the *auxiliary pair* (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4), and are not
called auxiliary primes; the *level prime* `T(u) = N(π_u)` of a word `u` (2.8) carries the new forms. The *twist places*
`Q(ξ)` of a tuple `ξ` of twisting characters are its full ramification set (2.5). Twisting involves two sets (2.5): the
*admissible twisting characteristics* `𝒮_tw`, the primes at which twisting is permitted, and the *twisted places* `Tw`,
a finite `c_𝕃`-stable set of places of `𝕃` above `𝒮_tw`, fixed with the twisting datum (2.5), outside which the tuple
`ξ_𝔭` used is the base tuple; sums "over the twisted places" run over `Tw`.

**Hermitian forms, definiteness and measures.** Hermitian forms are conjugate-linear in the first variable (the
convention of HermitianFormsAndLattices and ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups). Definiteness is that of
HermitianFormsAndLattices: a space is *definite at* a real place if its form has one sign there, *totally definite* if
it is definite at every real place (the sign may depend on the place), and *totally positive definite* if every sign is
`+`; a *definite unitary group* is the unitary group of a totally definite space. The column spaces `X_j`, the model
spaces `V_j` and the hyperplane `X^h ⊂ X_m` are totally positive definite (2.2). On `U(V)(𝔸_f)` the stabiliser of the
chosen self-dual lattice has volume `1`; for totally definite `V`, `∫_{[U(V)]} φ = Σ_𝒸 |Γ_𝒸|^{−1} φ(g_𝒸)` over the
classes `𝒸` (representatives `g_𝒸`, stabilisers `Γ_𝒸`), so the total volume is the mass; for indefinite `V` the
archimedean measure is transferred from the compact inner form, and formal degrees are taken for this measure. The
convention for the first variable and the class weights `|Γ_𝒸|^{−1}` are those of [FormalProof],
`Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataKernels.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.ColumnChain.wt`).

**The CM period.** `log Ω := γ + log(2π/rd_E) − (L′/L)(1, χ_K)`, `γ` Euler's constant ([FormalProof],
`Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataParams₂.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.SizeData₂.logOm`),
`Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataHost.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.Host.v`), `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataHost.lean`
(`Formalism.BasicNotions.Host.ka`)). Equivalently
`log Ω = log Ω_K − log(rd_E/√d)`, where `log Ω_K = γ + log(2π/√d) − (L′/L)(1, χ_K)` is the mean over the ideal
classes of `K` of `log(area(Λ)/π) + (1/6) log|Δ(Λ)|` (Chowla–Selberg).

**Theta lifts between definite groups.** The step of the column chain from level `j − 1` to level `j` is a definite dual
pair in the homomorphism model (3.2): the symplectic space is `𝕎_j := Hom_E(X_{j−1}, X_j)`, on which `(g, h)` acts by
`x ↦ h∘x∘g^{−1}`, with the alternating form `Tr_{E/F}(δ_T·tr(x^†y))`, the scaling element `δ_T := √−d/d = −1/√−d` (2.2),
and the standard additive character of `𝔸_F/F` (equivalently the form `Tr_{E/F}(δ_T·tr(y^†x))` with the complex
conjugate character). Every pair of spaces of the chain (column steps, row steps, model steps) is a homomorphism pair in
this normal form (2.2a; skew element `δ_T`, or a positive rational multiple of it for the other pairs formed from the
spaces of the chain); lifts integrate against the theta kernel, and the row descents integrate against its complex
conjugate at the complex conjugate vector, `θ̄_{S,T}(·; Φ) = conj θ_{S,T}(·; conj Φ)` for the test vector `Φ` (equivalently,
they are linear theta integrals of the conjugate datum: skew element `−δ_T`, inverse splitting characters, 2.2a (b)).
`x^†` is the adjoint of a homomorphism of Hermitian spaces. In the conventions of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups
(standard character, symplectic form built from the two Hermitian forms and a fixed purely imaginary element, here taken
to be `δ_T`, which is not the `√−d` of this roadmap) this is the homomorphism pair of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §0.1 whose first space is the
dual `X_{j−1}^∨` (on which `U(X_{j−1})` acts contragrediently) and whose second space is `X_j` with the skew form `δ_T⟨,⟩`,
read through the dictionary ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5. Since `Im τ(δ_T) > 0` for `τ ∈ Φ`, the embedding which that roadmap attaches to
the real place below `τ` is the conjugate embedding `c∘τ`, with sign `−1` and complex structure `(c∘τ)(i)`. Translation:
a weight `λ` of that roadmap on `U(X_{j−1})` is the weight `λ` at `τ`; a weight `λ` on `U(X_j)` is the dual weight
`λ^∨ = (−λ_n, …, −λ_1)` at `τ` (the rule "dual weight at `τ`" holds for the second group only); an infinity type `s` of
that roadmap is `−s` at `τ`. With the infinity types `−2w_j(τ)` of both groups at `τ`, the dictionary gives the Fock types
`(V_{μ+N_j/2−w_j(τ)})^∨ ⊗ V_{(μ,0)+N_{j−1}/2−w_j(τ)}` at `τ` (a half-integer added to a weight is added to each entry),
the dual of the first module tensor the second, and, at the split places where the splitting characters are unramified,
the Satake relation of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5 (d) with constant `1`, without inversion. The theta functions of Layer 3 are the complex
conjugates of the Bargmann readings of that roadmap (whose CM type is `cΦ`): they are holomorphic in the `Φ`-coordinates.
The weights and infinity types of Layers 2–3 are written at the places of `Φ`.

**Integral structures.** For a dominant weight `λ`, `∇_ℤ(λ)` is the dual Weyl lattice (the integral polynomials of the
polynomial model of 3.1, spanned by products of minors) and `Δ_ℤ(λ) ⊂ ∇_ℤ(λ)` the Weyl lattice, its dual for the
invariant form; the product of leading minors `v_λ` lies in both. The column side of the chain (the integral forms
`ℳ_j^{int}` of 3.1, the chain lift, the source lattices of 3.8 and their restriction to `U(X^h)`) takes values in `Δ_ℤ`,
in the coordinates of a basis of the dual class lattice; the row forms `Υ` on `U(X^h)` paired with it take values in
`∇_ℤ` (the forms `ℳ^∨`), so that every class term of the pairing is integral and the only loss is the stabiliser
orders (3.6, 3.10). At the top, a class `𝒸` of `U(X^h)` gives the class `𝒸̃` of level `m` with class lattice
`L_𝒸 ⊕ 𝒪_E z_u`, `z_u = π_u e_{N_m}`, and the restriction to `U(X^h)` is integral at every finite place (3.9).

**Petersson products.** For algebraic modular forms with values in an algebraic representation `V_λ`:
`⟨φ, φ′⟩ = Σ_𝒸 |Γ_𝒸|^{−1}⟨φ(𝒸), φ′(𝒸)⟩_λ`, with `⟨,⟩_λ` the invariant Hermitian form normalised by `⟨v_λ, v_λ⟩ = 1` on
the product of leading minors `v_λ` of the polynomial model of 3.1 (it lies in both lattices `Δ_ℤ ⊂ ∇_ℤ`); the chain
forms are functions of frames, and their coefficient module in Gross's model is `V_λ^∨` (3.1).

**Slopes.** Hermitian lattices are finitely generated `𝒪_𝕃`-submodules of full rank in Hermitian `𝕃`-spaces with a
metric at every complex embedding. This roadmap writes `μ(L̄) := 2μ̂(L̄)`, twice Bost's normalised slope
(`μ̂ = deĝ/([𝕃:ℚ]·rk)`), so `μ(𝒪_𝕃^n, standard) = 0`; `μ_max`, `μ_min` likewise. For a lattice `L` and a basis `e` of
`L ⊗ 𝕃`, `−rk L·μ(L) = mean_σ log det Gram_σ(e) − 2Σ_𝔭 ϱ(𝔭)·idx_𝔭(L : ⟨e⟩)`, `idx_𝔭` the signed length at `𝔭` (no
factor `2` on the mean). SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles uses `μ̂`; this paragraph is the dictionary, and its statements
are homogeneous in it.

**Hecke characters.** A Hecke character `μ` of `E` (2.4) has the value `μ(𝔓)` at a prime `𝔓` of `E` prime to its
conductor: its value at a uniformizer of `E_𝔓`.

**Indices of the chain.** Step `j` is the column lift `U(X_{j−1}) → U(X_j)` with Hecke character `μ_j` and step weight
`w_j = e_{j−1}` (2.3), the row descent `U(Y_j) → U(Y_{j−1})` (against the conjugate kernel `θ̄`, 2.2a), and the model
lift between `U(V_j)` and `U(W_j)`. Columns grow by `t + 1`, rows by `t`.

**Characters and levels at the type primes.** For a type prime `g ∈ {g₀, g₁}` the prime `π_g𝒪_K` of `K` above `g` is
fixed, and for a place `v` of `F` above `g`, `w_v` is the place of `E` above `v` and `π_g` (residue field `𝔽_g`). The
*type characters* of step `j` are `χ_{v,j} := ω_g^{2j}` on `𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^×`, `ω_g` a fixed generator of the characters of
`𝔽_g^×`, the same for all `v | g` and all `j` (2.4 (b)): squares, of depth zero, read as elements of `𝔽_g^×` through the
identification of 2.9a (its tame condition). The step character `μ_j` is tamely ramified there, with residue
`χ_{v,j}^{−1}` at `w_v` and `χ_{v,j}` at `w̄_v`. The levels of 2.9a carry these characters: on `U(V_j)` the character
`χ_{v,j}^{−1}` of the determinant of the `t`-block, and on the row stage of `Y_j` the character
`χ^{fl}_j = ∏_{i≤j} χ_{v,i}^{−1}` of the determinants of the added blocks. A *level* at the type primes is a pair
`(P, χ_P)` of a subgroup `P` of the finite group `G₀` and a character `χ_P` of `P`; the rows are formed at principal
level and the levels cut out their parts (base level `(P_0, χ_{P_0})`, row type level `(P_1, χ_{P_1})`, 2.9a). At the
twist places the levels carry `c_v(det of the lower block)`, `c_v = (ξ_{w_v}|_{𝒪^×})^{s}`, with exponent `+1` (2.9c); the
rule for all bad places is that of 2.9 (the residues `c_{i,v}` of the step characters). The
splitting character `η̃` and the twisting characters are unramified at the type places, and `μ_j`, `δ`, `δ′` are
unramified outside the *bad places* `S₀`, the places above `g₀, g₁, p_δ, p′_δ` (2.4 (b)).

**Periods and pairings.** The top period `P̃_ξ(φ, Υ)` (3.9) is linear in the column `φ` and conjugate-linear in the
row form `Υ`. Equivalently, it is a bilinear pairing applied to the row form with conjugated coefficients: for a column
form `φ′` of level `m`, `P̃(φ′, Υ) = P^{bil}(φ′, Υ^c)`, where `P^{bil}` is `𝕃`-bilinear (built from the `ℚ`-bilinear
contravariant forms `β_λ` whose Hermitian forms are those of 3.1) and `Υ^c`, the *coefficient conjugate* of `Υ`, is a form
for the CM type `cΦ` with the conjugate character (3.9). The conjugation is that of the coefficients of the row form,
never of the column or of the place at which the column is bounded: a bound for the period at a place `𝔭` of `𝕃` uses
the column at `𝔭` and the row form at `c_𝕃(𝔭)` (`Υ^c` is integral at `𝔭` exactly when `Υ` is integral at `c_𝕃(𝔭)`;
3.10).

**Routes.** The mathematics of this roadmap follows a complete formal proof ([FormalProof]); every target marked *NEW*
states what that proof establishes; where a target is stated in greater generality, its status line says so. Where a
route is not given, or not given in full, the target says so in its route. The
conditions (T1), (T2) of 2.5 are conditions on the twisting datum.

**Statements of the formal proof.** Where a target names [FormalProof], the form that proof establishes is recorded at the target: its
hypotheses, ranges and constants are those of the proof, including restrictions that the route written here does not
visibly use. A wider or sharper form stated beside it is this roadmap's own and is not in [FormalProof]; it is proved by
the route written here, except at the steps that the route names as not given, which [FormalProof] proves only in the
range recorded at the target (3.1d, 3.3).

**Quantifier order and sizes.** Constants named before `ε` do not depend on `ε`; the scale `x` and everything chosen at
it come after `ε`. Between them come the numbers that may depend on `ε`: the layer exponent `A_n` (`2 + 8ε` in the
construction, at most the constant `A_max`), the window constant `J` and the exponent `c_H` of the slope range, which
the size predicates of 2.1 take as arguments, and the exponent `c_Δ` of the letter step (2.3) and the exponent `C_S` of
`SizeSc` (2.3b). The size conditions are named predicates: those of 2.1, grouped into the
host predicates `SizeA`, `SizeN`, `SizeD`, `SizeRd`, `SizeQD`, `SizeInert`, `SizeDep` and the parameter predicates `SizeM`,
`SizeK`, `SizeW`, `SizeMf`, `SizeT`, `SizeTheta`, with their conjunction `Size(x, ε)`; and those named where their
quantities are defined: `SizeSc` (2.3b), `SizeGap`, `SizeSep` (2.3c), `SizeG` (2.7), `SizeT_u` (2.8: `16mx^ε ≤ log T*(u)` and
`log T*(u) + 1 ≤ C_T m x^ε ≤ x`). `SizeDep` uses the set `S_small` and the number `vd` of 2.1 and a constant `C_dep`
fixed before `ε`. "The size conditions of 2.1" means `Size(x, ε)`; a target that uses a single predicate names it.

**Constants.** Exponents, window ratios, the shares of the tops, the bound `t ≤ x^{C_t}`, the budgets and the
admissible range of `ε` are parameters with their true constraints. The exponents written in the size predicates of 2.1
are fixed choices. The values of one construction (such as `C_t = 401`) appear only in Checks.

### Table of symbols

| Symbol | Meaning | Defined |
|---|---|---|
| `E, F = E⁺, f, c, G, f_E` | host field, its maximal totally real subfield, `[F:ℚ]`, complex conjugation, `Gal(E/ℚ)`, conductor of `E` (of a primitive `χ`: `f_χ`) | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §§0.1, 0.2 |
| `h_E, μ_E, rd_E` | class number of `E`; group of roots of unity of `E`; root discriminant of `E` | Conventions |
| `K, χ_K, d, √−d` | the imaginary quadratic subfield and its character; the prime of `SplitRamification d` (`K = ℚ(√−d)` on the cyclic hosts) and the root with `Im ι₀(√−d) > 0` | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §§0.1–0.3, Conventions |
| `Φ, ι₀, τ` | `Gal(E/K)` and the CM type it defines through `ι₀`; its elements, the places | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1 |
| `σ_d` | the nontrivial element of the inertia group at `d` | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3 |
| `SplitRamification d, InertFreeUpTo B, HostSplitting E` | host predicates: every ramified prime other than `d` splits in `K` and `I_d = {1, σ_d}`, `σ_d ∉ Φ`; no prime `p ≤ B` is inert in `K`; `4 \| f` and every place of `F` above a prime dividing `f_E` splits in `E` | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3 |
| `ψ, o, f_ψ` | the primitive Dirichlet character of a cyclic host, its order and conductor | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 |
| `(q, d), n, c(ψ)` | auxiliary pair, layer of the cyclotomic `ℤ₂`-tower of a cyclic host, the integer `c(ψ) ≤ o` with `\|A\| = c(ψ)·2^n` for every layer `n ≥ n₀(ψ)` (a divisor of `o`; the equality holds for `n + 2 ≥ v₂(f_ψ)`, 2.1) | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 (iii), 2.1 |
| `A, A₀` | `Gal(E/ℚ(√−q, √−d)) ⊂ Φ`; `A₀ = ker(ψ\|_A)`, `[A:A₀] = o` | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 |
| `Φ_top, 𝒯, u₀` | tops `Φ_top ⊂ A`, thick set `𝒯 ⊂ A₀`, a translate (thick places `cσ_d u₀𝒯`) | FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §§0.6, 0.8a |
| `γ, log Ω, log Ω_K` | Euler's constant; CM periods of `E` and of `K` | Conventions |
| `μ, μ_max, μ_min` | twice Bost's slopes | Conventions |
| `𝔭, p(𝔭), e_𝔭, ϱ(𝔭)` | finite place of `𝕃`, residue characteristic, ramification index, `log N𝔭/[𝕃:ℚ]` | Conventions |
| `k, m, t, r, N_j, n_j` | base rank, chain length, rank step, `r = k + m − 1`, `N_j = k + j(t + 1)`, `n_j = r + jt` | 2.1 |
| `x, ε, b, a, X′, b′, w″, X″` | scale, exponent, `e^{x^ε}`, `x·b`, `e^{x^{ε/3}}`, `a/X′`, `b′/(x\|A\|)`, `x\|A\|X′` | 2.1 |
| `ϑ, B₀, N_c, Γ₀, H` | thick-weight width, the two budgets `B₀ = fmbx^ε`, `N_c = \|𝒯\|kbx^{2ε}/\|A\|²`, redundancy factor, slope range `x^{c_H}b` | 2.1 |
| `Size(x, ε)` | conjunction of the host predicates `SizeA, SizeN, SizeD, SizeRd, SizeQD, SizeInert, SizeDep` and the parameter predicates `SizeM, SizeK, SizeW, SizeMf, SizeT, SizeTheta` | 2.1 |
| `SizeQD, SizeInert` | `x^{19ε/(100J)} ≤ log q, log d ≤ x^{ε/5+ε/100}` and `\|(L′/L)(1, χ_K)\| ≤ x^ε`; inert-free up to `max(12, x^{ε/(5J)})` | 2.1 |
| `Q_S, S_small, vd, SizeDep` | `f_E/(qd)`, the part of the conductor prime to `qd`; the primes dividing `2f_E` other than `q`, `d` (those dividing `Q_S`); `log₂ Q_S`; the host predicate on them | 2.1 |
| `SizeSc, SizeGap, SizeSep, SizeG, SizeT_u` | size predicates of the words, the gaps, the separation, the type primes and the level primes | 2.3b, 2.3c, 2.7, 2.8 |
| `C_t, C_W, C_Γ, A_max, C_dep` | constants fixed before `ε`: of `SizeT`, of `SizeW`, of `Γ₀`, the bound of the layer exponent, the constant of `SizeDep` | 2.1 |
| `A_n, J, c_H` | numbers that may depend on `ε`: the layer exponent of `SizeA` (`A_n ∈ [1, A_max]`; `2 + 8ε` in the construction), the window constant, the exponent of `H`; chosen after `ε` and before the scale | 2.1 |
| `ε₀, x₀` | admissible range of `ε`, threshold of the scale | 2.1 |
| `La` | `log(a·rd_E)` | 2.1 |
| `X_j, X^h` | column spaces, hyperplane of `X_m` | 2.2 |
| `Y_j, z_m, r_S` | row spaces; the negative vector of `Y_j` (`j` even); `r_S = m/2 − 1`, the number of hyperbolic planes of `Y_0` | 2.2 |
| `W_j, V_j` | model spaces: rank `r`, signature `(k+j−1, m−j)`; totally positive definite of rank `r + t` | 2.2 |
| `𝔞₀, δ_T` | `𝔞₀𝔞̄₀ = 𝔇_{E/K}^{−1}`; the scaling element `δ_T = √−d/d = −1/√−d` of the steps | 2.2 |
| `θ_{S,T}, ω_{S,T}(χ_S, χ_T); ω̄, θ̄_{S,T}` | theta kernel and Weil representation of a homomorphism pair in the normal form; the conjugate datum `ω̄(g, h)φ = conj(ω(g, h)conj φ)` and its kernel `θ̄_{S,T}(·; φ) = conj θ_{S,T}(·; conj φ)` (descents with the test vector `Φ` integrate against `θ̄_{S,T}(·; Φ)`) | 2.2a |
| `W, u, u_*` | the set of words, a word, the base word | 2.3 |
| `u(τ), v(τ), e′(τ), e(τ)` | Harish-Chandra data at `τ` of the word `u` (`v, e′, e` shared by all words) | 2.3 |
| `a_τ, a⁻_τ, θ_a, A_w, C_c` | top entry and negative bottom entry, top window `θ_a = 1/(k·x^{A_w/2})`, constant of the cloud gap | 2.3b |
| `w_j(τ)` | step weights `e_{j−1}(τ)` (thick, top or thin) | 2.3 |
| `Λ_j(τ), ν_j(τ), σ(τ)` | weight on `X_j`, Young diagram of step `j`, top weight on `X^h` | 2.3 |
| `s⁺_j(τ), s⁻_j(τ), p_j, q_j` | the model parameter of step `j`: the first `p_j = k + j − 1` and the last `q_j = m − j` entries of `(v(τ); −e′(τ))`; `s⁻_{j,c} = −e′_{j+c}` | 2.3 |
| `Δ_w, Sc, c_Δ` | letter step `⌈x^{c_Δ}⌉`, cloud size `4kΔ_w`, the exponent of the letter step (chosen after `ε`) | 2.3 |
| `(U_j), (W1)–(W6), h_gap, κ_j` | unit condition, conditions of a weight datum, row gap, the shift `(k + (2j − 1)(t + 1))/2` of the step diagrams | 2.3 |
| `(W7), η_s, D_*, ℓ*, ℓ_A` | the separation condition, separation width, thresholds | 2.3c |
| `h₀` | node spacing of an adapted weight datum | 2.3a |
| `μ_j, η̃, δ, δ′, o_δ` | Hecke characters of the chain, unramified splitting character (not algebraic; `η̃²` and `η̃·‖·‖_{𝔸_E}^{1/2}` are, and `𝕂` contains the values of the latter, 2.6), auxiliary characters and their inertial order | 2.4 |
| `χ_{v,j}, ω_g` | type characters `ω_g^{2j}` at the place `w_v` of `E` above `π_g` (the residue of `μ_j` there is `χ_{v,j}^{−1}`); the fixed generator of the characters of `𝔽_g^×` | 2.4 |
| `S₀` | bad places: the places above `g₀, g₁, p_δ, p′_δ` | 2.4 |
| `ξ, Ξ, ξ_𝔭, Q(ξ), s, δ_ℓ` | tuple of twists (`ξ = 1` the base tuple), set of tuples, tuple used at `𝔭`, twist places (full ramification set), the sign of the twisted tuples (`s_j := s`; `s = +1` for the chain), auxiliary character of `ℓ` | 2.5 |
| `A(ℓ), Q(ψ_𝔭)` | places of `E` above the auxiliary prime of `ℓ`; ramification set of the twist `ψ_𝔭` (`Q(ξ_𝔭) = A(ℓ) ⊔ Q(ψ_𝔭)`) | 2.5 |
| `𝒮_tw, Tw` | admissible twisting characteristics; twisted places | 2.5 |
| `Z₀; (T1), (T2)` | the prescribed set of places of a twisting datum; conditions on the twisting datum | 2.5 |
| `𝕂, 𝕃, σ₀, c_𝕃` | coefficient field, field of places, the inclusion of `𝕃` in `ℂ`, the restriction of complex conjugation | 2.6 |
| `Ω, E(N), h*, H₁₂, V, n_c, sz(·)` | the compositum of 2.6 containing `𝕂` (not the CM period `log Ω`), the ray class field of `E` of modulus `N𝒪_E`, its degree over `E`, the `12`-torsion field of the CM curve, the Kummer field, the order of the roots of unity, the pair (log of the degree, log of the root discriminant) | 2.6 |
| `g₀, g₁; p_δ, p′_δ; 𝔅(N, g)` | type primes; auxiliary primes; the exceptional set of a type prime | 2.7 |
| `T(u), T*(u), π_u` | level prime of the word `u`, its target value, `T(u) = N(π_u)` | 2.8 |
| `G₀, (P, χ_P), (P_0, χ_{P_0}), (P_1, χ_{P_1}) = (K₂, χ₂), ψ_g` | finite group of the type primes, a level, base level, row type level, additive character | 2.9a |
| `χ^{fl}_j, K^{pr}_j(ξ), K^{rt}_j(ξ)` | character `∏_{i≤j}χ_{v,i}^{−1}` of the row stage groups; finite level groups of stage `j` at principal level and at the row type level | 2.9a |
| `S(ξ), c_{i,v}` | the places where levels are not hyperspecial; the residues of the step characters at the bad places | 2.9 |
| `χ_N, χ_L, i_col, i_row, Φ^{pr}_{j,v}, Φ^{rt}_{j,v}, Φ^{(H)}_j` | characters and indices of the type levels; test vectors of the row steps at principal level and at the row type level; the row test vector at all type places together | 2.9a |
| `K^{rt}_j(v), K^{fn}_j(v), χ^{fn}_j` | the local row type level; the fine levels (the groups under which the chain's data at the type primes are equivariant) and their characters | 2.9a |
| `K_col(v), K^X_j(v)` | column levels at the type primes (principal congruence of level `g` between the ends; the top group `diag(A, 1)` with `b ≡ c ≡ 0`, `d ≡ 1`) and at the twist places (level `p^{2m}`; the top group modulo `p^{4m+1}`) | 2.9a, 2.9c |
| `e_{P,χ_P}` | idempotent of a level `(P, χ_P)` at the type primes | 2.9a |
| `𝔡, L_𝔡, 𝔡₀^{(j)}, Maps(𝔡, 𝔡′), x_u, K_u, 𝔽_v, ⟨a⟩_v` | pairs and class lattices at `T(u)`, standard pairs, maps of pairs, column level of a word, residue field, diamond operators | 2.9b |
| `idx_v(j), Φ^Q_{j,v}` | parahoric indices at twist places; test vector of the row steps there | 2.9c |
| `∇_ℤ, Δ_ℤ` | dual Weyl and Weyl lattices | 3.1 |
| `ℳ_j, ℳ_j^{int}, ℳ_j^∨, 𝒸, L_𝒸` | chain forms of level `j`; the `Δ_ℤ`-valued (integral) and `∇_ℤ`-valued forms; a class, its class lattice | 3.1 |
| `Λ_K, 𝔙(L, L′), H_A, c_τ` | the lattice `δ_K·ι₀(𝒪_K)` of discriminant `1`, the period lattice, its Hermitian form, generators of the axis ideals `𝔩_τ𝒪_𝕃` | 3.1a |
| `χ_D` | the character on ideals of relative norm one | 3.1b |
| `θ, θ₀, s₂` | the reduced theta function of `ℂ/Λ_K`; its even normalised Fock translate, from which the family is built; the constant `s₂(Λ_K)` | 3.1c |
| `Θ^{CM}_{L,L′}` | the family of CM theta functions | 3.1d |
| `ℱ_j, Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ), ρ^ξ_j, K_j(ξ), col_ξ, A_j, φ^{(α,α′)}_j` | Fock summand of step `j`, theta function of a step at a pair of classes, class characters, step operator, chain lift; a system of data of the chain: index sets and admissible data (`col_ξ` is the sum over index paths of composites) | 3.2, 3.5 |
| `φ_f, φ_{T,v}, s♯_j, s_div,j` | finite datum of a step; the datum at `T(u)`; `w_j − N_j/2`, `w_j − N_{j−1}/2` | 3.2 |
| `D_Γ` | least common multiple of the stabiliser orders | 3.6 |
| `U_v, D_v, FF_v, FF_v^*, Gs_v, Gs_v^D` | level operator at `T(u)` and its transpose; the finite Fourier transform (inputs on the kernel of the output, `FF_v = D_vGs_v` on new forms) and its adjoint (`FF_v^* = U_vGs_v^D`); Gauss operators | 3.7 |
| `M_u^{all}, M_u, d_u^{all}, d_u` | full and new source lattices of the word `u`, their ranks | 3.8 |
| `P̃, P̃_ξ; Υ, φ′; P^{bil}, Υ^c, β_λ` | top period, of the chain of the tuple `ξ`; a row form on `U(X^h)`, a column form of level `m`; the bilinear form of the period, the coefficient conjugate of `Υ`, the bilinear contravariant form | 3.9 |
| `𝒸̃, res, br_τ, c_{Br,τ}, D₀(τ), D₁(τ)` | the class of level `m` of a class of `U(X^h)`, restriction to `U(X^h)`, the branching map, its constant, its degree bounds | 3.9 |

## Layer 2: The setting of the chain

Ranks, scales and every object the chain is built from, constructed explicitly: the column, row and model spaces and
the normal form of the pairs they form; the Harish-Chandra parameters, the step weights and the words; the Hecke
characters of the chain and the characters that twist it; a coefficient field in which the ideals of the host become
principal; the type and auxiliary primes; the level prime of each word, chosen so that the sources together have nearly
the dimension of the target; and the level structures at the type primes, the level primes and the twist places. In
this layer `E` is an abelian CM host (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1), in particular Galois over `ℚ`, with `F = E⁺`, `f = [F:ℚ]`, `K ⊂ E` imaginary
quadratic, `Φ = Gal(E/K)` and the embedding `ι₀`. From 2.2 on the host satisfies, for an odd prime `d`, `SplitRamification d`
(FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3) with `σ_d ≠ c` and `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)`: then `K = ℚ(√−d)`, `E/F` is unramified at every finite place, `f` is even
(FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3(b)–(c), FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1), the primes ramified in `E/K` lie above primes split in `K`, and every place of `F` above `2` splits in `E`
(`2` splits in `K`, and `E = FK`). The hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 satisfy all of this (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(i) and `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)`).
The chain is built, and the statements of Layer 3 that rest on [FormalProof] are proved there, for the hosts of
FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, which have `q ≡ 3 (mod 8)`, `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)` and layer `n ≥ 2`. For these `v₂(d + 1) = 3`, so
`n ≥ v₂(d + 1) − 1` holds and `HostSplitting E` is satisfied (2.4 (b)); `16qd` divides `f_E`, `f = 2|A|` and `f ≥ 8`. For
a general prime `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)` the statements of this roadmap are proved by the routes given with them, except at the
steps that the routes of 3.1d and 3.3 list as not given (at the end of each route): [FormalProof] covers those steps only
for the hosts named above, so for the other hosts 3.1d, 3.3 and the statements of Layer 3 that use them rest on steps for
which this roadmap gives no proof. The splitting character
`η̃` of the chain exists under `HostSplitting E` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3; 2.4 (b)), so every statement that uses `η̃` assumes it; its second
condition (every place of `F` above a prime dividing `f_E` splits in `E`) is a standing hypothesis of Layer 3 from 3.1d on.

### 2.1 Ranks, scales and size predicates

*Definitions.*

*Ranks.* `ChainRanks f` is the set of triples `(k, m, t)` of integers with `k, m` even, `m ≥ 2`, `t` odd and

- (R1) `m + 3 ≤ k`; (R3) `m ≤ f ≤ k`;
- (R5) `8fr + 4 ≤ r + t`, where `r := k + m − 1` (the rank condition `dim V_j = r + t ≥ 8fr + 4` on the model spaces of
  2.2).

Derived ranks: `r` (odd), the column ranks `N_j := k + j(t + 1)` (even) and the row ranks `n_j := r + jt` for
`0 ≤ j ≤ m`.

*Scale.* A scale is a pair `(x, ε)` with `x > 1` and `0 < ε < ε₀`, where `ε₀ ∈ (0, 1)` is a constant fixed before `ε`.
Put `b := e^{x^ε}`, `a := x·b`, `X′ := e^{x^{ε/3}}`, `b′ := a/X′`. For a host of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 with the group `A` and a thick set
`𝒯 ⊂ A₀` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.8a) put `w″ := b′/(x|A|)` and `X″ := x|A|X′ = a/w″`. Further numbers, all definitions and not bounds: the
thick-weight width `ϑ ≥ 1` (a parameter); the budgets `B₀ := f m b x^ε` and `N_c := |𝒯|·k·b·x^{2ε}/|A|²`; the redundancy
factor `Γ₀ := exp(C_Γ m⁵ f x^ε)`; the slope range `H := x^{c_H} b`. Here `C_Γ > 0` is a constant fixed before `ε`, and
the exponent `c_H > 0` is an argument of every statement that uses `H`: it is chosen after `ε` and before the scale, may
depend on `ε` and on the window constant `J` below, and never multiplies `ε` in an exponent.

*Size predicates* (named here and cited by name where they are assumed). Their arguments are: constants `C_t`, `C_W ≥ 1`,
`A_max ≥ 1` and `C_dep ≥ 1` fixed before `ε`; a number `A_n ∈ [1, A_max]`, which may depend on `ε`; and the
*window constant* `J ≥ 1`, a number which may depend on `ε` and is fixed before the scale. `f_E` is the conductor of the
host, `−d` the discriminant of `K` and `(q, d)` the auxiliary pair of the host (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4).

- *host predicates* (properties of the host at the scale; they do not follow from the other predicates):
  `SizeA`: `x^{A_n} ≤ |A| ≤ 2x^{A_n}`; `SizeN`: `log f_E ≤ 2x^{ε/5}·log² x`; `SizeD`: `d/log d ≥ 2x`;
  `SizeRd`: `log rd_E ≤ x^ε/8`; `SizeQD`: `x^{ε/(6J)} ≤ log q`, `x^{19ε/(100J)} ≤ log q ≤ x^{ε/5+ε/100}`,
  `x^{19ε/(100J)} ≤ log d ≤ x^{ε/5+ε/100}` and `|(L′/L)(1, χ_K)| ≤ x^ε` (the first bound follows from the second, and the
  upper bounds follow from `SizeN` for `x ≥ x₀(ε)`, as `q, d | f_E`). Put `Q_S := f_E/(q·d)`, the part of the conductor
  of the host prime to `q·d` (`Q_S = lcm(2^{n+2}, f_ψ)` for a host of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 with layer `n`, by
  its API (c); `S_small` below is the set of the primes dividing `Q_S`). Every host of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 has
  `1 ≤ rd_E ≤ (Q_S/2)·√(q·d) = f_E/(2√(q·d))`, so this bound, which [FormalProof] counts among its conditions on `q, d`,
  is a consequence and not a hypothesis (`1 ≤ rd_E` for every number field; the upper bound is proved in the route below);
  `SizeInert`: the host is inert-free up to `max(12, x^{ε/(5J)})` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3), so every prime inert in `K` is at least `13`;
  `SizeDep`: `log(qd) ≤ m` and `(Σ_{ℓ∈S_small} log ℓ)·(2 + vd) ≤ C_dep·x^ε`, where `S_small` is the set of the primes dividing
  `2f_E` other than `q` and `d` (they split in `K`, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(v); every prime ramified in `E` lies in `S_small ∪ {q, d}`), and `vd := log₂ Q_S` (`Q_S = f_E/(q·d)`
  as above; `S_small` is the set of the primes dividing `Q_S`). For every `ℓ ∈ S_small`, `vd ≥ v_ℓ(Q_S) = v_ℓ(f_E)`, and
  `v_ℓ(f_E)` is at least the *mean different exponent* of `E` at `ℓ` (the exponent of a prime above `ℓ` in the different of
  `E/ℚ`, divided by the ramification index);
- *parameter predicates:* `SizeM`: `x^{1/2−ε/16−ε/100} ≤ m ≤ x^{1/2−ε/16+ε/100}`;
  `SizeK`: `x^{9/2+10ε−ε/100} ≤ k ≤ x^{9/2+10ε+ε/100}`, `k ≤ |A|² x^{1/2−ε}`, `√(k/m) ≤ |A|x^{−ε}`, `m²x^ε ≤ k`,
  `m√x·log x ≤ k`, `x^{ε/100}·m⁶·x^ε ≤ k·log k`, `x^{ε/100}·m³·x^ε·log rd_E ≤ k·log² k`, `log f ≤ √(m/2 − 1)`,
  `log² f ≤ m²x^ε`, `x^{ε/100}·ε·log x ≤ r·f·log r`, `x^{ε/100}·x^ε ≤ f·log k`, `f ≤ m²|A|` and `f ≤ r²`;
  `SizeW`: `k ≥ x^{ε/100}·m⁵·f·x^{2ε}` and `k ≥ C_W m⁵ |A| x^ε` (the word count; the construction takes
  `C_W = 16C_Γ`); `SizeMf`: `m⁴ ≤ f`; `SizeT`: `t ≤ x^{C_t}`;
  `SizeTheta`: `t ≤ ϑ ≤ b/(t³fk³)`, `log ϑ ≤ x^{ε/4}`, `t·ϑ ≤ w″/x`, `m(t + 1)ϑ ≤ w″`, `m(Sc + ϑ) ≤ w″x^ε`,
  `N_m·ϑ ≤ (|𝒯|/|A|)·m·w″`, `ϑ²·r·x^ε ≤ (|𝒯|/|A|)·m·w″²` and `r(b′ + Sc)·log x·ℓ_A ≤ (|𝒯|/|A|)·m·b` (`Sc` of 2.3, `ℓ_A`
  of 2.3c).

`Size(x, ε)` is the conjunction of these predicates. It is this roadmap's packaging of conditions that the parameters of
the chain satisfy, not the full list of the size conditions under which the statements about the chain are proved. In
the construction `|A|` lies within a factor `x^{ε/100}` of `x^{2+8ε}`, and the parameters satisfy further conditions with no
counterpart among the predicates of this roadmap, among them: the conductor against `t`, `f_E^{36}·t^{150} ≤ √b`; on the
tops and the thick set, `x^{−ε/100}·|A|·x^{−ε/8} ≤ |Φ_top| ≤ 2|A|·x^{−ε/8}`, `x^{−ε} ≤ |𝒯|/|A|` and `L_th ≤ log² x` for the
length `L_th` of the thick set `𝒯` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.8a); on the node spacing `h₀` of 2.3a, `k·t·x^ε ≤ h₀`,
`x^{ε/100}·k·m^{9/2} ≤ h₀`, `2(r + mt)³ + t + 3 ≤ h₀` and `N_m·m^{10} ≤ h₀`; `√b ≤ w″`, and `w_j(τ) ≤ X″·w_{j′}(τ′)` for all
`j, τ, j′, τ′`; on the primes of 2.7, `g_i ≤ 2g_j`, `log g_i ≤ x^{ε/100}·x^ε` and `log p_δ, log p′_δ ≤ x^ε`; and
`|W| ≤ 2^{k−3}` on the words. A target that needs one of them states it.
Size conditions on quantities defined later are named where the
quantity is defined (`SizeSc` in 2.3b, `SizeGap`, `SizeSep` in 2.3c, `SizeG` in 2.7, `SizeT_u` in 2.8); the lower bound on
`ϑ` that the windows of 2.3b need is part of the hypotheses of 2.3a. The *standing size hypotheses* of the Gram bounds of
the row spaces are, by name: `SizeMf` and `SizeRd` (here), `SizeG` (2.7: `g₀, g₁ ≤ b^{C_g}`), `SizeT_u` (2.8:
`b^{16m} ≤ T*(u)` and `T(u) ≤ b^{C_T m}`; the Gram bounds need only `b^{c_T m} ≤ T(u)` for some constant `c_T > 0` fixed
before `ε`), and the rank window of 2.8 for the dimension numbers `d_u` (a consequence of the
choice of `T(u)`, proved in 2.8 and 3.8, not an assumption). *What `SizeInert` gives:* every prime inert in `K` is at least
`13` and at least `x^{ε/(5J)}`, so that the estimates of the chain at the inert primes (gains, depths of orbits,
congruence lengths) may use these lower bounds. *What `SizeQD` gives:* `q, d > x^{2C_t+5}` (Lemma). *What `SizeDep`
gives:* the bad primes `q`, `d` and those of `S_small` cost a bounded multiple of `B₀` per step.

*Lemma.* `n_j − N_j = m − 1 − j` for `0 ≤ j ≤ m`; in particular `n_m = N_m − 1` and `n_{m−1} = N_{m−1}`. (R5) is
`t ≥ (8f − 1)r + 4`; it implies `t ≥ 7r + 4`, hence `t ≥ r + 5`, `k + m + 4 ≤ t`, and the two conditions
(R2) `3m < t` and (R4) `m² ≤ k + m(t + 1)`. `SizeM` gives `m ≥ x^{1/3}` for `ε ≤ 1/6`, so `m` exceeds any constant fixed
before `ε` for `x ≥ x₀(ε)` (and `m ≥ 4` for `x ≥ 64`, so `f ≥ m ≥ 4` by (R3)); with `SizeK`, `r ≥ k ≥ x^{3/2−ε} ≥ 100` for `ε ≤ 1/6`, `x ≥ 32`, and
`k/m ≤ |A|²`. Under `SizeW`, `log Γ₀ = C_Γ m⁵ f x^ε ≤ (C_Γ/C_W)·f k/|A|`. (R5) gives `t ≥ 7fr ≥ 7fk`; if `f ≥ 2|A|`, then under `SizeA`,
`SizeM` and `SizeW` one has `t ≥ 14x^{2A_n + 5/2 − 4ε}`, so `SizeT` can hold only if `C_t > 2A_n + 5/2 − 4ε`; `C_t ≥ 2A_max + 5/2`
suffices for this necessary condition. For an abelian host, `SizeN` implies `log rd_E ≤ x^ε/C` for every constant `C` when
`x ≥ x₀(ε, C)` (in particular `SizeRd`), and then `La := log(a·rd_E) ≤ 2x^ε`. `SizeQD` implies `q, d > x^{2C_t+5}` and `SizeD`
for `x ≥ x₀(ε, J)`. For a host of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 with layer `n ≥ n₀(ψ)`: `|A| = c(ψ)·2^n` with `c(ψ) ≤ o` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(iii)), so
`2^n ≥ |A|/o ≥ |A|·x^{−ε/100}` once `x^{ε/100} ≥ o`. For a host of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 with layer `n ≥ 2`, `ε ≤ 1/6` and `x ≥ x₀(ψ, ε)`: `SizeN`
and `SizeM` give `log(qd) ≤ m`, and with `SizeA`, `(Σ_{ℓ∈S_small} log ℓ)·(2 + log₂ Q_S) ≤ 11x^ε`;
so `SizeDep` holds with `C_dep = 11`.

Route: arithmetic. For the bound on `t`: `r ≥ k ≥ C_W m⁵|A|x^ε ≥ x^{5/2 − 5ε}·x^{A_n}·x^ε` and `f ≥ 2x^{A_n}`. For `SizeRd`: the
root discriminant of an abelian field is at most its conductor (conductor–discriminant formula: `|d_E| = ∏_χ f_χ ≤ f_E^{[E:ℚ]}`),
and `2x^{ε/5}log²x ≤ x^ε/C` for `x ≥ x₀(ε, C)`; then `La = log x + x^ε + log rd_E ≤ 2x^ε`. `k/m ≤ |A|²x^{1/2−ε}/x^{1/2−ε}`.
`SizeQD`: `log q, log d ≥ x^{ε/(6J)}` exceed `(2C_t + 5)log x` and `log(2x log d)` for large `x`. `c(ψ)`: the characters of `G`
trivial on `A` form `⟨χ_{−q}, χ_{−d}⟩`, which meets `𝔛_n⟨ψ⟩` trivially (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 (ii); `𝔛_n` the cyclic group of
order `2^n` of even Dirichlet characters of conductor dividing `2^{n+2}`), so the characters of `A` are the restrictions of `𝔛_n⟨ψ⟩` and
`|A| = 2^n·o/|𝔛_n ∩ ⟨ψ⟩|`; for `n + 2 ≥ v₂(f_ψ)`, `𝔛_n ∩ ⟨ψ⟩` is the group of the powers of `ψ` that are even of `2`-power
conductor, which does not depend on `n`, so `c(ψ) = o/|𝔛_n ∩ ⟨ψ⟩|` (the integer of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 (iii)) divides `o`.
The bound on `rd_E`: by FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 (ii)–(iii) the characters of `G` are the products `χ_{−q}^aχ_{−d}^bη`
with `a, b ∈ {0, 1}` and `η` in `H := 𝔛_n⟨ψ⟩`, a group of order `|A|`; `f_η` divides `Q_S`, which is prime to `qd`
(`q, d ∤ f_ψ`), so that product has conductor `q^ad^bf_η`, and the conductor–discriminant formula gives
`rd_E = |d_E|^{1/(4|A|)} = √(qd)·(∏_{η∈H} f_η)^{1/|A|}`. Put `v := v₂(Q_S)` and let `e(η)` be the exponent of the
conductor of the `2`-component of `η`, so `f_η ≤ 2^{e(η)−v}Q_S`. These `2`-components form a group `H₂` of characters
modulo `2^v`, each taken `|A|/|H₂|` times; its subgroups `H₂^{(j)}` of the characters of conductor dividing `2^j` have
`|H₂^{(0)}| = |H₂^{(1)}| = 1` and `[H₂^{(j)} : H₂^{(j−1)}] ≤ 2` (the characters modulo `2^j` form a group of order
`2^{j−1}`), so `Σ_{χ∈H₂} e(χ) = v|H₂| − Σ_{0≤j<v} |H₂^{(j)}| ≤ (v − 1)|H₂|`, because
`Σ_{1≤j<v} |H₂^{(j)}| ≥ (1 − 2^{1−v})|H₂|` and `|H₂| ≤ 2^{v−1}`. Hence `(∏_η f_η)^{1/|A|} ≤ Q_S/2`, with equality for
`ψ` the nontrivial character modulo `4`, for which `H` is the group of all characters modulo `2^{n+2}`.
The bad primes: `q, d | f_E`, so
`log(qd) ≤ log f_E ≤ 2x^{ε/5}log²x ≤ x^{1/2−ε} ≤ m` (the third inequality for `x ≥ x₀(ε)` because `ε/5 < 1/2 − ε`, which
holds for `ε ≤ 1/6` and fails for `ε ≥ 5/12`); `f_E = lcm(2^{n+2}, f_ψ)·q·d` for `n ≥ 1` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 API (c)), so the primes of `S_small` divide
`2f_ψ`, and `log₂ Q_S ≤ n + 2 + log₂f_ψ` (`Q_S` divides `2^{n+2}f_ψ`) with `2^n ≤ |A| ≤ 2x^{A_n}`; so the product is at most
`log(2f_ψ)·(A_max·log₂x + 5 + log₂f_ψ) ≤ 11x^ε` for `x ≥ x₀(ψ, ε)`; by the conductor–discriminant formula the mean different exponent
of `E` at `ℓ` is the mean of the conductor exponents at `ℓ` of the characters of the host, at most `v_ℓ(f_E)`. `SizeMf` is compatible with
`SizeM` and `SizeA` when `A_n ≥ 2 + 2ε` and `f ≥ 2|A|` (`m⁴ ≤ x^{2+2ε}`). *Checks:* `n_0 − N_0 = m − 1 = r − k`;
`N_m − n_m = 1` is the codimension of the hyperplane `X^h` of 2.2. For the values of one construction, `ε < ε₀ = 1/52`,
`|A| ∈ [x^{2+8ε}, 2x^{2+8ε}]` (so `A_n = 2 + 8ε` depends on `ε`, and `A_max = 2 + 8ε₀ = 28/13`), `f = 2|A|`, `m ≍ x^{1/2−ε/16}`, `k ≍ x^{9/2+10ε}`
and `t ≤ x^{400}` with `C_t = 401`, every parameter predicate holds for large `x`; the smallest margins are a factor
`x^{ε/100}` in the windows of `SizeM` and `SizeK` and a factor `x^{0.30ε}` in the first bound of `SizeW`
(`x^{ε/100}m⁵fx^{2ε} ≍ x^{9/2+9.70ε}`).

### 2.2 The spaces of the chain

*Construction and lemma.* Let the host be as in the introduction of this layer, `(k, m, t)`
ranks of 2.1, and `T = N_{K/ℚ}(π_T)` with `π_T ∈ 𝒪_K`, `T > 0`. All spaces are `E^n` with diagonal Hermitian forms
(conjugate-linear in the first variable); `e_1, …, e_n` is the standard basis and `𝒪_E^n` the standard lattice.

- *Columns.* `X_j := E^{N_j}` with `⟨x, y⟩ = Σ_i x̄_i y_i`, `0 ≤ j ≤ m`. `X^h := ⟨e_1, …, e_{N_m − 1}⟩ ⊂ X_m` and
  `z := π_T e_{N_m}`, so that `X_m = X^h ⊥ ⟨z⟩` and `⟨z, z⟩ = T`.
- *Rows.* `Y_m := X^h`. For `j < m` put `h_j := ⌊(m − 1 − j)/2⌋` and
  `Y_j := X_j ⊥ ℍ^{h_j}` if `j` is odd, `Y_j := X_j ⊥ ℍ^{h_j} ⊥ E e₋` if `j` is even, where `ℍ = E e ⊕ E f` is the
  hyperbolic plane (`⟨e, e⟩ = ⟨f, f⟩ = 0`, `⟨e, f⟩ = 1`) and `⟨e₋, e₋⟩ = −1`; put `z_m := π_T e₋ ∈ Y_j` (`j` even), so
  `⟨z_m, z_m⟩ = −T`. The hyperbolic planes of `Y_0` are written `E e_a ⊕ E f_a`, `1 ≤ a ≤ r_S := m/2 − 1`.
- *Model spaces.* For `1 ≤ j ≤ m`: `W_j := E^r` with the form `diag(1^{k+j−1}, (−1)^{m−j})`, and `V_j := E^{r+t}` with
  the standard positive form.

*Lemma.* (a) `rank Y_j = n_j`, and at every `τ ∈ Φ` the signature of `Y_j` is `(n_j − ⌊(m−j)/2⌋, ⌊(m−j)/2⌋)`; in
particular `Y_0` has signature `(k + m/2 − 1, m/2)`, `Y_{m−1} = X_{m−1}`, and `Y_m = X^h` is totally positive definite of
rank `N_m − 1`. (b) `X_j ⊂ Y_j` isometrically for `j < m`, with orthogonal complement of rank `m − 1 − j`. (c) `W_j` has
rank `r` and signature `(k + j − 1, m − j)`, both entries positive for `1 ≤ j < m`; it is isometric to `Y_0` at every
finite place of `F`; `W_m` is totally positive definite. (d) Every space above carries a unimodular `𝒪_E`-lattice (the standard one, with `𝒪_E e ⊕ 𝒪_E f` on
each hyperbolic plane and `𝒪_E e₋` on the negative line); `z_m` and `z` lie in the standard lattices. (e) Every totally
positive definite Hermitian space over `E` of rank `n` containing a unimodular lattice is isometric to `E^n` with the
standard form. (f) There is an ideal `𝔟 ⊂ 𝒪_E`, stable under `Gal(E/K)`, with `𝔇_{E/K} = 𝔟𝔟̄` and `𝔟 + 𝔟̄ = 𝒪_E`; with
`𝔞₀ := 𝔟^{−1}` one has `𝔞₀𝔞̄₀ = 𝔇_{E/K}^{−1}` and `(√−d)·𝔞₀𝔞̄₀·𝔇_{E/ℚ} = (d)`, that is `(δ_T)·𝔞₀𝔞̄₀·𝔇_{E/ℚ} = 𝒪_E` for the
*skew element* `δ_T := −1/√−d = √−d/d` (so `Im τ(δ_T) > 0` for every `τ ∈ Φ`). (g) *Split places.* Every place of `F` above `2` splits in `E` (for the hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 this is part
of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(v)); if `3` splits in `K` (for instance under `SizeInert`), every place of `F` with residue field `𝔽_3` splits in `E`;
every place of `F` dividing the different of `F/ℚ` and not above `d` splits in `E`, and under `HostSplitting E` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3) so does
every place above `d`. A place of `F` above a prime `≡ 1 (mod f_E)` is prime to the different of `F/ℚ`.

Route: (a), (b) count the diagonal entries (`ℍ` contributes `(1, 1)`, `e₋` contributes `(0, 1)`; for `j` even
`2h_j + 1 = m − 1 − j`). (c) At a finite place `v` of `F` the spaces `W_j` and `Y_0` have the same dimension and
determinants `(−1)^{m−j}`, `(−1)^{m/2}`, both units; units are norms from `E_v` because `E/F` is unramified at `v`, so
the determinant classes agree and HermitianFormsAndLattices §2.1 (nonsplit `v`) and §2.3 (split `v`) give an isometry.
(d) The standard lattices are unimodular by inspection. (e) A unimodular lattice forces every local determinant to be
a unit, hence a norm; HermitianFormsAndLattices §3.1 (Hasse principle) with the signature `(n, 0)` at every real place.
(f) `E/K` is unramified above `d` because the inertia group `{1, σ_d}` meets `Φ = Gal(E/K)` trivially; so `𝔇_{E/K}` is
supported above the primes `p ≠ d` ramified in `E`, which split in `K` as `𝔭𝔭̄`. Choose one `𝔭` for each such `p` and let `𝔟`
be the part of `𝔇_{E/K}` above the chosen `𝔭`; it is `Gal(E/K)`-stable (that group fixes `𝔭`), and `𝔟̄` is the part above
the `𝔭̄`, because `𝔇_{E/K}` is stable under `c`. Since `d ≡ 3 (mod 4)`, `𝔇_{K/ℚ} = (√−d)`, so
`𝔇_{E/ℚ} = 𝔇_{E/K}·(√−d)` and `(√−d)𝔞₀𝔞̄₀𝔇_{E/ℚ} = (√−d)² = (d)`; `Im τ(√−d) > 0` on `Φ` (Conventions). (g) At a place `v` of `F` above a prime `p` split in `K`,
`E ⊗_F F_v ≅ F_v ⊗_{ℚ_p} (K ⊗ ℚ_p) = F_v × F_v` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3(e)); `2` splits in `K` because `−d ≡ 1 (mod 8)`; under `SizeInert` the
prime `3 ≠ d` is neither inert nor ramified in `K`; a place dividing the different of `F/ℚ` lies above a prime ramified in
`F`, hence in `E`, and such a prime other than `d` splits in `K` (`SplitRamification d`); `d` divides `f_E`; a
prime `≡ 1 (mod f_E)` is unramified in `E ⊂ ℚ(ζ_{f_E})`, hence in `F`.

*General form.* For any CM extension `E/F`, a Hermitian space with prescribed dimension, signatures at the real places
and determinant classes in `F_v^×/N(E_v^×)` at the finite places exists if and only if these local invariants satisfy
the product formula of HermitianFormsAndLattices §3.2, and it is unique up to isometry (HermitianFormsAndLattices §3.1); the diagonal spaces above
are the case in which every finite determinant class is that of a unit, hence trivial because `E/F` is unramified.

*Checks.* For `j = m − 2` the complement of `X_{m−2}` in `Y_{m−2}` is the single negative line `⟨z_m⟩`, of signature
`(0, 1)`, matching `⌊2/2⌋ = 1`. If `⟨z, z⟩` were not a local norm from `E` at some place, `X^h ⊥ ⟨z⟩` would have a different
determinant class from the standard `X_m` there and could not be isometric to it: `T = N(π_T)` is what lets both `X^h`
and `X_m` be standard. For `E = ℚ(√−3, √−7)` and `K = ℚ(√−7)` (`SplitRamification 7` fails, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3) `𝔇_{E/K}` is the prime
above `3`, which is its own conjugate, and no `𝔟` as in (f) exists.
