(`Formalism.BasicNotions.Spaces`), `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/Params.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.Ranks.sigY0`),
`Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataHost.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.Host.xi0`).
*Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3, 2.1; HermitianFormsAndLattices §§2.1, 2.3, 3.1, 3.2, 4.2;
NumberFieldArithmetic (Completed; the different in towers).

### 2.2a The normal form of a step

*Definition (the normal form of a step) and lemma.* Let the host be as in the introduction
of this layer, `S`, `T` Hermitian spaces over `E` (conjugate-linear in the first variable) and `δ ∈ ℚ_{>0}·δ_T` (2.2(f);
so `Im τ(δ) > 0` for `τ ∈ Φ`). The *homomorphism pair* of `(S, T)` with skew element `δ` is the symplectic `F`-space
`Hom_E(S, T)` with the action `(g, h)·x := h∘x∘g^{−1}` of `U(S) × U(T)`, the alternating form
`B_δ(x, y) := Tr_{E/F}(δ·tr(x^†y))` (`x^†` the adjoint, conjugate-linear in `x`) and the standard additive character
`ψ_F` of `𝔸_F/F` (`ψ_{F,∞}(x) = e^{2πix}`); equivalently the form `Tr_{E/F}(δ·tr(y^†x))` with `ψ̄_F`. *Splitting
characters* are unitary Hecke characters `χ_S`, `χ_T` of `E` with `χ_S|_{𝔸_F^×} = η_{E/F}^{dim S}` and
`χ_T|_{𝔸_F^×} = η_{E/F}^{dim T}` (`χ_S` is *attached to* `S`). The *Weil representation* `ω_{S,T}(χ_S, χ_T)` is the global
Weil representation of ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §4.4a for this pair: in the terms of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups
§§0.1 and 2.5(f) that of the pair `(S^∨, δT)` (`S^∨` the dual space with the induced form, on which `g ∈ U(S)` acts by
`(g^{−1})^∨`; that roadmap's `V = S^∨`, `W = T`) with `χ_V := χ_S` and `χ_W := χ_T`; its theta kernel is written
`θ_{S,T}`. A pair *in the normal form* is a homomorphism pair with `δ ∈ ℚ_{>0}·δ_T`, its Weil representation, and the
conventions (c) below for the integrals in which it is used.

*Lemma.* (a) *Split places.* Let `v` be a finite place of `F` split in `E`, `w | v` a place of `E`, through which
`U(S)(F_v) ≅ GL(S_w)` and `U(T)(F_v) ≅ GL(T_w)`, `n_S := dim S`, `n_T := dim T`, and `c_S`, `c_T` the restrictions of `χ_{S,w}`,
`χ_{T,w}` to `𝒪_{E_w}^×`. In the model on the Schwartz functions of `x ∈ Hom(S_w, T_w)`, the functional `φ ↦ φ(0)` transforms by
`r·χ̃_T(det g)^{−1}χ̃_S(det h)`, `r > 0` (`χ̃(y/ȳ) := χ(y)`), and for all `g ∈ GL(S_w)`, `h ∈ GL(T_w)`
`(ω(g, h)φ)(x) = χ_{T,w}(det g)^{−1}χ_{S,w}(det h)·|det g|_w^{n_T/2}|det h|_w^{−n_S/2}·φ(h^{−1}xg)`; in particular, for `g`, `h` in the
stabilisers of lattices of `S_w`, `T_w`, `(ω(g, h)φ)(x) = c_T(det g)^{−1}c_S(det h)·φ(h^{−1}xg)`.
(b) *The conjugate datum.* `ω̄(g, h)φ := conj(ω(g, h)·conj φ)` is the Weil representation of the homomorphism pair of `(S, T)`
with skew element `−δ` and splitting characters `χ_S^{−1}`, `χ_T^{−1}`; its theta kernel at a vector `φ` is
`θ̄_{S,T}(g, h; φ) = conj θ_{S,T}(g, h; conj φ)`, the complex conjugate of the kernel of `ω` at the complex conjugate vector; at a
split place as in (a) it acts by `φ ↦ χ_{T,w}(det g)χ_{S,w}(det h)^{−1}·|det g|_w^{n_T/2}|det h|_w^{−n_S/2}·φ(h^{−1}xg)`, on lattice
stabilisers by `φ ↦ c_T(det g)c_S(det h)^{−1}·φ(h^{−1}xg)`; at a real place below `τ ∈ Φ` at which `S` and `T` are definite, its
Fock vectors are the complex conjugates of those of `ω`, with the dual types (the readings of (c) of
ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5 become holomorphic for `cΦ` instead of `Φ`, and conversely).
(c) *Integrals (convention fixed here).* A *lift* along the pair (a column step, a model step, an up-lift along a row)
integrates the form on the source group against `θ_{S,T}`, linearly; a *descent* along the pair (the row descents) with a
finite vector `Φ`, which is a vector of the conjugate datum (b), integrates the form on the target group `U(T)` against
`θ̄_{S,T}(·; Φ) = conj θ_{S,T}(·; conj Φ)`: it is the linear theta integral of the conjugate datum at `Φ`, and the vector put
into the kernel `θ_{S,T}` of `ω` itself is `conj Φ`. Equivalently, a descent can be written with `ω` and the vectors `conj Φ`, whose
equivariance characters on lattice stabilisers are the inverses of those of `Φ` under `ω̄`; the levels and test vectors of the
row steps (2.9–2.9c) are stated for the conjugate datum.
(d) *Compact steps.* If `S`, `T` are totally positive definite of ranks `n`, `n + t` (`t ≥ 0`) and `χ_S`, `χ_T` have exponents
`m_S`, `m_T` at `τ ∈ Φ` (`χ_τ(z) = (z/z̄)^{m/2}`), the polynomial Fock vectors at `τ` realise `⊕_μ (V_{μ+a})^∨ ⊗ V_{(μ,0^t)+b}` of
`U(S) × U(T)`, `μ` running over the partitions with at most `n` rows, `a := (n + t + m_T)/2`, `b := (n + m_S)/2`, and the
vacuum transforms by `det(g)^{−a}det(h)^{b}`. A form on `U(S)` of `K_∞`-type `V_ρ` at `τ` can pair with `θ_{S,T}` only if
`V_ρ ≅ V_{μ+a}` for some `μ`, and its lift then has type `V_{(μ,0^t)+b}`. A form on `U(T)` of type `V_σ` can pair with `θ_{S,T}`
only if `V_σ ≅ (V_{(μ,0^t)+b})^∨` for some `μ`, and with `θ̄_{S,T}` only if `V_σ ≅ V_{(μ,0^t)+b}`; in the second case the descent
has type `V_{μ+a}` at `τ`, whose Harish-Chandra parameter is that of `σ` with its last `t` entries, the segment
`S(m_S/2, t)`, removed, and shifted by `(m_T − m_S)/2`. (This covers `dim S ≤ dim T`. For the model steps `(V_j, W_j)`, where the
source is the larger space and `W_j` is indefinite for `j < m`, no dictionary of types is asserted: the later roadmaps of the
chain work with one explicit joint vector and the representation it generates.)
(e) *Real places.* At the real place below `τ ∈ Φ` the pair is read as the pair `(V, W) = (S^∨, δT)` of the paragraph
*Other normalisations* of ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §3.46a, with `χ_V = χ_S` and `χ_W = χ_T`: `V` has the signature of
`S`, `W^♭` that of `T`, `m_V = m_S` and `m_W = m_T`. Its polynomial Fock space is the module `𝒫̄` of that target twisted
by `det^{m_T/2}` on `U(V)` and `det^{m_S/2}` on `U(W)`; so a pair of discrete series occurring in it, in the sense of that
target, has the parameters `−λ + m_T/2` on `U(V) = U(S^∨)` and `−λ′ + m_S/2` on `U(T)`, `(λ, λ′)` as there.

Route: (a) The split-place law of the Weil representation of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5(f)
(ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §4.4a, characterised by five properties, among them continuity and this law) gives the law of
`φ ↦ φ(0)`. In the model of the split place the Heisenberg group acts by translations and characters and `GL(S_w) × GL(T_w)`
by `φ ↦ φ(h^{−1}xg)` times a character of `(g, h)`, which the law of `φ ↦ φ(0)` determines; the factor `|det g|_w^{n_T/2}|det h|_w^{−n_S/2}`
is the one that makes `φ ↦ φ(h^{−1}xg)` unitary on `L²(Hom(S_w, T_w))` (the Jacobian of `x ↦ h^{−1}xg` is `|det g|_w^{n_T}|det h|_w^{−n_S}`),
so `r = |det g|_w^{n_T/2}|det h|_w^{−n_S/2}`. For a character `χ` trivial on `𝔸_F^×` at `v` one has `χ̃(y) = χ_w(y)` on
`U(1)(F_v) ≅ E_w^×`; on a lattice stabiliser `|det g|_w = |det h|_w = 1`. (b) Complex conjugation of
Schwartz functions intertwines the Weil representations for `ψ` and `ψ̄`, and `ψ̄_F∘B_δ = ψ_F∘B_{−δ}`; each of the five
properties of §4.4a passes to the conjugate with the conjugate splitting characters (`χ̄ = χ^{−1}` for unitary `χ`); the kernel:
`Σ_x (ω̄(g, h)φ)(x) = conj Σ_x (ω(g, h)conj φ)(x)`; for `−δ` the embedding that the sibling attaches to the real place below `τ`
is `τ` instead of `c∘τ`, which exchanges the CM types of the readings. (c) is a definition; the equivalence is (b). (d) The
first sentence is the dictionary of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5(a)–(b) with `δ′ = δ` (`τ_v = c∘τ`, `ε_v = −1`) for the
characters `χ_V = χ_S`, `χ_W = χ_T`, stated there for `dim S ≤ dim T`; in rank one it reads: the vector `z^k` transforms by
`τ(g)^{−(k+a)}τ(h)^{k+b}`. For a compact group the integral of a product of a vector of type `V_σ` and one of type `V_ρ` vanishes
unless `V_σ ⊗ V_ρ` contains the trivial representation, that is `V_ρ ≅ V_σ^∨`; so a form on `U(S)` pairs with the `U(S)`-factor
`(V_{μ+a})^∨` only if it has type `V_{μ+a}` (the occurrence condition of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5 (b)), and the conjugate kernel has the
conjugate (dual) types. The Harish-Chandra parameter of `V_{(μ,0^t)+b}` is `μ + b + ρ_{n+t}` on the first `n`
entries and `S(b − n/2, t) = S(m_S/2, t)` on the last `t`; that of `V_{μ+a}` is `μ + a + ρ_n`, and `ρ_{n+t} = ρ_n + t/2` on the first
`n` entries, `a − b − t/2 = (m_T − m_S)/2`. (e) is the paragraph *Other normalisations* of ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs
§3.46a for the pair `(S^∨, δT)`. The passage from `U(S^∨)` to `U(S)` (through `g ↦ (g^{−1})^∨`) is the translation rule of
ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §0.1, which its §2.5 (b) has already applied (the types there are those of `U(X₁) = U(S)` through `τ`); the
passage to the contragredient of the member that a theta integral integrates is the orthogonality of types used above.

*Checks.* (i) Rank one, `S = T = E` with `x̄y`, characters of exponent `−2w` at `τ` (`w ∈ ½ + ℤ`): `a = b = ½ − w`; a form of
weight `λ` on `U(S)` lifts along the pair (against `θ_{S,T}`) only if `λ = k + ½ − w` for some `k ≥ 0`, and the lift has weight
`k + ½ − w = λ`: the parameter persists. With the tensor pair `S ⊗ T` instead of the homomorphism pair the same computation
sends `λ` to `−λ`: a tower of tensor pairs could not keep the parameters of its earlier stages. (ii) Two totally positive definite `S`, `T` of ranks `2`, `3`, `m_S = −20`, `m_T = −21`: `b = −9`,
`a = −9`; the form on `U(T)` with Harish-Chandra parameter `(5, −3, −10)` has highest weight `σ = (4, −3, −9) = (13, 6, 0) + b`, a
type `V_{(μ,0)+b}`, and not the dual of such a type (those have first entry `−b = 9`); so only the conjugate kernel pairs with
it, and the descent has highest weight `(4, −3)`, Harish-Chandra parameter `(9/2, −7/2) = (5, −3) − ½`.

*KNOWN* in kind (Weil; Howe; Kudla's splittings), the normal form and the conventions *NEW* in this form. The conjugate
datum of (b) and the convention (c) are this roadmap's formulation: the construction that [FormalProof] follows uses the
representation of (a) with the complex conjugates of the row forms and row vectors of 2.9a, 2.9c, which is the same pair.
(a) at the split places: [FormalProof], `Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth.lean`
(`Formalism.L2.Col.Loc.om`), `Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Col.muAt`).
*Needs:* 2.2;
ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §§0.1, 2.5; MetaplecticAutomorphicForms §3.3 and ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §4.4a; ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §3.46a.

### 2.3 Chain weight data and words

*Definitions.* Fix ranks `(k, m, t)` (2.1). A *chain weight datum* consists of, for every
`τ ∈ Φ`:

- `u(τ) = (u_0 > u_1 > ⋯ > u_{k−1}) ∈ (ℤ + ½)^k` and `v(τ) = (v_0 > ⋯ > v_{k−2}) ∈ ℤ^{k−1}`, *interlacing*:
  `u_0 > v_0 > u_1 > v_1 > ⋯ > v_{k−2} > u_{k−1}`;
- `e′(τ), e(τ) ∈ ℤ^m` with `e′_0 < e_0 < e′_1 < e_1 < ⋯ < e′_{m−1} < e_{m−1}`;

satisfying

- (W1) *gaps:* `e_i − e′_i ≥ (t + 3)/2` and `e′_{i+1} − e_i ≥ (t + 3)/2`;
- (W2) *bottom:* `−e′_0 < u_{k−1}`;
- (W3) *node separation:* `e′_{i+1} − e′_i ≥ t + 2N_m³ + 1` for all `i`;
- (W4) *nodes:* `e′_i(τ) ≥ k^{C_b}` for all `i`, with `C_b` a constant fixed before `ε`;
- (W5) *row gap:* `e′_0(τ) ≥ Sc + h_gap` and `e′_{i+1}(τ) − e′_i(τ) ≥ h_gap`, for a parameter `h_gap > 0` (with `Sc` below);
- (W6) *size:* all entries of `v(τ)`, `e′(τ)`, `e(τ)` lie in `[−b², b²]`.

The *step weights* are `w_j(τ) := e_{j−1}(τ)`, `1 ≤ j ≤ m` (one indexing throughout: steps and step weights `1 ≤ j ≤ m`,
nodes `e′_i, e_i` with `0 ≤ i < m`); the *unit condition* `(U_j)` is
`∏_{τ∈Φ} τ(ζ)^{w_j(τ)} = 1` for every root of unity `ζ ∈ E` (for `ζ = −1` it says that `Σ_{τ∈Φ} w_j(τ)` is even).

*Words.* Fix `v(τ)`, `u_0(τ)`, `u_{k−1}(τ)`, `e′(τ)`, `e(τ)` (shared by all words) with `v_l(τ) − v_{l+1}(τ) = 4Δ_w` for
`0 ≤ l < k − 2`, where the *letter step* `Δ_w ≥ 1` is an integer; put `Sc := 4kΔ_w` (the *cloud size*). A *word code* is an
injective map `𝔴 : W ↪ {0, 1}^{k−2}` from a finite set `W` of *words*; the parameter of a word `u` has
`u_l(τ) := v_{l−1}(τ) − ½ − 𝔴(u)_l·Δ_w` for `1 ≤ l ≤ k − 2` (the same bits at every `τ`) and the fixed first and last
entries; `u_* ∈ W` denotes the base word. All words share `v, e′, e` and hence the step weights. The chain takes
`Δ_w := ⌈x^{c_Δ}⌉`, with an exponent `c_Δ` chosen after `ε` and after the constants fixed before `ε`, and before the node
spacing of 2.3a; `c_Δ` is taken so large that, for `x ≥ x₀`, `(k f x^ε)^{C} ≤ Δ_w` for the constants `C` fixed before `ε`
that occur (in particular `Δ_w > k⁴f⁴`) and `N_m·m^{10} ≤ Δ_w`; and `log Δ_w ≤ x^{ε/4}/2` for `x ≥ x₀`. With these
conditions on the letter step the chain also asks `x^{ε/100}·m·log(rd_E/2) ≤ k`.

*Derived weights.* With `S(c, ℓ) := {c + (ℓ − 1)/2 − i : 0 ≤ i < ℓ}` (a segment of `ℓ` consecutive numbers centred at `c`)
and `ρ_N := ((N−1)/2, (N−3)/2, …, −(N−1)/2)`:

- the *Harish-Chandra parameter on `U(X_j)`* at `τ`: `HC_j(τ) := (u(τ), S(−w_1(τ), t+1), …, S(−w_j(τ), t+1))`;
- the *highest weight on `U(X_j)`*: `Λ_j(τ) := HC_j(τ) − ρ_{N_j}`; `λ(τ) := Λ_0(τ)`, so `λ_0(τ) = a_τ` at a top place (2.3b);
- the *row parameter on `U(Y_j)`*: `v(τ) ∪ {−e′_i(τ) : 0 ≤ i < m} ∪ ⋃_{i ≤ j} S(−w_i(τ), t)`, shifted by `−½`
  when `j` is odd (`−½` is the exponent of `η̃^{−1}` at `τ`, `η̃_τ(z) = (z/z̄)^{1/2}`: the row steps attach `μ_iη̃^{−1}` to their
  member of odd rank and `μ_i` to the other, and along a step the parameter moves by half the difference of the two
  exponents, 2.2a(d); Check (iv)); the *top weight* `σ(τ)` on `U(X^h) = U(Y_m)` is the highest weight with this parameter for
  `j = m`;
- the *model parameter of step `j`* (`1 ≤ j ≤ m`): the row parameter `(v(τ); −e′(τ))` of `Y_0`, split into its first `p_j := k + j − 1`
  entries `s⁺_j(τ) := (v_0(τ), …, v_{k−2}(τ), −e′_0(τ), …, −e′_{j−1}(τ))` (the largest, by (W2) and the interlacing) and its last
  `q_j := m − j` entries `s⁻_j(τ) := (−e′_j(τ), …, −e′_{m−1}(τ))`, indexed from `0` (so `s⁻_{j,c}(τ) = −e′_{j+c}(τ)` for `0 ≤ c < q_j`, strictly
  decreasing in `c`); `(s⁺_j(τ); s⁻_j(τ))` is the Harish-Chandra parameter at `τ` of the holomorphic discrete series of
  `U(W_j)(F_τ) ≅ U(p_j, q_j)` (signature of 2.2(c)) used by the model steps;
- the *Young diagram of step `j`*: `ν_j(τ) := Λ_{j−1}(τ) + (w_j(τ) − N_j/2)·(1, …, 1) ∈ ℤ^{N_{j−1}}`;
- `κ_j := (k + (2j − 1)(t + 1))/2 ∈ ℤ`.

*Lemma (separation).* Under (W1)–(W2): (a) the entries of `HC_j(τ)` are strictly decreasing, the segments are pairwise
disjoint and lie below `u_{k−1}(τ)`, so `Λ_j(τ)` is a dominant integral weight of `GL_{N_j}`. (b)
`Λ_j(τ) = (Λ_{j−1}(τ) − ((t+1)/2)·1, (−w_j(τ) + N_{j−1}/2)^{t+1})`; the Harish-Chandra entries of the earlier levels
are unchanged, the highest weights of the first `N_{j−1}` coordinates drop by `(t+1)/2`. (c) `ν_j(τ)` is a partition:

`ν_j(τ) = ( λ(τ) + (w_j(τ) − κ_j)·1_k , ((w_j − w_i)(τ) − (j − i)(t + 1))^{t+1} for i = 1, …, j − 1 )`,

all rows positive and decreasing; at a top place its first row is `a_τ + w_j(τ) − κ_j`, so the large entry persists at
every step. (d) Every row of `ν_j(τ)` differs from the corresponding row of `Λ^♭_{j−1}(τ) + w_j(τ)·1`,
`Λ^♭_{j−1} := (λ, (−w_1)^{t+1}, …, (−w_{j−1})^{t+1})`, by at most `N_m`. (e) Under (W1)–(W2) the row parameter of
`Y_m` interlaces `HC_m`: `u_0 > v_0 > ⋯ > v_{k−2} > u_{k−1} > −e′_0 > S(−w_1, t+1) ≻ S(−w_1, t) > −e′_1 > ⋯`, so
`σ(τ)` interlaces `Λ_m(τ)` and `V_{σ(τ)}` occurs exactly once in the restriction of `V_{Λ_m(τ)}` to `GL_{N_m − 1}`.
(g) If `d ≥ 5`, then `N_{E/K}(ζ) = 1` for every root of unity
`ζ ∈ E`; hence `∏_{τ∈Φ} τ(ζ) = 1`, and `(U_j)` is unchanged when an integer constant is added to `w_j(τ)` at every `τ`. So for
parallel step weights (`w_j(τ) − w_1(τ)` independent of `τ` for every `j`) the conditions `(U_j)` for all `j` are equivalent
to `(U_1)`; for weights that are not parallel `(U_j)` is a condition for each `j`.
(h) *Entries of different signs.* Under (W1)–(W3), for `0 ≤ j ≤ m` and every `τ`, any two entries of the row parameter
of `Y_j` with different signs (the sign is `−` exactly for the nodes `−e′_i` with `i > j`, `i − j` odd) differ by at least
`t + 2N_m³ + 1`. (Lemma (f) is in 2.3b. The subsections 2.3b and 2.3c come before 2.3a, whose existence statement uses them.)

Route: (a) `u_{k−1} > −e′_0 ≥ −w_1 + (t+3)/2` by (W1)–(W2), while the top of `S(−w_1, t+1)` is `−w_1 + t/2`; consecutive
segments are `≥ t + 3` apart by (W1). (b) `ρ_{N_j, i} = ρ_{N_{j−1}, i} + (t+1)/2` for `i < N_{j−1}`, and the new entries
`−w_j + t/2 − a` minus `(t − N_{j−1} − 2a)/2` give `−w_j + N_{j−1}/2`. (c) Iterate (b): the first block of `Λ_{j−1}` is
`λ − (j−1)(t+1)/2` and the block created at step `i` is `−w_i + N_{i−1}/2 − (j−1−i)(t+1)/2`; add `w_j − N_j/2`. Since
`λ_{k−1} = u_{k−1} + (k−1)/2 > −w_1 + (t+3)/2 + (k−1)/2` and `w_j − w_1 ≥ (j−1)(t+3)`, the last row of the first block is
`> (w_j − w_1) + (t + 2 − (2j−1)(t+1))/2 ≥ (4j − 3)/2 > 0`; it dominates the rows of block 1 because
`u_{k−1} ≥ −w_1 + (t+2)/2`; block rows are positive and decrease because `w_{i+1} − w_i ≥ t + 3`. (d) The shifts are
`κ_j ≤ N_m` and `(j − i)(t + 1) ≤ N_m`. (e) Compare entries; interlacing gives multiplicity one by the branching rule
for `GL_N ↓ GL_{N−1}` (ClassicalGroups §6.1). (g) Let `ζ` have order `n`. For `n ≤ 2`, `N_{E/K}(ζ) = ζ^{[E:K]} = 1`
because `f` is even. For `n ≥ 3`, `K ⊄ ℚ(ζ)`: otherwise `d | n` (`K` has conductor `d`) and `ζ_d ∈ E`, so the inertia
group at `d` would have order at least `d − 1 > 2`; hence `Gal(ℚ(ζ)K/K) ≅ Gal(ℚ(ζ)/ℚ)` and
`N_{E/K}(ζ) = N_{ℚ(ζ)/ℚ}(ζ)^{[E : ℚ(ζ)K]} = 1`, the primitive `n`-th roots of unity pairing off with their inverses.
(h) An entry of sign `−` is `−e′_i` with `i > j`. Another node is at distance `≥ t + 2N_m³ + 1` by (W3). An entry of a
string `S(−w_a, t)`, `a ≤ j`, is at distance `≥ (e′_i − e′_a) + (e′_a − e_{a−1}) − (t−1)/2 ≥ (t + 2N_m³ + 1) + 2` by (W3) and
(W1), since `i > a`. An entry `v_l` exceeds `−e′_0` by (W2) and the interlacing, and `i ≥ 1`.

*Checks.* (i) The highest weights move and the Harish-Chandra parameters do not: a definition that kept `λ` as the
first block of `Λ_j` (as `Λ^♭_j` does) has the right Harish-Chandra parameters only after the shift `ρ_{N_j} − ρ_{N_{j−1}}`,
so its "diagram" `Λ^♭_{j−1} + w_j` is not the polynomial degree of the step (it exceeds `ν_j` by `κ_j` on the first `k`
rows). (ii) For `j = 1` the diagram is `ν_1 = λ + (w_1 − (k + t + 1)/2)·1_k`, a partition with `k` rows, and
`|ν_1| = |λ| + k(w_1 − κ_1)`. (iii) A datum with `Σ_τ w_j(τ)` odd violates `(U_j)` at `ζ = −1`; for it the character `μ_j` of 2.4(b) does not exist (2.4(a) at
`x = √−d`, with `√−d/conj(√−d) = −1`). (iv) *The top step and the shift.* At the top row step `(Y_{m−1}, Y_m)`, both
totally positive definite (2.2(a)), with `χ_{Y_{m−1}} = μ_m` (even rank `N_{m−1}`) and `χ_{Y_m} = μ_mη̃^{−1}` (odd rank), so
`m_S = −2w_m(τ)` and `m_T = −2w_m(τ) − 1`: the last `t` entries of `σ(τ)` all equal `n_{m−1}/2 − w_m(τ) = b` of 2.2a(d), so `V_{σ(τ)}`
is a type `V_{(μ,0^t)+b}` with `μ = (σ_i(τ) − b)_{i<n_{m−1}}` a partition, while the dual of such a type has its first `t` entries
equal and the first entries of `σ(τ)` (from `v(τ)`) are pairwise distinct: only the conjugate kernel pairs with the forms of
weight `σ`, and the descended parameter is that of stage `m` without `S(−w_m, t)`, shifted by `(m_T − m_S)/2 = −½`; at the
other stages the same rule gives `+½` from an odd to an even stage and `−½` from an even to an odd one, hence the shift
`−½` exactly at the odd stages.

*NEW* (definitions); the lemma is elementary. The data `u(τ)`, `v(τ)`, `e′(τ)`, `e(τ)` with the interlacing, (W1), (W2),
the step weights, `(U_j)`, the words and `Sc`: [FormalProof], `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/Params.lean`
(`Formalism.BasicNotions.HCParam`), `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataHost.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.Host.MuCond`),
`Formalism/SharpRestriction/CuspSpanNormBounds/DataS.lean` (`Formalism.MainNotions.S.Draft.SData`); (W3)–(W6) follow from
[FormalProof], `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/HeightBoundGeFourthPowerRank.lean`
(`Formalism.BasicNotions.ParameterChoice'.nodeGap_ge_cubic_rank`),
`Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/HeightBoundGeFourthPowerRank.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.D7.NodeGap`).
*Needs:* 2.1, 2.2, 2.2a; ClassicalGroups §6.1.

### 2.3b Adapted weight data

*Definitions and lemma (adapted data).* A chain weight datum (2.3) is *adapted* to a host
with tops `Φ_top` and a translate `u₀` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.6), to a scale (2.1) and to the letter step `Δ_w` if:

- at a top place `τ ∈ Φ_top`: `u_0(τ) = a_τ + (k−1)/2`, `u_{k−1}(τ) = −a⁻_τ − (k−1)/2` with integers `a_τ`, `a⁻_τ` in the
  *top window* `a ≤ a_τ ≤ a(1 + θ_a)`, where `θ_a := 1/(k·x^{A_w/2})` with a constant `A_w ≥ 2` fixed before `ε` (so `θ_a ≤ 1/k`,
  and `θ_a ≤ x^{−2}` under `SizeK` and `SizeM`), and the *bottom window* `b′ ≤ a⁻_τ ≤ 3b′/2` (in the construction
  `a⁻_τ ≤ b′(1 + 2|A|x^{−ε/8}·x^{2ε+ε/100}/k)`, so `a⁻_τ = b′(1 + o(1))`), the first node satisfies
  `e′_0(τ) − a⁻_τ > (k − 1)/2 + 3`, the first entry satisfies the cloud gap `u_0(τ) − v_0(τ) ≥ Sc`, the last entry satisfies
  `e′_0(τ)/2 ≤ −u_{k−1}(τ) ≤ e′_0(τ) − 3` (the upper bound follows from the condition on the first node), and the other
  entries of `u(τ)` lie in `[0, Sc]`;
- at every other place the entries `u_l(τ)`, `1 ≤ l ≤ k − 2`, lie in `[0, Sc]`, the first entry satisfies the *cloud gap*
  `Sc ≤ u_0(τ) − v_0(τ) ≤ C_c·Sc`, with a constant `C_c ≥ 1` fixed before `ε` (`C_c = 2` in the construction and in the
  standing data of the Gram bounds of the row spaces), and
  `−Sc ≤ u_{k−1}(τ) < v_{k−2}(τ)` (in the construction, and in the standing data of the Gram bounds of the row spaces,
  `u_{k−1}(τ) = v_{k−2}(τ) − ½`);
- at every place the entries of `v(τ)` lie in `[0, Sc]`, and the nodes satisfy `e′_0(τ) ≥ 8·Sc` and `e′_{m−1}(τ) ≤ 2e′_0(τ)`
  (so the entries `v(τ)` of the row parameter `(v(τ); −e′(τ))` of `Y_0` lie in `[0, e′_0(τ)/8]` and the entries `−e′(τ)` in
  `[−2e′_0(τ), −e′_0(τ)]`);
- the step weights lie in three windows: `w_j(τ) ∈ [b, b + ϑ + 1]` at the thick places `cσ_d u₀𝒯` of `u₀` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.6),
  `w_j(τ) ∈ [a⁻_τ + b′/x, a⁻_τ + b′/x + ϑ]` at the top places, and `w_j(τ) ∈ [w″, w″ + ϑ]` at the remaining (thin)
  places; the left ends `w₀(τ) := b`, `a⁻_τ + b′/x`, `w″` are the *base sizes*;
- the parameters stay near the step weights: `e′_0(τ) ≥ w_1(τ) − ϑ`, and `ϑ ≤ b′/4`;
- the unit conditions `(U_j)` hold for `1 ≤ j ≤ m`;
- `SizeSc`: `Sc ≤ x^{C_S}`, where the exponent `C_S` may depend on `ε` (it is chosen after `ε`, with `c_Δ`, and before the
  scale). This form is this roadmap's, not a predicate of [FormalProof], whose conditions on `Sc` are `Sc ≤ e^{x^{ε/4}}`,
  `k·Sc ≤ w″` and `4Sc ≤ Λ_log·b′/k` (`Λ_log` the log-size parameter of the local gains of the chain); `SizeSc` holds at
  the values of [FormalProof] (`Sc = 4k⌈x^{c_Δ}⌉`) with `C_S` chosen after `c_Δ`.

The *window* is `η_w := t + max_τ(e_{m−1}(τ) − e′_0(τ))`.

*Lemma.* (f) For an adapted datum and `x ≥ x₀(ε)` (under `Size(x, ε)` and `SizeSc`): at a top place `λ_0(τ) = a_τ` and
`λ_{k−1}(τ) = −a⁻_τ`; every other entry of `λ(τ)`, at every `τ`, has absolute value `≤ (C_c + 1)·Sc + (k − 1)/2` (so every
entry of `u(τ)` has absolute value `≤ 2a ≤ b²`), and
`|v_l(τ)| ≤ Sc ≤ min(b, w″/x)`; `w_1(τ) < ⋯ < w_m(τ)` and `w_1(τ) ≥ −λ_{k−1}(τ)` at every `τ`; every step weight at a
place that is not thick is `≤ 2b′`; and `η_w ≤ 2ϑ + t + 1`.

Route: (f) `λ_l = u_l − (k−1)/2 + l`; `Sc` is polynomial in
`x` while `b`, `w″ = b/(X′|A|)` are not; at a top place `w_j ≤ 3b′/2 + b′/x + b′/4 ≤ 2b′` and `w_1 ≥ a⁻_τ + b′/x > −λ_{k−1}`,
at a thin place `w_j ≤ w″ + ϑ ≤ 2b′`, and elsewhere `−λ_{k−1} = −u_{k−1} − (k−1)/2 ≤ Sc ≤ w″ ≤ w_1`; the step weights lie
in a window of width `≤ ϑ + 1` and `e′_0 ≥ w_1 − ϑ`.

*NEW* (definitions); the lemma is elementary. *Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.6, 2.1, 2.3.

### 2.3c Thresholds, separation and the gap size

*Definitions (thresholds, separation, the gap size).* For a chain weight datum (2.3) on a host
of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 at a scale (2.1), put `D_* := |A|·t²·(N_m + t)·x^{2ε} + k·|A|²·(Sc + t + x^ε)`, `ℓ* := m b/D_*` and
`ℓ_A := k x^ε |A|/(m|𝒯|)`; under `Size(x, ε)` and `SizeSc`, `D_* ≤ x^{C}` (`C` depending on `C_S` and on the
constants fixed before `ε`), so
`X″ < b′ < ℓ* ≤ b·x^{−ε} < b` and `ℓ*² > 8b` for `x ≥ x₀(ε)` (`D_* ≥ k|A|²x^ε ≥ m x^ε`, and `ℓ* ≥ b·x^{−C}`), and every prime
dividing `f_E` is `< ℓ*` under `SizeN`. Let `η_s` be a number with
`|e′_i(τ) − e_j(τ)| ≤ η_s` for all `τ, i, j` and with every step weight in `[w₀(τ), w₀(τ) + η_s + 1]` for its base size `w₀(τ)`
(2.3b). The datum is *separated* if

- (W7) `|e′_i(τ) − e_j(τ)| ≥ 2b/ℓ* + t/2 + 3` for all `τ`, `i`, `j`;
- `SizeSep`: `b′ + Sc + η_s + b/ℓ* < ℓ*`, `2η_s + t + 8b/ℓ* + 7 < ℓ*`, and `w₀(τ) > η_s` for every `τ`.

`SizeGap`: `h_gap ≥ C_gap·r^{19} f⁵ log(b·rd_E)` with a constant `C_gap ≥ 120` fixed before `ε`. With `p_S := k + r_S`,
`q_S := r_S + 1`, it implies the polynomial bounds `h_gap ≥ 120r³`, `h_gap > 32r²m + 8r m^{3/2}`, `h_gap > 16√(2q_S)·r·q_S`,
`h_gap > r·p_S·q_S + 1` and `h_gap > 8√(2q_S)(4r²√(2q_S) + 2r q_S)`, `h_gap ≥ 100r⁴`, and, for `h := h_gap/4`, with
`a(h) := h/(8√(2q_S))` and `d_N := r_S(2r_S + 2k + 1)`: `a(h) ≥ 4(r_S² + r_S + 1)((r − 1)d_N + 4p_Sq_S) + 4p_Sq_S + 3` and
`a(h) ≥ 9q_Sr² + 12p_Sq_S + 3` (the right sides are at most `21r⁵` and `24r³`, while `a(h_gap/4) ≥ 120r^{19}/(32√(2r)) > 2.6r^{18.5}`).
Together with (W5), `SizeW` and `SizeMf`, `SizeGap` gives the two hypotheses under which the chain instances of the
Bessel functions and of the relative Schur orthogonality of the row spaces are established: the node gaps
`e′_{i+1}(τ) − e′_i(τ) ≥ 16r^{19}f⁵·log b ≥ 16r^{19}` (the clause `C_lr^{19}f⁵L ≤ e′_{j+1}(τ) − e′_j(τ)` of the standing
conditions of the Gram bounds, with `C_l = 16` and `L = log b`), and `r ≥ 1801`, since `SizeW` gives `29m⁵f·log b ≤ k`
for `x ≥ x₀(ε)` (the clause `C′m⁵fL ≤ k` there, with `C′ = 29`) and so `r ≥ k ≥ 29·2⁵·2⁴ > 1801` (`m ≥ 2`, `f ≥ m⁴`).
*Gaps of the row parameter.* For an adapted datum (2.3b) with (W5) for an `h_gap` with `SizeGap`, and under `SizeMf` (2.1),
consecutive entries of the row parameter `(v(τ); −e′(τ))` of `Y_0` differ by at least `100r⁴` (so by more than `1000(r + 1)³`
when `r ≥ 13`), by more than `k m⁴ + r m^{10}`, and, for every integer `0 ≤ s ≤ 2r`, by more than `120(r + m·s)³`. Indeed the
gaps inside `v(τ)` are `4Δ_w > 4k⁴f⁴ ≥ 4k⁴m^{16}` (2.3, `f ≥ m⁴`), the others are at least `h_gap ≥ 120r^{19}`
((W5), `v(τ) ⊂ [0, Sc]`, `e′_0 ≥ Sc + h_gap`), and with `r < 2k`, `m < k`, `k ≥ 5`, `m ≥ 2`: `100r⁴ < 1600k⁴`,
`k m⁴ + r m^{10} < 3k m^{10}`, `120(r + 2mr)³ ≤ 3240m³r³ < 25920m³k³`, all below `4k⁴m^{16}` and below `120r^{19}`. (These are
the gap conditions of the Gram bounds and of the separation statements for the row spaces.)

*Check.* A datum with `e_i − e′_i = (t + 3)/2` satisfies (W1) and violates (W7).

*NEW* (definitions; the implications are elementary). `D_*`, `ℓ*`, `ℓ_A` and the first two clauses of `SizeSep`:
[FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/ElevenLe.lean` (`Formalism.MainNotions.szlStar`); (W7), `SizeGap` and the gaps
of the row parameter follow from [FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/ElevenLe.lean` (`Formalism.MainNotions.SizeNodes`).
*Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, 2.1, 2.3, 2.3b.

### 2.3a Existence of adapted, separated data

*Existence of adapted, separated data.* Let a host of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 satisfying the
hypotheses of this layer with `d ≥ 5` be given, with tops `Φ_top`, a translate `u₀` with at least one thick place, a
scale satisfying `Size(x, ε)`, ranks `(k, m, t)`, integer top weights `a_τ` in the top window and integer bottom entries
`a⁻_τ ∈ [b′, 3b′/2]` (`τ ∈ Φ_top`), the letter step `Δ_w` of 2.3, `h_gap` with `SizeGap`, an even integer
`h₀ ≥ max(t + 2N_m³ + 1, h_gap, 4b/ℓ* + t + 6)` and a width `ϑ` with `SizeTheta` and `m·h₀ ≤ ϑ ≤ w″/x` (the construction takes
`ϑ = m·h₀`), where `μ_E` is the group of roots of unity of `E`.
Put `v_l(τ) := (k − 2 − l)·4Δ_w`; off the tops `u_0(τ) := v_0(τ) + Sc + ½` (so that
`u_0(τ) − v_0(τ) = Sc + ½`) and `u_{k−1}(τ) := −½`, and the values of 2.3b at the tops; base points
`b₁(τ) := a⁻_τ + ⌊b′/x⌋` at the tops, `⌈w″⌉` at the thin places and `⌈b⌉` at the thick places other than one thick place
`τ₁`, where `b₁(τ₁) := ⌈b⌉ + s₁` with an integer `s₁ ∈ [0, |μ_E|)`; and
`e′_i(τ) := b₁(τ) + i·h₀`, `e_i(τ) := e′_i(τ) + h₀/2` (`0 ≤ i < m`). Then for `x ≥ x₀(ε)` there is exactly one
`s₁ ∈ [0, |μ_E|)` for which this is a chain weight datum adapted to the host, `u₀`, the scale and `Δ_w`, separated,
with (W5) for `h_gap`; its step weights are `w_j(τ) = b₁(τ) + (j − ½)h₀`, pairwise different at every `τ`.

Route: (W1) and (W7): `e_i − e′_i = e′_{i+1} − e_i = h₀/2` and `|e′_i − e_j| ≥ h₀/2`; (W3), (W5) from `h₀`; (W2), (W4),
(W5) at `i = 0`, (W6) and `e′_0 ≥ 8·Sc` because `b₁(τ) ≥ w″ ≥ x·ϑ ≥ x·h_gap`, `w″` exceeds every fixed power of `x` (so `k^{C_b}`
and `Sc`); `e′_{m−1} = b₁ + (m − 1)h₀ ≤ 2b₁` because `(m − 1)h₀ ≤ ϑ ≤ w″`; `v_l = (k − 2 − l)·4Δ_w ∈ [0, Sc]`; the cloud gap:
`u_0(τ) − v_0(τ) = Sc + ½ ∈ [Sc, 2Sc]` off the tops; and `b₁(τ) > a⁻_τ + b′/x − 1 > a⁻_τ + (k − 1)/2 + 3` at the tops, and
`w_1(τ) = b₁(τ) + h₀/2 ≥ a⁻_τ + b′/x`; at the tops the cloud gap holds, `u_0(τ) − v_0(τ) ≥ a − Sc ≥ Sc` as
`a = x·b ≥ 2w″ ≥ 2Sc`; at the tops the bounds for the last entry hold, `e′_0(τ)/2 ≤ a⁻_τ < −u_{k−1}(τ) < e′_0(τ) − 3`, as
`e′_0(τ) = b₁(τ) ≤ a⁻_τ + b′/x ≤ 2a⁻_τ` and `−u_{k−1}(τ) = a⁻_τ + (k − 1)/2`;
the windows: `w_1 = b₁ + h₀/2` and `w_m = b₁ + (m − ½)h₀`, and
`b₁(τ) ≤ w₀(τ) + h₀/2` at every place (at a thick place because `|μ_E| ≤ 8f² ≤ h₀/2`, by an argument of this roadmap:
`φ(|μ_E|)` divides `[E:ℚ] = 2f` and `φ(n) ≥ √(n/2)`, while `h₀ ≥ h_gap ≥ 120r^{19}` and `r ≥ f`), so the width `ϑ ≥ m·h₀`
suffices at every kind of place, `e′_0 = w_1 − h₀/2 ≥ w_1 − ϑ`, and `ϑ ≤ w″/x < b′/4`; `SizeSep` with
`η_s := ϑ`: every base size is `≥ w″ > ϑ`,
`b/ℓ* = D_*/m ≤ x^C` and `ℓ* ≥ b·x^{−C}`. The unit condition: by 2.3(g),
`∏_τ τ(ζ)^{w_j(τ)} = ∏_τ τ(ζ)^{b₁(τ)}` for `ζ ∈ μ_E`; for a generator `ζ_E` of `μ_E`, `τ₁(ζ_E)` has order `|μ_E|`, so
exactly one residue of `s₁` modulo `|μ_E|` makes `∏_τ τ(ζ_E)^{b₁(τ)} = 1`, and then `(U_j)` holds for every `ζ ∈ μ_E`
and every `j`.

*Check.* `μ_E = {±1}`: the condition is that `Σ_τ b₁(τ)` be even, and `s₁ ∈ {0, 1}` corrects its parity.

*NEW* (elementary). *Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.6, 2.1, 2.3, 2.3b, 2.3c.

### 2.4 Hecke characters of the chain

(a) *Existence criterion.* Let `E/F`
be a CM extension unramified at every finite place, `η = η_{E/F}`, `n_η ∈ {0, 1}`, `S_θ` a finite set of finite places of
`F` split in `E`, for each `v ∈ S_θ` a place `w_v | v` of `E` and a continuous character `θ_v` of `𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^×`, a CM type `Φ`
of `E` (a set of embeddings `E ↪ ℂ` containing exactly one of `τ`, `τ̄ = conj∘τ` for every embedding `τ`; for the host, the `Φ` of the
Conventions), and integers `m_τ ≡ n_η (mod 2)` for `τ ∈ Φ` (replacing `τ` by `τ̄` in `Φ` and `m_τ` by `−m_τ` changes neither the
character nor `(★)`). Put `P_{E/F} := {x ∈ E^× : (x) = 𝔞_x𝒪_E for a fractional ideal 𝔞_x of F}` and
`η(𝔞) := ∏_{v finite} η_v(ϖ_v)^{ord_v 𝔞}`. There is a unitary Hecke character `χ` of `E` with

- `χ|_{𝔸_F^×} = η^{n_η}`;
- `χ` unramified at every finite place of `E` not above `S_θ`, `χ_{w_v}|_{𝒪^×} = θ_v` and `χ_{w̄_v}|_{𝒪^×} = θ_v^{−1}` (through
  `𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^× = 𝒪_{F_v}^× = 𝒪_{E_{w̄_v}}^×`) for `v ∈ S_θ`;
- `χ_τ(z) = (z/|z|)^{m_τ}` at the archimedean place of `τ ∈ Φ`;

if and only if

`(★)` `η^{n_η}(𝔞_x) · ∏_{v ∈ S_θ} θ_v(x_{w_v}/x_{w̄_v}) · ∏_{τ ∈ Φ} (τ(x)/|τ(x)|)^{m_τ} = 1` for every `x ∈ P_{E/F}`.

For `x ∈ P_{E/F}` the quotient `ζ_x := x/x̄` is a root of unity; for `n_η = 0` condition `(★)` reads
`∏_{v∈S_θ} θ_v(ζ_x) · ∏_τ τ(ζ_x)^{m_τ/2} = 1` on the subgroup `μ_{E/F} := {x/x̄ : x ∈ P_{E/F}}` of `μ_E`; in particular, if the
reductions of `μ_{E/F}` at the places `w_v` are jointly injective, then for every infinity type the characters `θ_v` of
depth zero satisfying `(★)` form one coset of the group of those trivial on the image of `μ_{E/F}`. Two characters with the
same data differ by an unramified character of `E^×𝔸_F^×\𝔸_E^×` trivial at infinity (a character of a finite class group).

(b) *The characters of the chain.* Let the host be as in the introduction of this layer, `g₀ ≠ g₁` primes of the form
`g = N_{K/ℚ}(π_g)`, `π_g ∈ 𝒪_K`, splitting completely in `E`, an integer `o_δ ≥ 2`, distinct primes `p_δ, p′_δ ≡ 1 (mod 2o_δ f_E)`
not in `{g₀, g₁}` (2.7 provides all of them), and a chain weight datum (2.3) with the unit conditions `(U_j)` be given. For each type prime
the prime `π_g𝒪_K` of `K` over `g` is a fixed choice (one of the two; the congruence `π ≡ 1 (mod f_E𝒪_K)` of 2.7 holds for
`π̄` as well and does not single one out). Then:

- *Type characters.* For `g ∈ {g₀, g₁}` and a place `v` of `F` above `g`, let `w_v` be the place of `E`
  above `v` and above `π_g𝒪_K`; its residue field is `𝒪_K/π_g = 𝔽_g`. Fix a generator `ω_g` of the group of characters
  `𝔽_g^× → μ_{g−1}` (for instance the Teichmüller character), the same for all `v | g` and all `j`, and put `χ_{v,j} := ω_g^{2j}`
  (`1 ≤ j ≤ m`), read on `𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^×` through the reduction; these are the *type characters* of step `j`, squares, of depth
  zero.
- `μ_1, …, μ_m` exist: `n_η = 0`, `μ_{j,τ}(z) = (z/z̄)^{−w_j(τ)}` (that is `m_τ = −2w_j(τ)`), unramified outside the places
  above `g₀g₁`, tame there, with `μ_j|_{𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^×} = χ_{v,j}^{−1} = ω_g^{−2j}∘(reduction)` and `μ_j|_{𝒪_{E_{w̄_v}}^×} = χ_{v,j}` at
  every place `v` of `F` above `g₀g₁`: the step characters are tamely ramified at the type places with residue a power of
  `ω_g`, and the type characters are the inverses of these residues. The `μ_j` have pairwise different infinity types.
- `η̃`, an unramified splitting character (`n_η = 1`, `m_τ = 1` for all `τ`, `S_θ = ∅`, so `η̃|_{𝔸_F^×} = η_{E/F}`, `η̃` unramified at
  every finite place, of type `z/|z|` at every `τ ∈ Φ`), exists if and only if
  `η(𝔞_x)·∏_{τ∈Φ} τ(x)/|τ(x)| = 1` for every `x ∈ P_{E/F}` (the instance of `(★)`; it contains
  `(−1)^f = 1` at `x = −1`). This condition holds under `HostSplitting E` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3: `4 | f`, and every place of `F` above a prime dividing
  `f_E` splits in `E`; under the hypotheses of this layer only the places above `d` need checking, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3(e)): this is why the
  chain asks `HostSplitting E` of its hosts. The hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, which have `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)`
  and layer `n ≥ 2 = v₂(d + 1) − 1`, satisfy it (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(vi)), in particular those with a
  `B`-admissible pair (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.9(vi)); so `η̃` exists for these hosts (which have `4 | f`, so `f ≥ 4`).
- `δ, δ′` exist: `n_η = 0`, `m_τ = 0` (so of finite
  order), ramified exactly at the places above `p_δ` (resp. `p′_δ`), tame there with inertial image of order `o_δ`. The
  chain takes `o_δ` such that, for every prime `ℓ`, the prime-to-`ℓ` part of `o_δ` is at least `3` (that is, `o_δ` is
  neither a prime power nor twice a prime power; for instance `o_δ = 15`). Then for every prime `ℓ` and every place `v`
  of `E` above `p_δ` or `p′_δ` there is a tame inertia element `y` at `v`, of order prime to `ℓ·N(v)` in every finite
  quotient, with `δ_v(y)² − 1` a unit at every place above `ℓ` (`δ_v` the component of `δ` or `δ′` there).
- *Algebraicity.* `μ_j`, `δ`, `δ′` and `η̃²` take their values in number fields, and so does `η̃·‖·‖_{𝔸_E}^{1/2}` (the algebraic
  normalisation of `η̃`, of infinity type `z` at every `τ ∈ Φ`); `η̃` itself does not: no number field contains all its values
  (Check (vi)) (a remark of this roadmap, not used later).
- *Bad places.* `μ_j`, `η̃`, `δ`, `δ′` are unramified outside the set `S₀` of the places above `g₀`, `g₁`, `p_δ`, `p′_δ` (the two
  type primes and the two auxiliary primes), and `δ`, `δ′` are unramified at the places above `g₀g₁`. The four primes are
  unramified in `E` (the type primes split completely in `E`, and `p_δ, p′_δ ≡ 1 (mod f_E)`), so the places of `S₀` are prime
  to `f_E` and to the different of `F/ℚ`.

Route: put `U := ∏_{w finite} 𝒪_{E_w}^× × E_∞^×`; `H := E^× 𝔸_F^× U` is open of finite index in `𝔸_E^×` (the quotient is
a quotient of the class group of `E`). Define `φ` on `𝔸_F^× U` by `η^{n_η}` on `𝔸_F^×`, `θ_v`, `θ_v^{−1}` on the units at
`w_v`, `w̄_v`, `1` on the other local units and `(z/|z|)^{m_τ}` at infinity; it is consistent on `𝔸_F^× ∩ U` because `η`
is unramified at the finite places, `θ_vθ_v^{−1} = 1` on `𝒪_{F_v}^×` and `(x/|x|)^{m_τ} = sgn(x)^{n_η} = η_τ^{n_η}(x)` on `ℝ^×`.
`φ` extends to `E^×𝔸_F^×U` trivially on `E^×` exactly when it is `1` on `E^× ∩ 𝔸_F^×U`, which is `P_{E/F}` (at an inert
place every element of `E_w^×` lies in `F_v^×𝒪_{E_w}^×`; at a split place this means equal valuations at `w` and `w̄`);
writing `x = a·(x_f/a_f)·x_∞` with `a ∈ 𝔸_{F,f}^×` generating `𝔞_x` gives the value `(★)`. Characters of a finite
index open subgroup extend to `𝔸_E^×` (Pontryagin duality). For `x ∈ P_{E/F}` the ideal of `x/x̄` is trivial and all its
archimedean absolute values are `1`, so `x/x̄ ∈ μ_E` (Kronecker); `x_{w}/x_{w̄}` is `ζ_x` read at `w`, and
`(τ(x)/|τ(x)|)^{2n} = τ(ζ_x)^n`. (b) For `μ_j`: take `S_θ` the places of `F` above `g₀g₁` and `θ_v := χ_{v,j}^{−1}` at `w_v`. The
places `w_v`, `v | g`, are the places of `E` above `π_g`, permuted simply transitively by `Φ = Gal(E/K)`, so the type part of
`(★)` at `g` is `ω_g(∏_{τ∈Φ} τ(ζ_x) mod π_g)^{−2j} = ω_g(N_{E/K}(ζ_x) mod π_g)^{−2j} = 1` by 2.3(g) (`d ≥ 7`); the archimedean part
is `∏_τ τ(ζ_x)^{−w_j(τ)} = 1` by `(U_j)`. For `δ`: a character of `𝔽_{p_δ}^×` of order `o_δ` trivial on the image of `μ_E`
exists because `o_δ·|μ_E|` divides `p_δ − 1` (`μ_E ⊂ μ_{2f_E}`); take it at every place above `p_δ`. For the inertia
element: let `n ≥ 3` be the prime-to-`ℓ` part of `o_δ`, `y₀` a tame inertia element whose image under `δ_v` has order `n`, and
`y` the component of `y₀` of order prime to `ℓ·N(v)`; then `δ_v(y)²` is a root of unity of order prime to `ℓ` and `≠ 1`
(its order divides `n` and `n ∤ 2`), so `δ_v(y)² − 1` divides a power of the order of `δ_v(y)²` and is a unit at `ℓ`. For `η̃`
under `HostSplitting E`: `x ↦ x/x̄` maps `P_{E/F}` onto `μ_{E/F}` with kernel `F^×`, on which `(★)` is the product formula, so it
suffices to check `(★)` at one `x` for a generator `ζ` of `μ_{E/F}`. For `ζ = −1` take `x = √−d`: its ideal is the product of the
primes of `F` above `d`, on which `η = 1` because they split in `E`, and every phase `τ(√−d)/|τ(√−d)|` is `i`, with `i^f = 1` as
`4 | f`. For `ζ ≠ ±1` take `x = 1 + ζ` (`x/x̄ = ζ`): its ideal is supported above primes dividing `f_E`, where `η = 1`, and its
phases are `e^{iθ_τ/2}` with `τ(ζ) = e^{iθ_τ}`, `|θ_τ| < π`; since `K ⊄ ℚ(ζ)` (2.3(g)), `Φ` contains an element acting on `ℚ(ζ)` as
complex conjugation, so the `θ_τ` come in opposite pairs and the product is `1`. The cyclic hosts: FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(vi), FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.9(vi). *Bad
places:* by construction (`S_θ` is the set of places above `g₀g₁` for `μ_j`, the places above
`p_δ`, resp. `p′_δ`, for `δ`, `δ′`, and empty for `η̃`), and `p_δ, p′_δ ∉ {g₀, g₁}`. *Algebraicity:* a unitary Hecke character
`χ` with `χ_τ(z) = (z/z̄)^{n_τ}`, `n_τ ∈ ℤ`, takes at the finite part of a principal idele `α ∈ E^×` the value
`χ_∞(α)^{−1} = ∏_{τ∈Φ}(τ(α)/τ̄(α))^{−n_τ} ∈ ι₀(E)`; it has finite order on `∏_w 𝒪_{E_w}^×` (a continuous character of a profinite
group); and the `h_E`-th power of a finite idele is a principal idele times a unit; so its values lie in the extension of
`ι₀(E)` by finitely many roots of unity and `h_E`-th roots, a number field. This applies to `μ_j` (`n_τ = −w_j(τ)`), to `δ`, `δ′`
(`n_τ = 0`) and to `η̃²` (`n_τ = 1`); for `χ′ := η̃·‖·‖_{𝔸_E}^{1/2}` (trivial on `E^×` by the product formula) `χ′_∞(α) = ∏_{τ∈Φ}τ(α)`,
so `χ′(α_f) = N_{E/K}(α)^{−1}` (read through `ι₀`), and the same argument applies.

*Checks.* (i) For `x ∈ F^×` condition `(★)` is the product formula `η(x) = 1`. (ii) For `x = −1`, `n_η = 1`, `S_θ = ∅` and
all `m_τ = 1`, `(★)` reads `(−1)^f = 1`: an everywhere unramified splitting character of infinity type `z/|z|` at every
place of `Φ` forces `[F:ℚ]` even, as FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1 forces it for `E/F` unramified. Evenness of `f` is not sufficient for
the existence of `η̃`: for `E = ℚ(√−3, √−7)`, `K = ℚ(√−7)` (`f = 2`) the condition fails at `x = √−7`; both conditions of
`HostSplitting E` fail there (`f = 2`, and the prime of `F` above `3` is inert in `E`). (iii) Dropping
the hypothesis that `E/F` is unramified fails already for `E = K`: no unitary character of `K` restricting to `χ_K` on
`𝔸_ℚ^×` is unramified at `d`. (iv) For `o_δ = 6` and `ℓ = 3` the prime-to-`ℓ` part is `2` and `δ_v(y)² = 1` for every `y`
of order prime to `3`: the inertia element of (b) does not exist. (v) For the field of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4
with `ψ = 1`, `(q, d) = (3, 71)` and the layer `n = 1`, below the range `n ≥ 2` of the hosts, `HostSplitting E` fails
(FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, Checks: the places of `F` above `71` are inert in `E`); `η̃` is then not
provided by this target. (vi) *`η̃` is not algebraic.* Let `p` be a prime splitting completely in the Galois closure over `ℚ` of
the Hilbert class field of `E` (infinitely many, Chebotarev), and `𝔓 = (α)` a prime of `E` above `p` (principal, of norm `p`). As `η̃`
is trivial on `E^×` and unramified, `η̃(ϖ_𝔓) = η̃_∞(α)^{−1} = (∏_{τ∈Φ}τ(α)/|τ(α)|)^{−1} = √p/N_{E/K}(α)` (`∏_{τ∈Φ}|τ(α)| = N_{E/ℚ}(α)^{1/2}`);
the fields `K(√p)` for these `p` are pairwise different, so the values of `η̃` generate an infinite extension of `ℚ`.

*KNOWN* in kind (Hecke; Weil 1955). The criterion in this generality, with its "only if" direction and the description
of the solutions, is this roadmap's own formulation, proved by the extension argument above; [FormalProof] establishes
the existence of the characters of (b) for the hosts of the chain, which is the case of the "if" direction used later.

### 2.5 Twisting characters and twisting data

(a) *Admissible twisting primes.* Let `E/F` be a CM extension,
`ℓ` a prime, `M ≥ 1` and `Z` a finite set of places of `E`. Put `E_{ℓ,M} := E(μ_{ℓ^M}, β^{1/ℓ^M} : β ∈ 𝒪_E[1/ℓ]^×)` and let
`𝒫(ℓ, M, Z)` be the set of primes `𝔓` of `E` of degree one over `ℚ`, not above `ℓ`, with `𝔓, 𝔓̄ ∉ Z` and `𝔓 ≠ 𝔓̄`, that
split completely in `E_{ℓ,M}`. Then `𝒫(ℓ, M, Z)` has Dirichlet density `1/[E_{ℓ,M} : E]`, and for every `𝔓 ∈ 𝒫(ℓ, M, Z)`
there is a character `ψ_𝔓 : G_E → μ_{ℓ^∞}` with

- `ψ_𝔓` *anticyclotomic*: trivial on `𝔸_F^×` (through reciprocity), equivalently `ψ_𝔓(c g c^{−1}) = ψ_𝔓(g)^{−1}`;
- `ψ_𝔓` unramified outside `{𝔓, 𝔓̄}`, tame at `𝔓` and `𝔓̄`, and of order exactly `ℓ^M` on the inertia group at `𝔓`;
- `ψ_𝔓` trivial on the decomposition groups of the places above `ℓ`.

For `𝔓_1, …, 𝔓_R ∈ 𝒫(ℓ, M, Z)` with `𝔓_i ∉ {𝔓_{i′}, 𝔓̄_{i′}}` for `i ≠ i′`, and integers `a_i` prime to `ℓ`, `∏_i ψ_{𝔓_i}^{a_i}` has the
same three properties and is ramified exactly at `{𝔓_i, 𝔓̄_i : i ≤ R}`: degree-one places of `E` above places of `F` split in
`E`, stable under `c`, disjoint from `Z`.

(b) *Twisting data.* Let the host be as in the introduction of this layer, with the characters of 2.4(b), and let `𝕃 ⊃ E`
be a number field, Galois over `ℚ` and totally complex, with an automorphism `c_𝕃` of order `2` restricting to `c` on `E`
(for the chain: the field of places `𝕃` and the conjugation `c_𝕃` of 2.6). For a prime `ℓ` the *auxiliary character of `ℓ`*
is `δ_ℓ := δ′` if `ℓ = p_δ` and `δ_ℓ := δ` otherwise; its ramification lies above the *auxiliary prime of `ℓ`* (`p′_δ`,
resp. `p_δ`), which is never `ℓ`; `A(ℓ)` is the set of places of `E` above it. A *twisting datum* consists of:

- a finite set `𝒮_tw` of primes, the *admissible twisting characteristics* (the residue characteristics at which twisting
  is permitted; membership prescribes nothing by itself);
- for every finite place `𝔭` of `𝕃`, of residue characteristic `ℓ`, a character `ψ_𝔭` of `ℓ`-power order, either trivial or a
  product as in (a) for `ℓ`, some `M ≥ 1` and a set `Z` containing the places of `E` above `ℓ` and above the primes dividing
  `30·f_E`, `g₀g₁` and `p_δp′_δ`, and a prescribed finite set `Z₀` of places of `E` containing no place above `p_δp′_δ`, the same for
  all `𝔭` (for the chain: the places above its level primes, 2.8, and above the rational prime of the auxiliary place of its
  grid, all different from `p_δ, p′_δ` and chosen before the twisting datum): anticyclotomic, tame,
  ramified exactly at a `c`-stable finite set `Q(ψ_𝔭)` of degree-one places of `E` above places of `F` split in `E`, and
  trivial on the decomposition groups above `ℓ`; with `ψ_𝔭 = 1` if `ℓ ∉ 𝒮_tw`, and `ψ_{c_𝕃(𝔭)} = ψ_𝔭`;
- a finite set `Tw` of finite places of `𝕃`, the *twisted places*, stable under `c_𝕃`, contained in the set of places above
  `𝒮_tw`, such that `ψ_𝔭 = 1` for `𝔭 ∉ Tw`;
- a sign `s ∈ {±1}` (the chain uses `s = +1`; the statements of the later roadmaps that rest on [FormalProof] are for
  `s = +1`); write `s_j := s` for `1 ≤ j ≤ m` (one sign for all steps).

The *tuple used at `𝔭`* is `ξ_𝔭 := δ_ℓ·ψ_𝔭` for `𝔭 ∈ Tw` above `ℓ` and `ξ_𝔭 := 1` (the *base tuple*) for `𝔭 ∉ Tw`;
`Ξ := {1, δ, δ′} ∪ {δ_ℓψ_𝔭 : 𝔭}`; for `ξ ∈ Ξ` the *twisted tuple* is `μ_j(ξ) := μ_j ξ^{s_j}` (`1 ≤ j ≤ m`); the *twist places*
`Q(ξ)` of a tuple are the places of `E` at which `ξ` is ramified. For every `ξ ∈ Ξ`, `μ_j(ξ)` is unramified outside
`S₀ ∪ Q(ξ)` (`S₀` of 2.4(b)), and `Q(ξ)` contains no place above `g₀g₁` or above a prime dividing `30·f_E` (by (i) below
for `ξ_𝔭`; for `δ`, `δ′` by 2.4(b), `p_δ, p′_δ ≡ 1 (mod 2o_δf_E)` being `> 5` and not in `{g₀, g₁}`), so it is prime to the different
of `F/ℚ` (2.2(g)).

*Lemma.* For every finite place `𝔭` of `𝕃`, of residue characteristic `ℓ`: (i) `ξ_𝔭 ≠ 1` exactly when `𝔭 ∈ Tw`; then `ξ_𝔭` is an
anticyclotomic character of finite order, tame, with `Q(ξ_𝔭) = A(ℓ) ⊔ Q(ψ_𝔭)`, a `c`-stable set of degree-one places of `E`
above places of `F` split in `E`, containing no place above `ℓ`, above `g₀g₁`, above a prime dividing `30·f_E` or in `Z₀`;
so `ξ_𝔭` is unramified at every place above `ℓ`. (ii) `ψ_𝔭` is trivial on the decomposition groups above `ℓ`; `δ_ℓ` is unramified above
`ℓ` and need not be trivial on those decomposition groups. (iii) `ξ_{c_𝕃(𝔭)} = ξ_𝔭`. (iv) Replacing `𝕃` by a larger field of places `𝕃′`
(2.6: a finite Galois extension of `ℚ` inside `ℂ`, stable under complex conjugation, containing `𝕃`) and assigning to a place of `𝕃′` the data of the place of `𝕃` below it gives a twisting datum for `𝕃′`; so
`𝕃` may be enlarged after a twisting datum is fixed.

*Which statements quantify over which set.* Statements about the kernels of the chain hold for every tuple of `Ξ` at
every place where that tuple is unramified, and are applied at `𝔭` to `ξ_𝔭` (legitimate at every `𝔭`, by (i)); they involve
neither `𝒮_tw` nor `Tw`. Sums "over the twisted places" run over `Tw`. A twisting datum says nothing about where the base
tuple has a cost; a statement that needs such a property of `Tw` assumes it.

*For the chain* the datum satisfies in addition:

- (T1) `𝒮_tw` contains `g₀`, `g₁`, `d` and every prime `≤ X″`;
- (T2) `Tw` contains every place above `g₀`, `g₁`, `d` and every place above a prime `≤ X″` inert in `K`.

Route for (a) (class field theory): through `x ↦ x/x̄` and Hilbert 90 the anticyclotomic characters of `G_E^{ab}` are the
characters of `C := U(1)(F)\U(1)(𝔸_F)` trivial on the identity component at infinity; those unramified outside
`{𝔓, 𝔓̄}` and tame there factor through a finite quotient `C_𝔓` of `C`, an extension of the class group of `U(1)` of
level one by the image `B_𝔓` of `𝔽_p^× = U(1)(𝒪_{F_v})/(1 + p)` (`v` below `𝔓`, `p = N𝔓`). The decomposition groups above `ℓ`
are generated by the classes `a_λ` of uniformisers at the places `λ | ℓ`. The intersection `B_𝔓 ∩ ⟨a_λ⟩` is contained in the
group of reductions modulo `𝔓` of the norm-one `ℓ`-units of `E` (which contains `μ_E`), and these are `ℓ^M`-th powers in `𝔽_p^×` because `𝔓`
splits completely in `E_{ℓ,M}`; since `ℓ^M | p − 1`, `B_𝔓/(B_𝔓 ∩ ⟨a_λ⟩)` has a character of order `ℓ^M`, which extends to
`C_𝔓/⟨a_λ⟩` and can be taken of `ℓ`-power order. The density is Chebotarev's theorem for `E_{ℓ,M}/E` with degree-one
primes of density one. (b) consists of definitions; the lemma: (i) `δ_ℓ` is ramified exactly at `A(ℓ)` (2.4(b)) and `ψ_𝔭`
is not (its set `Z` contains the places above `p_δp′_δ`), so `δ_ℓψ_𝔭 ≠ 1`; `Z` also contains the places above `ℓ`, the type
primes, the primes dividing `30·f_E` (`d` among them) and `Z₀`, while the auxiliary primes split completely
in `E` (they are `≡ 1 (mod 2o_δf_E)`, hence prime to `30·f_E`), are different from `ℓ` and from `g₀, g₁` (2.4(b)), and have no place
in `Z₀` (by the choice of `Z₀`); (ii) by (a) and 2.4(b); (iii), (iv) by construction.

*Checks.* (i) The splitting condition cannot be dropped: the reduction of a norm-one `ℓ`-unit `α` of `E` lies in `B_𝔓 ∩ ⟨a_λ⟩`
(the idele of `α` is trivial in `C`); if it is not an `ℓ^M`-th power in `B_𝔓`, every character of `B_𝔓` of exact order
`ℓ^M` is nontrivial on it (the `ℓ`-part of `B_𝔓` is cyclic, so all such characters have the same kernel), and no
anticyclotomic character ramified only at `{𝔓, 𝔓̄}` with inertial order `ℓ^M` is trivial at `ℓ`. (ii) For `ℓ = p_δ` the rule
`δ_ℓ = δ′` is what keeps the tuple unramified above `ℓ`: `δ` itself is ramified at every place above `p_δ`. (iii) At a place
outside `Tw` the tuple is the base tuple, whatever its residue characteristic: a place above a prime `≤ X″` split in `K` need
not be twisted, although its residue characteristic lies in `𝒮_tw`. (iv) With signs depending on the step, the residues of
the step characters at a twist place would differ from block to block of a row stage, and the level of 2.9c (one determinant
character on a parahoric of type `(r, n_j − r)`) would not exist: hence one sign. (v) The condition on `Z₀` cannot be dropped:
if `Z₀` contained a place above `p_δ`, then for `ℓ ≠ p_δ` and `𝔭 ∈ Tw` with `ψ_𝔭 = 1` (allowed) `Q(ξ_𝔭) = A(ℓ)` would meet `Z₀`.

*KNOWN* in kind (class field theory; Chebotarev). The explicit governing field `E_{ℓ,M}` and the density are this roadmap's
formulation, by the route above; what the later roadmaps use is the existence of `ψ_𝔓` for the primes that split completely
in a finite extension of `E` unramified at the auxiliary places, outside a finite set.
*Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1, 2.2, 2.4; ClassFieldTheory Layers 11–13;
GlobalNumberFields (Completed) Layer 9; Chebotarev (Completed) Layer 10.

### 2.6 The two fields of the chain

*The two fields of the chain.* Let `E` be a CM field, abelian over `ℚ`, of degree `2f`, containing `K = ℚ(√−d)` with `d > 3`
a prime, `d ≡ 3 (mod 4)`; `N ≥ 1` and `n_c ≥ 1` integers; `𝒞` an elliptic curve over `ℚ(j(𝒪_K))` with `j(𝒞) = j(ℂ/𝒪_K)` and
good reduction outside `d`. Inside `ℂ` (through `ι₀`) put: `E(N)`, the ray class field of `E` of modulus `N𝒪_E`, and
`h* := [E(N):E]`; `H₁₂ := K(j(𝒪_K))(𝒞[12])`; `V := E(ζ_{h*})(y^{1/h*} : y ∈ E^×, (y)` an `h*`-th power of a fractional ideal`)`;
`Ω := H₁₂ · E(N) · ℚ(ζ_{n_c}) · V`.

(a) *Contents.* (i) Every ideal of `𝒪_E` becomes principal in `E(N)`. (ii) `ζ_n ∈ E(N)` for `n | N`; for odd `n | N`, `n ≥ 3`,
the ray class field `K(n)` of `K` lies in `E(N)`, so every automorphism of `ℂ` fixing `E(N)` fixes `ℂ/𝒪_K` with its level
structure of level `n` up to isomorphism. (iii) The twelfth roots of `Δ(𝒞)` lie in `H₁₂`. (iv) For an algebraic Hecke
character `χ` of `E` with integral infinity type `(n_τ)`, `n_τ ≡ r (mod 2)`, unramified outside `N` and at most tamely
ramified, the values `χ(ϖ_v)·N v^{−r/2}` at the unramified places lie in `V`. (v) For `p, q ≥ 0` and a nonzero ideal `𝔞` of
`𝒪_E` there is `β ∈ V^×` with `β·𝒪_V = ∏_{τ∈Φ} τ(𝔞)^p·τ̄(𝔞)^q·𝒪_V` and `|σ(β)|² = N(𝔞)^{p+q}` at every embedding `σ` of `V`; the same
holds in every finite extension of `V`.

(b) *Size.* Write `sz(F) := (log[F:ℚ], log rd(F))` and `ρ := f − 1`. Then, coordinatewise,
`sz(Ω) ≤ (log d + 20, 3·log d + 20) + (log 2f + log h*, log rd_E + log N + log h*) + (log n_c, log n_c) + (log 2f, log rd_E) + ((ρ + 2)·log h* + log h_E)·(1, 1)`,
with `h* ≤ h_E·N^{2f}` and `log h_E ≤ f·log rd_E + 2f·log(1 + f·log rd_E)`. Every subfield of `Ω` satisfies the same bounds.

*The fields of the chain.* `N := g₀g₁p_δp′_δ`, `n_c := lcm(|G₀|, 3⁴·5⁴)` with `G₀ := ∏_{i=0,1} GL_r(𝔽_{g_i})^f`. The *coefficient
field* `𝕂` is the subfield of `Ω` generated by `E`, `H₁₂`, `E(N)`, the roots of unity of the orders `|G₀|`, `g₀(g₀ − 1)`,
`g₁(g₁ − 1)`, `3⁴`, `5⁴`, `h_E`, the values of (iv) for `μ_j` (`1 ≤ j ≤ m`), `η̃·‖·‖^{1/2}`, `δ`, `δ′`, the values of the characters
of `(ℤ/g_i)^×` of 2.4 (b), and, for a basis `𝔠_1, …, 𝔠_s` of the class group with `𝔠_i^{h_i} = (γ_i)`, the `h_i`-th roots of
the type norms `∏_τ τ(γ_i)^{p_j}τ̄(γ_i)^{q_j}` (`1 ≤ j ≤ m`). It does not depend on the word, and contains no root of unity
attached to a level prime `T(u)`. Under the size conditions `r ≥ 62`, `f ≥ 8`, `L ≥ 4`, `log f ≤ L`, `log g_i ≤ 9L + 1`,
`log p_δ, log p′_δ ≤ L`, `log rd_E ≤ 2L`, for a real number `L` (the chain takes `L = x^ε`, 2.1):

`log[𝕂:ℚ] ≤ 19·f·r²·L + 198·f²·L` and `log rd(𝕂) ≤ 19·f·r²·L + 198·f²·L`.

The *field of places* `𝕃` is a finite Galois extension of `ℚ` inside `ℂ`, stable under complex conjugation, containing `𝕂`
and the finitely many algebraic numbers that later targets name; `σ₀` is the inclusion, `c_𝕃` the restriction of complex
conjugation. No bound on `𝕃` is asserted or used. *Convention.* The Hermitian lattices of the chain and the places `𝔭`, with
the weights `ϱ(𝔭)`, are over `𝒪_𝕃`; cusp forms on the Shimura varieties of the steps, their lattices of integral forms, theta
rules and short theta series are over `𝒪_𝕂` and enter the chain after extension of scalars to `𝒪_𝕃`, which changes neither
the normalised slope nor the maximal slope (SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles §3.5). The level at `T(u)` carries the
trivial character (2.9b): no root of unity of order `T(u)` is needed in `𝕂`.

*Route.* (a) (i) the principal ideal theorem in the Hilbert class field (ClassFieldTheory §13.1,
`isPrincipal_map_hilbertClassField`), which lies in `E(N)` (`isAdmissibleModulus_iff_le_rayClassField`). (ii) the conductor of
`E(ζ_n)/E` divides `n`, so `ζ_n ∈ E(N)` (`isAdmissibleModulus_iff_le_rayClassField`); an automorphism of `ℂ` fixing `K(n)` fixes
`ℂ/𝒪_K` with its level structure of level `n` up to isomorphism by ShimuraVarieties §5.9 (`cm_ideal_reciprocity` at `E = K`,
`m = n`); the generation of `K(n)` by level-`n` values is ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §3.2 and
ImaginaryQuadraticClassNumber H3.1. `K(n)·E ⊂ E(N)` for `n | N`: if `α ≡ 1 mod^× N𝒪_E`, its conjugates over `K` are `≡ 1` as
well, so `N_{E/K}(α) ≡ 1 mod^× n𝒪_K`; the Frobenius of `K(n)·E/E` at a prime `𝔓 ∤ N` restricts on `K(n)` to the Artin symbol of
`N_{E/K}𝔓` (Frobenius in a tower), so the Artin symbols of `K(n)·E/E` are trivial on the ray group of `E` modulo `N𝒪_E` (neither
field has a real place), and `K(n)·E ⊂ E(N)` (`isAdmissibleModulus_iff_le_rayClassField`). (iii) In a model `y² = x³ + Ax + B` of `𝒞`
(`Δ = −16(4A³ + 27B²)`; another model changes `Δ` by a twelfth power in `ℚ(j(𝒪_K))^×`): halving the point `(e_i, 0)` of order `2`
gives `√(e_i − e_k)` in `ℚ(j)(𝒞[4])` for `k ≠ i`, so the fourth root `2∏_{i<k}√(e_i − e_k)` of `Δ` lies there; with `x₁, …, x₄` the
`x`-coordinates of the four pairs `±P` of points of order `3`, `(√−3·(x₁ − x₂)(x₃ − x₄))³ = ±Δ` and `√−3 ∈ ℚ(j)(𝒞[3])` (Weil pairing),
so a cube root of `Δ` lies in `ℚ(j)(𝒞[3])`; their quotient is a twelfth root of `Δ`, and `μ₁₂ ⊂ ℚ(j)(𝒞[12])`.
(iv) for `𝔞` prime to `N`, `𝔞^{h*} = (γ)` with `γ ≡ 1 (mod N)` and `χ(𝔞)^{h*} = ∏_τ τ(γ)^{n_τ}`-type power of `γ`, an element of `E`
generating an `h*`-th power. (v) take `𝔞^{h_E} = (γ)` and `β` an `h_E`-th root of the type norm `∏_τ τ(γ)^pτ̄(γ)^q` of `γ`
(`h_E | h*`); then `(β)^{h_E}` is the `h_E`-th power of the type norm of `𝔞`. For an embedding `σ` of `V`, `ρ := σ|_E` satisfies
`conj∘ρ = ρ∘c` (`E` is CM), so `|ρτ̄(γ)| = |ρτ(γ)|` and `|σ(β)|^{2h_E} = ∏_{τ∈Φ}|ρτ(γ)|^{2(p+q)} = (∏_{g∈G} ρg(γ))^{p+q} = N_{E/ℚ}(γ)^{p+q}`
(`G = Φ ⊔ cΦ`), which is `N(𝔞)^{h_E(p+q)}`; take positive `h_E`-th roots. (b) Logarithms of degrees and of root discriminants are subadditive
under composita. For a Galois extension `M/F` unramified outside an integer `n`, `n·[M:F]` lies in the different
(NumberFieldArithmetic §6.4: `v_P(𝔡) ≤ e_P − 1 + v_P(e_P)`, `e_P` the ramification index of `P`, while `v_P(n) ≥ e_P` and
`e_P | [M:F]` at `P | n`, and `v_P(𝔡) = 0` at the other `P`), hence
`log rd(M) ≤ log rd(F) + log(n·[M:F])`: apply it to `H₁₂/K(j)` (`n = 6d`, degree `≤ |GL₂(ℤ/12)| ≤ 20736`; `K(j)/K` unramified of
degree `≤ 9d`). For the cyclotomic steps `ℚ(ζ_n)/ℚ` and `E(ζ_{h*})/E`, and for a Kummer step `F(y^{1/k})/F` with `ζ_k ∈ F` and `(y)`
a `k`-th power, the relative different divides `n`, `h*`, resp. `k` (locally `y` is a unit `u` times a `k`-th power, and the different
divides the derivative `kη^{k−1}` of `X^k − u` at a root `η`, a unit times `k`; likewise for `X^n − 1`), so such a step raises the
logarithm of the root discriminant by at most `log n`, `log h*`, resp. `log k`; `V` is reached from `E(ζ_{h*})` by `ρ + 1` roots of order
`h*` (units) and roots of orders with product `h_E` (class group). For
`E(N)/E`: every character of `Gal(E(N)/E)` has conductor dividing `N𝒪_E` (`isAdmissibleModulus_iff_le_rayClassField`), so the
conductor–discriminant formula (ClassFieldTheory §13.1, `count_relDiscr_eq_finsum_abelianConductor_exponent`) gives
`N(𝔡_{E(N)/E}) ≤ N^{2f·h*}`, that is `log rd(E(N)) ≤ log rd_E + log N`. `h* = |Cl_{N𝒪_E}(E)|`
(`gal_rayClassField_equiv_rayClassGroup`) `≤ h_E·N(N𝒪_E) = h_E·N^{2f}` by the ray-class exact sequence and the class-number
formula (GlobalNumberFields (Completed) Layer 2; `E` has no real place). The bound for `h_E`: every ideal class contains an
integral ideal of norm at most Minkowski's bound `(4/π)^f·((2f)!/(2f)^{2f})·√|d_E| ≤ rd_E^f` (the constant is below `1`; Mathlib
`NumberField.exists_ideal_in_class_of_norm_le`; `E` has `f` complex places); the number of integral ideals of norm `m` is at most the
number `τ_{2f}(m)` of ordered factorisations of `m` into `2f` factors (the Euler factor of `ζ_E` at `p` is dominated coefficientwise by
`(1 − p^{−s})^{−2f}`), and `Σ_{m≤M} τ_{2f}(m) ≤ M(1 + log M)^{2f−1}` (induction on the number of factors, with `Σ_{a≤M} 1/a ≤ 1 + log M`);
so `log h_E ≤ f·log rd_E + (2f − 1)·log(1 + f·log rd_E)` for every `f ≥ 1`. The
numerical bound: `log h_E ≤ 10fL`, `log N ≤ 20L + 2`, `log h* ≤ 51fL`, `log|G₀| ≤ f·r²·(18L + 2)`, `log d ≤ 4L`.

*KNOWN* ingredients (class field theory; Furtwängler; Weber; the different of a tamely bounded Galois extension), *NEW*

### 2.7 Type primes and auxiliary primes

Let `E` be an abelian CM host of conductor `f_E`, `K = ℚ(√−d) ⊂ E`,
and `M` a multiple of `f_E` with `M ≥ 3`.

(a) *Type primes.* Suppose `d ≥ 100`. There are absolute constants `A_gap, c₁, c₂, X₀ > 0` such that for every `Y ≥ X₀` with
`log(dM²) ≤ c₁√(log Y)` the set `𝒢(Y)` of primes `g ∈ (Y, 2Y]` with `g = N_{K/ℚ}(π)`, `π ∈ 𝒪_K`, `π ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K)`, has at
least `c₂Y/((dM²)^{A_gap} log Y)` elements. Every `g ∈ 𝒢(Y)` satisfies `g ≡ 1 (mod M)` and splits completely in `E`. In
particular, if moreover `(dM²)^{A_gap} log Y·(n + 2) < c₂Y`, there are `g₀ ≠ g₁ ∈ 𝒢(Y)` outside any prescribed set of `n`
primes.
*Disjointness.* For a prime `g` and `N ≥ 1` the *exceptional set* is `𝔅(N, g) := {g} ∪ {ℓ prime : ℓ^{4v_ℓ(g^e − 1)} > g^e for some 1 ≤ e ≤ N}`.
Then `#𝔅(N, g) ≤ 3N + 1`, and for `Y ≥ 2` and a prime `g′ ∈ [Y, 2Y]` the number of primes `g ∈ [Y, 2Y]` with
`𝔅(N, g) ∩ 𝔅(N, g′) ≠ ∅` is at most `(3N + 1)(1 + 2N²(Y^{3/4} + 1))`. Hence, if `𝒢(Y)` has at least `Y^{9/10}` elements and
`20r³ < Y^{3/20}`, there are `g₀ ≠ g₁ ∈ 𝒢(Y)`, outside any prescribed set of `n < Y^{9/10}/2` primes, with
`𝔅(r, g₀) ∩ 𝔅(r, g₁) = ∅`.
The *type primes* of the chain are such `g₀, g₁` for `M = f_E` with `𝔅(r, g₀) ∩ 𝔅(r, g₁) = ∅`,
`g_i > max(2fk + 1, 4fr, 16(r + t)²)` and `g_i ∤ 6d·disc(E)`; for the scale of 2.1, `Y = (kb)⁸` satisfies all hypotheses
under `SizeN` and `Size(x, ε)` for `x ≥ x₀(ε)` (`d ≥ 100` by `SizeD`; `d | f_E`, so `log(d f_E²) ≤ 6x^{ε/5}log²x`,
while `log Y ≥ 8x^ε`), and then
`SizeG`: `(kb)⁸ < g₀, g₁ ≤ b^{C_g}` holds with `C_g = 9` (named here and assumed where cited). For the hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4 with
`n ≥ 2`, `16qd` divides `f_E`, so `16qd | g_i − 1`; and `g_i ≥ (kb)⁸ ≥ 2^{12}`.

(b) *Auxiliary primes.* For an integer `o_δ ≥ 2` (the order of `δ, δ′`, 2.4) there are distinct primes
`p_δ, p′_δ ≡ 1 (mod 2o_δ f_E)`, both `≤ (2o_δ f_E)^{L_2}` with `L_2` absolute; they split completely in `E`, are different
from `d`, and under `SizeN` they are `< X′` (so `< X″`) and smaller than the type primes for `x ≥ x₀(ε)`.

Route: (a) is RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros §2.9b(iv)(c′) at `h = 1` for the modulus `M` (`B = dM²`, the count
`c₂hX/(B^{A_gap} log X)` for `X ≥ 1`): it counts primes `p = N(α)`, `α ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K)`, in `(Y, 2Y]` with the
polynomial bound for a possible exceptional zero. `π ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K)` gives `ππ̄ ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K ∩ ℤ = Mℤ)`; a prime `≡ 1 (mod f_E)`
splits completely in `ℚ(ζ_{f_E}) ⊃ E`. Disjointness: for each `e` the primes `ℓ` with `ℓ^{4v_ℓ(g′^e − 1)} > g′^e` have
`ℓ^{v_ℓ} > g′^{e/4}` and their product divides `g′^e − 1`, so there are at most three; for `ℓ ∈ 𝔅(N, g′)` a prime `g ≠ ℓ` with
`ℓ ∈ 𝔅(N, g)` satisfies `g^e ≡ 1` modulo a power `ℓ^v > Y^{e/4}` for some `e ≤ N`, which leaves at most `2e` classes, each
with fewer than `Y^{1−e/4} + 1 ≤ Y^{3/4} + 1` members in `[Y, 2Y]` (`e` classes for odd `ℓ`); summing `2e` over `e ≤ N` gives
`N(N + 1) ≤ 2N²`, so the excluded `g` number less than `Y^{9/10}/2` when `20r³ < Y^{3/20}`. The count is elementary.
At `Y = (kb)⁸`: `(d f_E²)^{A_gap} log Y ≤ e^{6A_gap·x^{ε/5}log²x}·9x^ε ≤ c₂Y^{1/10}` for `x ≥ x₀(ε)`. (b) is Linnik's theorem in the form of
RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros §1.7b(iii) (for each `r` there are `r` primes `≡ 1 (mod M)` below `M^{L_r}`) at `r = 2` and
`M = 2o_δ f_E`; a prime `≡ 1 (mod f_E)` is prime to `d | f_E`; and `log p_δ ≤ L_2(log 2o_δ + 2x^{ε/5}log²x) < x^{ε/3} < log Y`.

*Checks.* The count of (a) cannot be stated free of the conductor: beside a possible exceptional real zero of a
character of the ray class group modulo `M`, the principal class loses a factor `(1 − β)log Y`; the factor
`(dM²)^{−A_gap}` is what a polynomial lower bound `1 − β ≥ c(dM²)^{−A′_gap}` for the gap pays. Disjointness is a condition:
`𝔅(3, 11) = {2, 5, 7, 11, 19}` and `𝔅(3, 19) = {2, 3, 5, 19, 127}`, with intersection `{2, 5, 19}`.

*KNOWN* (prime ideals of a ray class with conductor uniformity; Linnik; the count of `𝔅` is elementary). The type primes of
the chain, the exceptional sets, their disjointness and `SizeG`: [FormalProof],
`Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Definitions/GlobalTwisting/PairRowIffQdRows.lean` (`GlobalTheory.EX.HostArith`),
`Formalism/CyclotomicNorms/StatementI.lean` (`Formalism.CyclotomicNorms.Exc`), `Formalism/SharpRestriction/ElevenLe.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.SizeG`). *Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.1,
2.1; RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros §1.7b, §2.9b.

### 2.8 The level prime of a word

(a) *Level prime in a short interval.* There are absolute constants
`A_gap, c₁, c₂, X₀ > 0` with: for `K = ℚ(√−d)` with `d` a prime, `d ≡ 3 (mod 4)`, `d ≥ 100`, an integer `M ≥ 3`, `X ≥ X₀` and
`V ≥ 1` with `log(dM²) ≤ c₁√(log X)` and `log V ≤ c₁√(log X)`, the number of primes `T ∈ (X, (1 + 1/V)X]` with
`T = N_{K/ℚ}(π)`, `π ∈ 𝒪_K`, `π ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K)`, is at least `c₂X/(V(dM²)^{A_gap} log X)`; in particular, when this exceeds `n`,
such a prime exists outside any prescribed set of `n` primes.

(b) *Dimension data and the level prime of a word.* For a word `u` (2.3) with highest weights `λ_u(τ) := Λ_0(τ)` and
positive integers `i_col`, `i_row` (the indices of the type levels at `g₀, g₁`, 2.9a), put

`d*_u := mass(𝒪_E^k)·i_col·∏_{τ∈Φ} dim V_{λ_u(τ)}`,  `D* := mass(𝒪_E^r)·i_row·∏_{τ∈Φ} d(𝒟_τ)`,

where `mass(𝒪_E^n)` is the mass of the standard unimodular lattice of rank `n` (HermitianFormsAndLattices §§5.5, 6.3),
`dim V_λ` is given by Weyl's formula, and `d(𝒟_τ) := ∏_{a<b} |h_a − h_b|/(b − a)` for the row parameter
`h(τ) = (h_1 > ⋯ > h_r)` of `Y_0` (2.3), Harish-Chandra's formal degree of the discrete series of `U(Y_0)(F_τ)` with that
parameter for the Haar measure normalised by the compact dual. Put

`T*(u) := (D*/d*_u)^{1/(fk)} · Γ₀^{−2/(fk)}`.

*Size predicate* `SizeT_u` (named here and assumed where cited): for every word `u`, `16·m·x^ε ≤ log T*(u)` and
`log T*(u) + 1 ≤ C_T·m·x^ε ≤ x`, with a constant `C_T` fixed before `ε`.

Let `M := f_E·(g₀g₁)²` and `V := fk`. Under `Size(x, ε)`, `SizeG` (2.7) and `SizeT_u`, for `ε ≤ 1/6` and `x ≥ x₀(ε)`, the
hypotheses of (a) hold at `X = T*(u)` and the count exceeds the number of primes to be avoided: there is a prime
`T(u) ∈ (T*(u), (1 + 1/(fk))T*(u)]` with `T(u) = N(π_u)`, `π_u ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K)`, different from `g₀, g₁, p_δ, p′_δ`; the chain takes
such a prime, the *level prime* of `u`. Then:

- `b^{16m} < T(u) ≤ b^{C_T m}` and `log T(u) ≤ x`; in particular `T(u) > 2fN_m + 1` (so that `T(u)` does not divide `D_Γ`, 3.6);
- `T(u) ≡ 1 (mod f_E(g₀g₁)²)`, so `T(u) ≡ T(u′) (mod (g₀g₁)²)` for all words, and `T(u)` splits completely in `E`;
- the ratio `π_{u_*}/π_u ∈ K` (`u_*` the base word) has norm `T(u_*)/T(u)` and is `≡ 1 (mod g₀g₁)` at the places above
  `g₀g₁`; the vectors `z_u := (π_u/π_{u_*})z ∈ X_m` and `z_{m,u} := (π_u/π_{u_*})z_m ∈ Y_0` (2.2 with `T = T(u_*)`,
  `π_T = π_{u_*}`) have norms `T(u)` and `−T(u)`, and `z_u = π_u e_{N_m}`;
- *rank window:* the numbers `ď_u^{all} := d*_u·(T(u)^k − 1)^f` and `ď_u := ď_u^{all}·((T(u) − 2)/(T(u) − 1))^f` (the ranks of
  the source lattices, 3.8) satisfy `C₀^{−1} ≤ ď_u^{all}Γ₀²/D* ≤ C₀` and `C₀^{−1} ≤ ď_uΓ₀²/D* ≤ C₀` with
  `C₀ = 3`; precisely `1/2 ≤ ď_u^{all}Γ₀²/D* ≤ e` and `3/8 ≤ ď_uΓ₀²/D* ≤ e`.

Route: (a) is RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros §2.9b(iv)(c′) with `h = 1/V` (`e^{−c₁√(log X)} ≤ 1/V` by the hypothesis on `V`). (b):
(a) at `X = T*(u)` (`d ≥ 100` by `SizeD`):
`log T*(u) ≥ 16mx^ε ≥ 16x^{1/2}` (`SizeM`), while `log(dM²) ≤ 3 log f_E + 4 log(g₀g₁) ≤ 73x^ε` (`SizeN`, `SizeG`) and
`log(fk) ≤ C log x` (`Size(x, ε)`), both below `c₁√(log T*(u))` for `ε ≤ 1/6` and `x ≥ x₀(ε)`; the count
`c₂T*(u)/(fk(dM²)^{A_gap} log T*(u))` then exceeds `4`. `T(u) ≤ (1 + 1/(fk))T*(u) ≤ e·T*(u)` gives the upper bounds, and
`b^{16m} ≤ T*(u) < T(u)` the lower one. The congruences follow from `π_u ≡ 1 (mod M𝒪_K)` and `M𝒪_K ∩ ℤ = Mℤ`; `π_{u_*}/π_u ≡ 1`
modulo `g₀g₁` because `π_u ≡ π_{u_*} ≡ 1`. Window: `T(u)^{fk}/T*(u)^{fk} ∈ (1, (1 + 1/(fk))^{fk}] ⊂ (1, e]` and
`ď_u^{all}Γ₀²/D* = (T^k − 1)^f/T*^{fk}`, which lies between `(1 − T^{−k})^f ≥ 1/2` and `e`; the factor
`((T − 2)/(T − 1))^f ≥ 1 − f/(T − 1) ≥ 3/4`. The numbers `ď_u^{all}` and `ď_u` are the expected dimensions of the full and of
the new column space of the word (mass times index times Weyl dimension, counted over the pairs at `T(u)`).

*Checks.* (i) The lower bound on `log T*(u)` is necessary: for `T*(u) < fk` the interval `(T*, (1 + 1/(fk))T*]` has length
`< 1` and may contain no integer. (ii) `d(𝒟_τ)` reduces to Weyl's dimension formula when the group is compact
(`q = 0`): the formal degree of a finite-dimensional representation for the probability Haar measure is its
dimension. (iii) This target only assumes both bounds of `SizeT_u`; at the parameters of the chain they are a consequence of
the identity behind `T*(u)` (the comparison of `d*_u` with `D*`), not of a choice.

*KNOWN* ingredients (prime ideals of the principal ray class in short intervals; mass and formal degree), *NEW*
design. (b), the dimension data, the level prime and the rank window: [FormalProof],
`Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/TheoremThreeOfExplicitAndCompact.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.StandardLattice.VandermondeCountExplicit`),
`Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Definitions/AutomorphicMembership/CmQuadraticFormEqAlternatingForm.lean`
(`GlobalTheory.Mem.Top.duP`),
`Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Definitions/Foundations/CdScaleTieIff.lean` (`GlobalTheory.Mem.Vol.trueDsig`); `SizeT_u`
follows from [FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/JointsL.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.GlobalTheoryTheory.lowB_ge`), `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/StandardLattice.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.StandardLattice.lowB`), `Formalism/SharpRestriction/ParamsHeightRankConstant.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.TuSize`).
