### 2.9 The level structure, the unramified places and the rule for the characters

*Definitions (the level structure; the unramified places; the rule for the characters).*
Fix a host as in the introduction of this layer, ranks, weights and words (2.1–2.3c), type primes `g₀, g₁` (2.7), level
primes `T(u)` (2.8) and a twisting datum (2.5). For a finite place `v` of `F` write `L_{j,v}`, `L^Y_{j,v}`, `L^V_{j,v}`, `L^W_{j,v}`
for the completions of the standard lattices of `X_j`, `Y_j`, `V_j`, `W_j`, and `L^h_v` for that of `X^h`. For a tuple `ξ ∈ Ξ`,
`S(ξ)` is the set of places of `F` above `g₀g₁` or below `Q(ξ)`. The *level structure* of the chain consists of compact open
subgroups, with characters, of the unitary groups of `X_j` (`0 ≤ j ≤ m`), `X^h`, `Y_j` (`0 ≤ j ≤ m`), `V_j` and `W_j`
(`1 ≤ j ≤ m`) at every finite place, and of the finite test vectors of the row steps at the places of `S(ξ)`; it is defined
at the unramified places here, at the type primes in 2.9a, at the level primes in 2.9b and at the twist places in 2.9c.

- *Unramified places.* At `v ∉ S(ξ)` not above `T(u)`: the stabilisers of the standard lattices (hyperspecial), with trivial
  characters; the test vectors of the row steps are the lattice vectors.
- *The characters at the bad split places (one rule).* Every `v ∈ S(ξ)` is split in `E` (2.4(b), 2.5); a place `w_v | v` of `E`
  is fixed (at the type primes the place above `π_g𝒪_K` of 2.4(b); at a twist place, 2.9c), through which every unitary
  group of the chain at `v` is a general linear group over `E_{w_v} = F_v` and the Weil representations act in the model on
  matrices of 2.2a (a). The *residues* are `c_{i,v} := μ_i(ξ)|_{𝒪_{E_{w_v}}^×}` (`1 ≤ i ≤ m`): `c_{i,v} = χ_{v,i}^{−1}` at the type
  primes (2.4(b); the twisting characters are unramified there), `c_{i,v} = (ξ_{w_v}|_{𝒪^×})^{s}` at the twist places (`s` the sign
  of 2.5, `+1` for the chain; `μ_i` is unramified there). The levels of 2.9a and 2.9c are such that the forms of the row
  stage `j` (on `U(Y_j)`)
  transform on the `i`-th added block of size `t` by `c_{i,v}∘det` (`1 ≤ i ≤ j`), the forms on `U(V_j)` on their block of size `t`
  by `c_{j,v}∘det`, and the test vector of the row step `j` carries `c_{j,v}∘det^{−1}` on the block on which it is supported.

*API.* The levels of different words differ only at the places above the level primes, and `T(u) ≡ T(u′) ≡ 1 (mod
(g₀g₁)²)`; the levels of different tuples differ only at the places below `Q(ξ)`, and not on `U(Y_0)` (2.9c); the residues
`c_{i,v}` at the type primes do not depend on the tuple. Outside `S(ξ)` the levels of `Y_j` are hyperspecial, and outside `S(ξ)`
and the places above `T(u)` those of `X_j` and `X^h` are. The class sets of `U(X_j)` for these levels are finite and the
stabilisers of their classes are finite (HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2).

*Check (the rule is that of the row normal form).* In the conjugate datum of 2.2a(b), the representation in which the
row steps are used (Layer 3), a pair `(g, h)` of units acts on functions on matrices by `c(det g)c(det h)^{−1}·φ(h^{−1}xg)`
when the two splitting characters have the same residue `c` (`η̃` is unramified, 2.4(b)). For the type-prime vector
`Φ(x) = χ(x′)c(det x′)^{−1}·1[x′ ∈ K, x″ ≡ 0]` of 2.9a (`x′` the upper square block, `x″` the lower `t` rows, `(K, χ)` the level of
the source stage): `ω(g, 1)Φ = χ(g)Φ` for `g ∈ K`, so the theta integral against `Φ` has the source level character; and for
`h = (A B; 0 D)` block upper triangular modulo the prime with `A ∈ K`, `ω(1, h)Φ = χ(A)^{−1}c(det D)^{−1}Φ`, so the forms of the
target stage that pair with it transform by `χ(A)c(det D)`: the new block carries `c`. For the twist-place vector
`Φ^Q(x) = c(det x_{rr})^{−1}·1[…]` of 2.9c and `g` in the parahoric of type `(r, n − r)`, `ω(g, 1)Φ^Q = c(det of the lower block of
g)·Φ^Q`: the factor on the `GL_r`-block cancels, and both stages carry `c(det of the lower block)` with the exponent `+1`. With
the law `c(det g)^{−1}c(det h)` of 2.2a(a) instead, `ω(g, 1)Φ = χ(g)c(det g)^{−2}Φ`: the levels, the vectors and the descent
against the conjugate kernel belong together. The vector at which the descent evaluates the conjugate datum is `Φ` itself:
the kernel is `θ̄(·; Φ) = conj θ(·; conj Φ)` (2.2a(c)); the kernel `conj θ(·; Φ)` would be that of the conjugate datum at
`conj Φ`, which `ω̄(g, 1)` multiplies by `conj χ(g)·c(det g)²` instead of `χ(g)`.

*NEW* (definitions). *Needs:* 2.2–2.8, 2.2a; HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2.

### 2.9a Levels at the type primes

*Definitions and lemma (the type primes).* At `v | g ∈ {g₀, g₁}` (split in `E`; through the
place `w_v` of 2.4(b), `U(V)(F_v) ≅ GL_n(ℚ_g)` for `V` of rank `n`), with a nontrivial additive character `ψ_g` of `𝔽_g`, the
type characters `χ_{v,j} = ω_g^{2j}` of 2.4(b) (of depth zero: trivial on `1 + g𝒪_{E_{w_v}}`) and the residues `c_{j,v} = χ_{v,j}^{−1}`
of 2.9:

- *tame condition:* read as elements of `𝔽_g^× ⊂ 𝔽̄_g^×` through the tame labelled form of
  FiniteReductiveGroups (Conventions) fixed by a topological generator of tame inertia at `w_v` (the
  choice for which the roots of a label, FiniteReductiveGroups §5.7, are the eigenvalues of the tame generator on the Langlands
  parameter), every `χ_{v,j}` is a square, the `2m + 1` elements `1, χ_{v,j}, χ_{v,j}^{−1}` (`1 ≤ j ≤ m`) are distinct, and their
  prime-to-`ℓ` parts are distinct for every prime `ℓ ∉ 𝔅(r, g)` (2.7); it holds by the lemma below;
- *the finite group:* `K(g)_v` is the principal congruence subgroup of level `g` of `Stab(L^Y_{0,v})`, and
  `G₀ := ∏_{v | g₀g₁} Stab(L^Y_{0,v})/K(g)_v ≅ ∏_{v | g₀g₁} GL_r(𝔽_{g_v})`. A *level* at the type primes is a pair `(P, χ_P)` of a
  subgroup `P ⊂ G₀` and a character `χ_P` of `P`; the *level-`(P, χ_P)` part* of a `G₀`-module `M` is
  `M^{(P,χ_P)} := {x ∈ M : p·x = χ_P(p)x for all p ∈ P}`, the image of the idempotent `e_{P,χ_P} := |P|^{−1}Σ_{p∈P} χ_P(p)^{−1}p`;
- *the rows and the model spaces of rank `r`:* the forms on `U(Y_0)` and on `U(W_j)` are taken at the *principal level*
  `K(g)` at the type primes (on `U(W_j)` through the isometries `W_j ≅ Y_0` at the places above `g₀g₁`, 2.2(c), fixed once and
  carrying the standard lattice to the standard lattice), as `G₀`-modules; levels `(P, χ_P)` only cut out parts. The row type
  level below is the level at which the row forms are paired and counted; the base level enters only dimension counts of
  label parts;
- the *base level* `(P_0, χ_{P_0})`: with the ordered basis `b_v := (e_1, …, e_{r_S}, z_m, β_1, …, β_k, f_{r_S}, …, f_1)` of
  `L^Y_{0,v}` read at `w_v` (`β_1, …, β_k` an orthonormal basis of `L_{0,v}`), `P_0` is the product over `v` of the upper
  unitriangular groups in the basis `b_v` and `χ_{P_0}(n) := ∏_v ψ_g(Σ_{i=1}^{r−1} n_{i,i+1})` (the sum of the `r − 1` superdiagonal
  entries): the Gelfand–Graev pair, so every irreducible representation of `G₀` contains `χ_{P_0}` at most once on `P_0`;
- the *row type level* `(P_1, χ_{P_1}) := (∏_v K₂(v)/K(g)_v, χ₂)`, where `K₂(v) ⊂ U(Y_0)(F_v)` consists of the elements of
  `Stab(L^Y_{0,v})` whose reduction modulo `g` lies in `N·U_{X_0}`: `N` the unipotent radical of the parabolic subgroup
  of `U(Y_0)` stabilising the isotropic flag `⟨e_1⟩ ⊂ ⋯ ⊂ ⟨e_1, …, e_{r_S}⟩` (it acts trivially on
  `⟨e_1, …, e_{r_S}⟩^⊥/⟨e_1, …, e_{r_S}⟩ ∋ z_m, β_i`), and `U_{X_0}` the upper unipotent group of `U(X_0) ≅ GL_k` for the
  basis `β` (it fixes `z_m`); that is, the elements unipotent modulo `g` for the flag `(e, z_m, β)` of the Bessel
  subgroup `N ⋊ U(X_0)`. `χ₂ = χ_N·χ_L`: in the ordered basis `b′_v := (e_1, …, e_{r_S}, β_1, …, β_k, z_m, f_{r_S}, …, f_1)` read
  at `w_v`, `χ_N` is the character `n ↦ ψ_g(Σ_{a<r_S}(n_{e_a,e_{a+1}} + n_{f_{a+1},f_a}) + n_{e_{r_S},z_m} + n_{z_m,f_{r_S}})` of the
  Bessel unipotent part (the `2r_S` entries of the chain `e_1 → ⋯ → e_{r_S} → z_m → f_{r_S} → ⋯ → f_1`, that is, the pairings
  `⟨f_a, n e_{a+1}⟩`, `⟨f_{r_S}, n z_m⟩` read on both factors of `E_v = E_{w_v} × E_{w̄_v}`), and `χ_L` the generic character
  `ψ_g(Σ_{i=1}^{k−1} n_{β_i,β_{i+1}})` (the `k − 1` superdiagonal entries) of the upper unipotent group on the block of `X_0` (for `r_S = 0`,
  that is `m = 2`, the flag is empty, `N = 1` and `χ_N` is trivial); `K₂(v) ⊃ K(g)_v`; replacing `z_m` by `z_{m,u}` does not change `K₂` and `χ₂`, because
  `π_{u_*}/π_u ≡ 1 (mod g)` (2.8). In the basis `b′_v`, `(P_1(v), χ_{P_1})` at `v` is the pair `(H_{r_S}, ϕ_{r_S})` of
  FiniteReductiveGroups §2.7(b) for `GL_r`, with block `β` of size `k` in the middle of the chain. The adelic group is `K₂ := ∏_{v|g₀g₁} K₂(v) × ∏_{v∤g₀g₁} Stab(L^Y_{0,v})`;
- the *row stage groups* at `v | g`, for `0 ≤ j ≤ m` (`Y_m = X^h`), read at `w_v` in the following coordinates (a convention):
  a basis `(β_1, …, β_k; 𝔞_1, …, 𝔞_{m−1}; j blocks of size t)` of the standard lattice of `Y_j` in which, for `j ≤ m − 1`, `β_1, …, β_k`,
  the last `j` vectors `𝔞_{m−j}, …, 𝔞_{m−1}` and the blocks form a basis of `L_{j,w_v}` and the first `m − 1 − j` vectors of `𝔞` a basis of
  the lattice of the complement of `X_j` (2.2(b); empty for `j = m − 1`), and which for `j = m` is a basis of `L^h_{w_v}`; the first `r`
  vectors are ordered
  `𝔞_1, …, 𝔞_{r_S}, β_1, …, β_k, 𝔞_{r_S+1}, …, 𝔞_{m−1}`; for `j = 0` this is `b′_v` with `𝔞 = (e_1, …, e_{r_S}, z_m, f_{r_S}, …, f_1)`, and
  `P_1(v) := K₂(v)/K(g)_v ⊂ GL_r(𝔽_g)` is read on the first `r` vectors in this order (unitriangular with the `k` entries (row `β_i`,
  column `𝔞_{r_S+1}`) zero, with the character `ψ_g` of the sum of the entries `(β_i, β_{i+1})` and `(𝔞_i, 𝔞_{i+1})`). The *flag group* `K^{fl}_j(v)` is
  the group of the elements of `GL_{n_j}(ℤ_g)` that are block upper triangular modulo `g` for the blocks `(r, t, …, t)`; the
  *principal group* `K^{pr}_j(v) ⊂ K^{fl}_j(v)` is cut out by "the first block is `≡ 1 (mod g)`"; so
  `K^{fl}_j(v)/K^{pr}_j(v) ≅ GL_r(𝔽_g)`, and `χ^{fl}_j(M) := ∏_{i≤j} c_{i,v}(det of the i-th block of size t) = ∏_{i≤j} χ_{v,i}^{−1}(det of the i-th block)`
  is a character of `K^{fl}_j(v)` (the rule of 2.9). For a level `(H, χ_H)`, `H ⊂ G₀`, the *row stage level* `K^{(H)}_j` is the preimage of `H` in `∏_v K^{fl}_j(v)`
  with the character `χ_H·χ^{fl}_j`; for a *product level* `(H, χ_H) = ∏_v (H_v, χ_{H_v})` it is the product of the local groups
  `K^{(H_v)}_j(v)` (the preimage of `H_v` in `K^{fl}_j(v)`, with `χ_{H_v}·χ^{fl}_j`), among them `K^{pr}_j(v)` (`H_v = 1`) and the *row type
  level* `K^{rt}_j(v) := K^{(P_1(v))}_j(v)` with `χ_{P_1}·χ^{fl}_j`. Forms of principal level are those of level `(∏_v K^{pr}_j(v), χ^{fl}_j)`; `G₀` acts on them
  by `g₀·f := χ^{fl}_j(κ)^{−1}R(κ)f`, `κ ∈ ∏_v K^{fl}_j(v)` any element above `g₀`, and the forms of level `(H, χ_H)` are the range
  of `e_{(H,χ_H)}`. For a tuple `ξ`, `K^{pr}_j(ξ)` and `K^{rt}_j(ξ)` denote the finite adelic level groups of stage `j` of
  principal level and of row type level `(H, χ_H) = (P_1, χ_{P_1})` (with the levels of the twist places of `ξ`, 2.9c, and the
  hyperspecial ones elsewhere), so `vol(K^{rt}_j(ξ)) = |P_1|·vol(K^{pr}_j(ξ))`;
- *the test vectors of the row steps:* for `1 ≤ j ≤ m` and a level `(H, χ_H)`, one vector at all the type places together, in the
  model on `∏_{v|g₀g₁} Hom(Y_{j−1,w_v}, Y_{j,w_v}) ≅ ∏_v M_{n_j×n_{j−1}}(ℚ_g)` of 2.2a(a), in coordinates adapted to the standard lattices
  and to the row stage groups (vectors of the conjugate datum, 2.2a(c)):
  `Φ^{(H)}_j((x_v)_v) := (χ_H·χ^{fl}_{j−1})((x′_v)_v)·∏_v c_{j,v}(det x′_v)^{−1}` if the upper square blocks `x′_v` of the `x_v` form an element
  of `K^{(H)}_{j−1}` and every lower `t × n_{j−1}` block is `≡ 0 (mod g)`, and `0` otherwise. For a product level it is the product of the
  local vectors `Φ^{(H_v)}_{j,v}` given by the same formula at each `v`: the *principal-level vector* `Φ^{pr}_{j,v} := Φ^{(1)}_{j,v}`
  (`x′ ∈ K^{pr}_{j−1}(v)`) and the *row type vector* `Φ^{rt}_{j,v} := Φ^{(P_1(v))}_{j,v}`, with `Φ^{pr}_j = ⊗_vΦ^{pr}_{j,v}` and
  `Φ^{rt}_j = ⊗_vΦ^{rt}_{j,v}`. (For a level that is not a product, for instance a diagonal subgroup of two factors above the same `g`,
  the vector does not factor over the places.)
- *the model spaces* `U(V_j)` (rank `r + t`): the subgroup of the parahoric of type `(r, t)` whose upper left `r × r` block is
  `≡ 1 (mod g)`, with the character `c_{j,v}(det of the t-block) = χ_{v,j}^{−1}(det of the t-block)`; `G₀` acts through the upper left
  block, and only the centre of its group algebra is used on `U(V_j)` and `U(X^h)`;
- *the fine levels* (the groups under which the finite data of the chain at the type primes are equivariant): for
  `0 ≤ j ≤ m − 1`, `K^{fn}_j(v) := {κ ∈ Stab(L_{j,v}) : κ ⊕ 1 ∈ K^{rt}_j(v)}` (`κ ⊕ 1` the extension by the identity on the lattice of
  the complement of `X_j` in `Y_j`), with the character `χ^{fn}_j`, the restriction of `χ_{P_1}·χ^{fl}_j`; and `K^{fn}_m(v)` the group of
  the `κ = (A b; c d) ∈ Stab(L_{m,v})` with `b ≡ 0`, `c ≡ 0`, `d ≡ 1 (mod g)` and `A ∈ K^{rt}_m(v)`, with the character of `A`, in a basis
  of `L_{m,v} = L^h_v ⊕ 𝒪_{E,v}e_{N_m}` made of the basis of `L^h_v = L^Y_{m,v}` above and `e_{N_m}`. So `K^{fn}_0(v) = K_col(v)` (below)
  with `χ_L`, and `K^{fn}_{m−1}(v) = K^{rt}_{m−1}(v)`;
- *the column levels:* `K^X_0(v) := K_col(v) := U(X_0)(F_v) ∩ K₂(v)`, the pro-`g` Iwahori subgroup of `GL_k(ℤ_g)` for the flag `β`,
  with the character `χ_L`; for `1 ≤ j ≤ m − 1`, `K^X_j(v)` := the principal congruence subgroup of level `g` of `Stab(L_{j,v})`, with
  the trivial character; `K^X_m(v)` := the group of the `κ = (A b; c d) ∈ Stab(L_{m,v})` (basis as above) with `b ≡ 0`, `c ≡ 0`,
  `d ≡ 1 (mod g)` and `A ∈ K^{pr}_m(v)`, with the character `κ ↦ χ^{fl}_m(A)`; the forms on `U(X^h)` are taken at the principal level
  of stage `m`, `(K^{pr}_m(v), χ^{fl}_m)`;
- the indices of 2.8: `i_col := ∏_{v | g₀g₁} [GL_k(ℤ_{g_v}) : K_col(v)]` and `i_row := ∏_{v | g₀g₁} [Stab(L^Y_{0,v}) : K₂(v)]`.

*Lemma (type levels).* (a) `P_1 ⊂ P_0` with `[P_0 : P_1] = ∏_v g_v^k`; hence `[Stab(L^Y_{0,v}) : K₂(v)] = g^k ∏_{i=1}^r (g^i − 1)`,
`[GL_k(ℤ_g) : K_col(v)] = ∏_{i=1}^k (g^i − 1)`, and
`log(i_row/i_col) = f·(m(2k + m − 1)/2)·log(g₀g₁) + f·Σ_{g∈{g₀,g₁}} Σ_{k<i≤r} log(1 − g^{−i})`.
(b) `log|G₀| ≤ f r²·log(g₀g₁)`.
(c) If `g − 1 > 4m·g^{1/4}`, the type characters `χ_{v,j} = ω_g^{2j}` satisfy the tame condition; this holds for the type
primes of 2.7 (`g > 16(r + t)²`).
(d) `K^X_m(v)` is a group (the preimage of the block diagonal image of the reduction of `K^{pr}_m(v)`), `χ^{fl}_m(A)` is a character
of it, `U(X^h)(F_v) ∩ K^X_m(v) = K^{pr}_m(v)` (through `κ_h ↦ diag(κ_h, 1)`) and the character restricts to `χ^{fl}_m`; the same holds
for `K^{fn}_m(v)` with `K^{rt}_m(v)`. For every `0 ≤ j ≤ m`, `K^X_j(v) ⊂ K^{fn}_j(v)` and `χ^{fn}_j` restricts to the character of
`K^X_j(v)`; for `1 ≤ j ≤ m − 1` the principal congruence subgroup is the intersection of the stabilisers in `K^{fn}_j(v)` of the
cosets of `g^{−1}L_{j,w_v}` modulo `L_{j,w_v}`. `K_col(v)` and `K^X_j(v)` (`1 ≤ j ≤ m − 1`) are sufficiently small in the sense of
HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2(d)(ii) when `g > 2fN_m + 1`, which holds for the type primes of 2.7; that criterion does not apply
to the fine levels `K^{fn}_j(v)`, `j ≥ 1` (their reductions contain `GL_t(𝔽_g)^j`).

Route. (a) At `w_v` the isotropic flag and its orthogonal complements give the flag of `b_v`, with graded
pieces of dimensions `1^{r_S}, k + 1, 1^{r_S}`; `N` is the unipotent radical of the corresponding parabolic subgroup of
`GL_r` (dimension `r(r−1)/2 − k(k+1)/2`), and `U_{X_0}` adds the unitriangular `k × k` block of `β` (dimension
`k(k−1)/2`) and fixes `z_m`. So `P_1(v)` is the group of unitriangular matrices in `b_v` whose `k` entries in the row of
`z_m` and the columns of `β_1, …, β_k` vanish, of index `g^k` in `P_0(v)`. Then
`|GL_r(𝔽_g)|/|P_1(v)| = g^k·∏_{i ≤ r}(g^i − 1)`; the ratio with `∏_{i ≤ k}(g^i − 1)` is
`g^{k + (m−1)(2k+m)/2}∏_{k<i≤r}(1 − g^{−i})`, and `k + (m − 1)(2k + m)/2 = m(2k + m − 1)/2`; there are `f` places above each
`g`. (b) `|GL_r(𝔽_g)| ≤ g^{r²}`. (c) The quotient of two of the `2m + 1` elements is `ω_g^{2a}` with `1 ≤ |a| ≤ 2m`, of order
`(g − 1)/gcd(g − 1, 2a) ≥ (g − 1)/(4m) > 1`; two of them have the same prime-to-`ℓ` part exactly when that order is a power of
`ℓ`, hence at most `ℓ^{v_ℓ(g−1)}`, which is `≤ g^{1/4}` for `ℓ ∉ 𝔅(r, g)` (take `e = 1` in the definition of `𝔅`). (d) `K^X_m(v)` is the
preimage under reduction of the group of the `(Ā 0; 0 1)` with `Ā` in the reduction of `K^{pr}_m(v)`; `χ^{fl}_m` depends only on the
diagonal blocks of `A` modulo `g`, which multiply; `diag(κ_h, 1) ∈ K^X_m(v)` iff `κ_h ∈ K^{pr}_m(v)`; likewise for `K^{fn}_m(v)`.
Containments: `K^{pr}_m(v) ⊂ K^{rt}_m(v)`, and `χ_{P_1} = 1` on a first block `≡ 1`; an element `≡ 1 (mod g)` of `Stab(L_{j,v})` extends by
the identity to an element `≡ 1 (mod g)` of the stabiliser of the standard lattice of `Y_j`, which lies in `K^{pr}_j(v) ⊂ K^{rt}_j(v)`, and
`χ^{fl}_j = 1` on it (the `c_{i,v}` have depth zero); `K₂(v)` restricted to `X_0` is `K_col(v)`, with `χ_N = 1` on `κ ⊕ 1`; and `κ ∈ GL(L)`
fixes every coset of `g^{−1}L/L` iff `κ ≡ 1 (mod g)`. The sufficient smallness is
HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2(d)(ii): the elements are unipotent modulo the prime above `g` on a lattice of rank at most `N_m`
over `𝒪_E`, and `g > N_m[E:ℚ] + 1`; for the type primes of 2.7, `g > 16(r + t)² > 2fN_m + 1` because `t ≥ 7fr` (2.1).

*Remark.* The statements of Layer 3 hold, with the same proofs, for any compact open subgroups of the fine levels with the
restricted characters, provided that at `j = m` the intersection with `U(X^h)` is the level of the forms on `U(X^h)` used in the
period, and that at `j = m − 1` the group fixes the cosets of `g^{−1}L_{m−1,w_v}` modulo `L_{m−1,w_v}` (the line variable of the last two
steps of the chain). They are not asserted for groups not contained in the fine levels: at `j = m` an element `κ = (1 b; 0 1)` with
`b ≢ 0 (mod g)` replaces the `X^h`-rows `x_h` of `x ∈ Hom(X_{m−1,w_v}, X_{m,w_v})` by `x_h − b·ℓ` (`ℓ` the last row), which can move a
datum supported on matrices whose first square block lies in a unipotent pattern modulo `g` off its support.

*Check.* If `r_S ≥ 1` and `χ_N` were defined with the first `r_S` entries of the chain only (one factor of `E_v`), cuspidal
representations of `G₀` would not occur in the level `(P_1, χ_{P_1})` part. At `j = 0` the reduction of `K₂(v)` has index `g^k` in the
Gelfand–Graev group `P_0(v)`; for `r_S ≥ 1`, `χ₂` (which involves the entry linking `z_m` to `f_{r_S}`) is not the restriction of
`χ_{P_0}`, while for `r_S = 0` (`m = 2`) `N = 1` and `χ₂ = χ_L = χ_{P_0}|_{P_1}`.

*NEW* (definitions; the lemma is elementary). The levels and the test vectors follow from [FormalProof],
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Col.LocE.JGsym`).
*Needs:* 2.1–2.9; HermitianFormsAndLattices §§2.3, 5.2(d)(ii);
FiniteReductiveGroups Conventions (tame labelled form), §5.7 (labels), §2.2 (Gelfand–Graev multiplicity
one), §2.7 (b) (Bessel-type pairs).

### 2.9b Levels at the level primes

*Definitions (the level primes).* At `v | T(u)` (split in `E`; `v = w w̄` with `w` above `π_u`;
`U(X_j)(F_v) ≅ GL_{N_j}(E_w)`; residue field `𝔽_v := 𝒪_w/ϖ_w ≅ 𝔽_{T(u)}`):

- a *pair* at level `j` is `𝔡 = (Λ, fn)`, `Λ ⊂ X_{j,w} = E_w^{N_j}` an `𝒪_w`-lattice and `fn : Λ → 𝔽_v` a nonzero `𝒪_w`-linear functional.
  Its *class lattice* `L_𝔡 ⊂ X_{j,v} = X_{j,w} ⊕ X_{j,w̄}` is `ker(fn)` at `w` and `Λ^*` at `w̄` (`Λ^*` the dual of
  `Λ` for the pairing of `X_{j,w}` with `X_{j,w̄}` given by the Hermitian form); its dual is `L_𝔡^∨ = Λ ⊕ ker(fn)^*`, so
  `L_𝔡 ⊂ L_𝔡^∨`, `T(u)L_𝔡^∨ ⊂ L_𝔡`, `L_𝔡^∨/L_𝔡 ≅ 𝔽_v²`, and `fn` is a nonzero functional on `L_𝔡^∨/L_𝔡` vanishing on `ker(fn)^*/Λ^*`;
  the self-dual lattice of `𝔡` is `Λ ⊕ Λ^* = L_𝔡 + π̄_u L_𝔡^∨`. The *diamond operators* `⟨a⟩_v`, `a ∈ 𝔽_v^×`, replace `fn` by `a·fn`;
  a *map of pairs* `x : 𝔡 → 𝔡′` (levels `j − 1`, `j`) is an `x ∈ Hom(X_{j−1,w}, X_{j,w})` with `x(Λ) ⊂ Λ′` and `fn′∘x = fn` on `Λ`;
  `Maps(𝔡, 𝔡′)` is their set;
- the *standard pair* at level `j` is `𝔡₀^{(j)} := (L_{j,w}, fn₀^{(j)})`, `fn₀^{(j)}` the coordinate of `e_{N_j}` modulo `ϖ_w` for `j < m` and
  `fn₀^{(m)} : y ↦ ⟨z_u, y⟩` modulo `w` (`z_u = π_u e_{N_m}`, 2.8; at `w` this is a unit multiple of the coordinate of `e_{N_m}`); the
  *column level* of `X_j` at `v` is `Stab(𝔡₀^{(j)})`, the stabiliser of `L_{j,w}` and of `fn₀^{(j)}`, with the trivial character (replacing
  the functional by a nonzero multiple `a·fn₀^{(j)}`, `a ∈ 𝔽_v^×`, keeps the stabiliser and changes the pairs of the classes by the
  diamond operator `⟨a⟩_v`; another nonzero functional `fn′` gives the stabiliser `γ·Stab(𝔡₀^{(j)})·γ^{−1}`, `γ ∈ Stab(L_{j,w})` with
  `fn′ = fn₀^{(j)}∘γ^{−1}`, which in general differs: for `N_j ≥ 2`, `1 + E_{1,N_j}` fixes the coordinate of `e_{N_j}` and not that of `e_1`);
- globally a class of level `j` at `T(u)` has a lattice `L_𝒸` with `L_𝒸 ⊂ L_𝒸^∨`, `T(u)L_𝒸^∨ ⊂ L_𝒸`, `[L_𝒸^∨ : L_𝒸] = T(u)^{2f}`;
- the *glued lattice* of a pair at level `0` is `L^{gl}_𝔡 := ker(fn) ⊕ 0 + 𝒪_w·(y + ϖ^{−1}z_{m,u})` in `X_{0,v} ⊕ ⟨z_{m,u}⟩` for any
  `y ∈ Λ` with `fn(y) = 1`: unimodular, with projection `Λ` to `X_{0,v}` and intersection `ker fn` with `X_{0,v}`. Let
  `x_u ∈ U(Y_0)(𝔸_f)` be trivial outside the places above `T(u)` and move `L^Y_{0,v}` to the glued lattice of the standard pair
  `𝔡₀^{(0)}` at each `v | T(u)` (it exists: both lattices are unimodular in a split local space,
  HermitianFormsAndLattices §2.3). The *column level of the word `u`* is `K_u := U(X_0)(𝔸_f) ∩ x_u K₂ x_u^{−1}` (`K₂` of 2.9a), with the
  character induced by `χ₂`; at `v | T(u)` it is `Stab(𝔡₀^{(0)})`, and it does not depend on the choices of `x_u` and of the functional
  up to conjugation by `U(X_0)(𝔸_f)`.

The rows, the model spaces and `X^h` have hyperspecial levels at `v | T(u)`.

*Check.* At `v | T(u)` the line `⟨z_{m,u}⟩` cannot be split off a unimodular lattice of `X_{0,v} ⊕ ⟨z_{m,u}⟩`:
`⟨z_{m,u}, z_{m,u}⟩` has valuation `1` at `v`, so a unimodular lattice containing `z_{m,u}` as an orthogonal summand does not
exist; the glued lattice is the replacement, and the extra datum it carries is exactly `fn`. For a pair, the class lattice
`L_𝔡 = ker(fn) ⊕ Λ^*` and the self-dual lattice `Λ ⊕ Λ^*` differ at `w` only, by the index `T(u)`, and the dual `L_𝔡^∨` contains
the self-dual lattice with the same index at `w̄`.

*NEW* (definitions). The pairs, the class lattices, the standard pair and the glued lattice: [FormalProof],
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/StdLatUnimodular.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Col.Deco.Ok`),
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/StdLatUnimodular.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Col.Deco.act`),
`Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/GL3Theta.lean` (`Formalism.MainNotions.GL3Theta.LiftObj.IsMem`).
*Needs:* 2.2, 2.8, 2.9, 2.9a; HermitianFormsAndLattices §§2.3, 4.2.

### 2.9c Levels at the twist places

*Definitions (the twist places).* At `v` below `Q(ξ)` (degree one, split in `E`, residue field
`𝔽_p`; this includes the places above the auxiliary prime of a twisted tuple, 2.5; `Q(ξ)` is `c`-stable, and `w_v` is one of the
two places of `Q(ξ)` above `v`, fixed for each conjugate pair, which identifies the local groups with general linear groups),
with `c_v := (ξ_{w_v}|_{𝒪^×})^{s}`, the common value of the residues `c_{i,v}` of 2.9 (one sign, 2.5):

- *rows:* for the row stage `Y_j`, the parahoric subgroup of `GL_{n_j}(ℤ_p)` stabilising the standard lattice and the lattice
  scaled by `ϖ` on the last `n_j − r` coordinates (type `(r, n_j − r)`), with the character `c_v(det of the lower block)`;
  `idx_v(j)` is the index of this row level in `GL_{n_j}(ℤ_p)` (a Gaussian binomial coefficient in `p`). At `j = 0` the parahoric
  has type `(r, 0)` and is the hyperspecial stabiliser, so the rows of stage `0` and their level are the same for every tuple;
- *the test vectors of the row steps:* for `1 ≤ j ≤ m`, in the model on `Hom(Y_{j−1,w_v}, Y_{j,w_v}) ≅ M_{n_j×n_{j−1}}(ℚ_p)` in
  coordinates adapted to the standard lattices, `Φ^Q_{j,v}(x) := c_v(det x_{rr})^{−1}` if `x` is integral, its upper left `r × r` block
  `x_{rr}` lies in `GL_r(ℤ_p)` and the entries below the first `r` rows in the first `r` columns are `≡ 0 (mod p)`, and `0` otherwise;
- *the model spaces* `U(V_j)`: the parahoric of type `(r, t)` with the character `c_v(det of the lower block)`; `U(W_j)`: the
  hyperspecial stabiliser;
- *the column levels:* for `X_0`, the hyperspecial stabiliser; for `X_j`, `1 ≤ j ≤ m − 1`, the principal congruence subgroup of
  level `p^{2m}` of `Stab(L_{j,v})` with the trivial character; for `X_m`, the group `K^X_m(v)` of the `κ = (A b; c d) ∈ Stab(L_{m,v})`
  (coordinates as in 2.9a) with `b ≡ 0`, `c ≡ 0`, `d ≡ 1 (mod p^{4m+1})` and `A` in the row level of stage `m` above (the product of
  the image of that row level under `A ↦ diag(A, 1)` and the principal congruence subgroup of level `p^{4m+1}`), with the
  character `κ ↦ c_v(det of the lower block of A)`; the forms on `U(X^h)` are taken at the row level of stage `m`, so that
  `U(X^h)(F_v) ∩ K^X_m(v)` is that level with its character (as in 2.9a(d)).

*Check.* `c_v∘det` is trivial on the principal congruence subgroups because `ξ` is tame (2.5). The finite data of the chain
at a twist place are constructed in the sub-roadmap SeesawForThetaChains (its section on charts), a later roadmap of the family, and are indexed by its *charts*
(finitely many double cosets at each twist place, each with its *chart level*, a compact open subgroup with a character); by that
target the chart levels of stage `j ≤ m − 2` contain the principal congruence subgroup of level `p^{j+1}` with trivial character, and
the cosets of `p^{−m}L_{m−1,w_v}` modulo `p^mL_{m−1,w_v}` (the line variable of the last two steps) are fixed by level `p^{2m}`; so the
column levels above are contained in all of them. Level `p` is not asserted: by the same target, for a chart whose representative
has, in its normal form, the first denominator `p^{−a}` and the shear vector `z ∈ L_{j,w_v}`, every element `κ` of the chart level
satisfies `(κ − 1)z ∈ p^aL_{j,w_v}`, so level `p` fails for a chart with `z ∉ p^{a−1}L_{j,w_v}`.

*NEW* (definitions). The levels and the test vector follow from [FormalProof],
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Col.LocE.JQ`),
`Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Proofs/SatakeParameters/CongruentOneMemStepLevelsCharacterOneStatement.lean`
(`GlobalTheory.T.congruent_one_mem_step_levels_character_one`).
*Needs:* 2.5, 2.9, 2.9a.

### Examples

- *A construction* (2.1): for `ε < ε₀ = 1/52`, `|A| ∈ [x^{2+8ε}, 2x^{2+8ε}]` (so `A_n = 2 + 8ε` and `A_max = 28/13`), `f = 2|A|`, `m ≍ x^{1/2−ε/16}`, `k ≍ x^{9/2+10ε}` and `t ≤ x^{400}` with `C_t = 401`, every parameter predicate holds for large `x`; the smallest margins are a factor `x^{ε/100}` in the windows of `SizeM` and `SizeK` and `x^{0.30ε}` in the first bound of `SizeW`.
- *A host that fails* (2.2, 2.4): for `E = ℚ(√−3, √−7)` and `K = ℚ(√−7)` (`SplitRamification 7` fails) `𝔇_{E/K}` is the prime above `3`, its own conjugate, so no ideal `𝔟` as in 2.2 (f) exists; the existence condition of the splitting character `η̃` fails at `x = √−7`, and both conditions of `HostSplitting E` fail (`f = 2`, and the prime of `F` above `3` is inert in `E`).
- *A descent at a compact step* (2.2a, Check (ii)): for totally positive definite `S`, `T` of ranks `2`, `3` and exponents `m_S = −20`, `m_T = −21`, `a = b = −9`; the form on `U(T)` with Harish-Chandra parameter `(5, −3, −10)` pairs only with the conjugate kernel, and its descent has Harish-Chandra parameter `(9/2, −7/2)`.
- *The unit condition* (2.3a): for `μ_E = {±1}` the condition is that `Σ_τ b₁(τ)` be even, and `s₁ ∈ {0, 1}` corrects its parity.
- *Exceptional sets of type primes* (2.7): `𝔅(3, 11) = {2, 5, 7, 11, 19}` and `𝔅(3, 19) = {2, 3, 5, 19, 127}` meet in `{2, 5, 19}`.

### Dependencies

FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §§0.1, 0.3, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8a, 0.9; HermitianFormsAndLattices §§2.1, 2.3, 3.1, 3.2, 4.2, 5.2, 5.5, 6.3; ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §§0.1, 2.5; ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §§3.46a, 4.4a; MetaplecticAutomorphicForms §3.3; FiniteReductiveGroups (Conventions; §§2.2, 2.7, 5.7); RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros §§1.7b, 2.9b; AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §2.4; DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §8.3; ClassicalGroups §6.1 and Layer 5; BaseChangeAndGaloisRepresentationsForUnitaryGroups §6.3a; SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles §3.5; ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §§3.2, 4.3; ClassFieldTheory Layers 11–13; GlobalNumberFields (Completed) Layers 2, 8–10; Chebotarev (Completed) Layer 10; NumberFieldArithmetic (Completed); Mathlib `NumberField.exists_ideal_in_class_of_norm_le`; ShimuraVarieties §5.9; ImaginaryQuadraticClassNumber H3.1; EllipticCurves Layer 4.

## Layer 3: The column chain on definite unitary groups

Forms of the weights of 2.3 on the definite groups `U(X_j)`, written as functions of frames; a family of theta
functions of CM abelian varieties attached to pairs of self-dual lattices; the step operators, defined as class sums of
jets of these theta functions, and identified with class-set theta lifts for a definite pair in the homomorphism model;
the chain lift, its level structure at `T(u)`, its integral structures and its period against forms on `U(X^h)`.
Throughout, `𝕎_j := Hom_E(X_{j−1}, X_j)`, `𝕃` is the coefficient field of 2.6 read in `ℂ` through `σ₀`, Hermitian
products are conjugate-linear in the first variable, and the host satisfies the hypotheses of Layer 2. Classes of the
definite groups are written `𝒸, 𝒸′`, with representatives `t_𝒸`, stabilisers `Γ_𝒸` and class lattices `L_𝒸`.

*The normal form of the pairs of the chain (columns and rows).* Every pair of spaces of the chain is a homomorphism pair
in the normal form of 2.2a, with the skew element `δ_T` of 2.2(f), the standard additive character and
`(g, h)·x = h∘x∘g^{−1}`: the *column step* `j` is the pair `(X_{j−1}, X_j)` with the splitting characters `(μ_j(ξ), μ_j(ξ))` (3.2);
the *row step* `j` is the pair `(Y_{j−1}, Y_j)` with the splitting character `μ_j(ξ)η̃^{−1}` attached to the member of odd rank
and `μ_j(ξ)` to the other (by 2.1, `Y_{j−1}` has odd rank exactly when `j` is odd); the *model step* `j` is the pair `(V_j, W_j)`
with `(μ_j(ξ), μ_j(ξ)η̃^{−1})` (`μ_j(ξ)` of 2.5, `η̃` of 2.4(b)). Column lifts and model lifts integrate the form on the source
group against the theta kernel; the *row descent* along step `j` integrates the form on `U(Y_j)` against the theta kernel `θ̄` of
the conjugate datum of 2.2a(b) (the pair `(Y_{j−1}, Y_j)` with the skew element `−δ_T` and the splitting characters
`μ_j(ξ)^{−1}η̃`, `μ_j(ξ)^{−1}`) at the finite test vectors `Φ` of 2.9–2.9c, that is, against the complex conjugate of the theta
kernel of the row step at the complex conjugate vectors `conj Φ` (2.2a(c)). In this normal form the row parameters of 2.3 (with the shift `−½` at the odd stages) and the levels of
2.9–2.9c are those of the forms that the descents relate (2.2a(d), 2.3 Check (iv), 2.9 Check), and the parts of row spaces are
labelled by the Hecke eigensystems of the forms themselves. Every other pair formed from the spaces of the chain is taken
in the same normal form, with a skew element in `ℚ_{>0}·δ_T`.

*The second condition of `HostSplitting E`.* From 3.1d on, this layer assumes in addition that every place of `F` above a
prime dividing `f_E` splits in `E` (the second condition of `HostSplitting E`, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3; equivalently, no prime of `E` above a prime
dividing `f_E` is fixed by complex conjugation; by 2.2(g) only the places above `d` need checking). It is used in 3.1d (e) (the two places `w ≠ c(w)` of `E` above `d` that `τ` and `cσ_dτ` see), in 3.2 (the lattice
vector at the places above `d`) and in 3.3 (iv) (the Hecke relation there). It holds for the hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4, which have layer
`n ≥ 2 = v₂(d + 1) − 1` (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.4(vi)), in particular for those with a `B`-admissible auxiliary pair (FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.9(vi)).

### 3.1 Chain forms and their integral structures

*Definitions and lemma.*

*Polynomial modules.* For a dominant weight `λ` of `GL_N` with last entry `λ_N`, let `𝒫_λ` be the space of polynomials
with rational coefficients in the entries of an `N × N` matrix variable `X` that have column degrees
`(λ_1 − λ_N, …, λ_{N−1} − λ_N, 0)` and are invariant under adding a multiple of an earlier column to a later one; `GL_N`
acts by `(g·P)(X) := det(g)^{λ_N}P(ᵗg X)`, with highest weight `λ` and highest-weight vector the product of leading minors
`v_λ := ∏_{i<N} Δ_i(X)^{λ_i − λ_{i+1}}` (`Δ_i` the leading principal `i × i` minor). `⟨·,·⟩_λ` is the `U(N)`-invariant Hermitian
form on `𝒫_λ ⊗ ℂ` with `⟨v_λ, v_λ⟩_λ = 1` (the Hermitian form of the `ℚ`-rational contravariant form). The *dual Weyl lattice*
`∇_ℤ(λ) := 𝒫_λ ∩ ℤ[X]`, and the *Weyl lattice* `Δ_ℤ(λ)` is its dual for `⟨·,·⟩_λ`.

*Lemma (integral structures).* `𝒫_λ ⊗ ℂ ≅ V_λ`; `Δ_ℤ(λ) ⊂ ∇_ℤ(λ)` are `GL_N(ℤ)`-stable lattices, `Δ_ℤ(λ) ⊗ R` and `∇_ℤ(λ) ⊗ R`
are `GL_N(R)`-stable for every ring `R`, and they are the Weyl module and the induced (dual Weyl) module of
ReductiveGroups 10B over `ℤ` in the polynomial model, `⟨·,·⟩_λ` restricting to its top-normalised contravariant pairing.

Route: the polynomial model is the induced module of 10B (functions on `GL_N` with the right equivariance under the upper
triangular group, transported between the two Borel subgroups by transposition); the contravariant pairing of 10B and
`⟨·,·⟩_λ` are invariant and agree on `v_λ`, hence everywhere (irreducibility over `ℚ`); `D_τΔ ≅ ∇` and the perfect integral
pairing are 10B.

*Forms.* For a level `j`, a word `u` and a tuple `ξ`, let `Cl_j = Cl_j(u, ξ)` be the class set of `U(X_j)` for the column
levels of 2.9–2.9c (`K_u` at level `0`, 2.9b; `K^X_j(v)` at the type primes, 2.9a; `Stab(𝔡₀^{(j)})` at the places above `T(u)`, 2.9b;
the levels of 2.9c at the twist places), with representatives `t_𝒸` and finite stabilisers `Γ_𝒸`. The space `ℳ_j = ℳ_j(u, ξ)` of
*chain forms of level `j`* consists of the functions
`f : U(X_j)(𝔸_f) × ∏_{τ∈Φ} GL_{N_j}(ℂ) → ℂ` (*frames* `y = (y_τ)` at the places of `Φ`) with `f(γtκ, γ_∞y) = χ(κ)f(t, y)` for
`γ ∈ U(X_j)(F)` (`γ_∞ = (τ(γ))_τ`) and `κ` in the level with its character `χ`, such that `y ↦ f(t, y)` lies in
`W_{Λ_j} := {y ↦ ∏_τ det(y_τ)^{Λ_{j,N_j}(τ)}·P(y) : P ∈ ⊗_τ 𝒫_{Λ_j(τ)} ⊗ ℂ}` for every `t`. Evaluation at the representatives
identifies `ℳ_j` with the twisted `Γ_𝒸`-invariants of `⊕_𝒸 W_{Λ_j}` (`Γ_𝒸` acting by left translation of frames). Under left
translation `(γ·f)(y) = f(γ^{−1}y)`, `W_{Λ_j} ≅ V_{Λ_j}^∨` through `Φ`: in the model of AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §7.4 the
coefficient module of `ℳ_j` is `V_{Λ_j}^∨`, and the automorphic functions `g ↦ f(g_f, g_∞y)` have `K_∞`-type `V_{Λ_j}`. The
*Petersson product* is `⟨φ, φ′⟩ := Σ_𝒸 |Γ_𝒸|^{−1}⟨φ(𝒸), φ′(𝒸)⟩_{Λ_j}`, `⟨·,·⟩_{Λ_j} := ⊗_τ⟨·,·⟩_{Λ_j(τ)}` on the polynomial
parts; at an embedding `σ` of `𝕃` the metric of an `𝕃`-rational form is the same expression for its image under `σ`, with the
Hermitian form taken for the conjugation `c_σ := σ^{−1}∘(complex conjugation)∘σ` of `𝕃` (it restricts to `c` on `E`, `E`
being CM, and differs from `c_𝕃` in general).

*Class lattices.* The lattice of a class `𝒸` of level `j` is `L_𝒸 := t_𝒸·L_j` away from the level primes (self-dual), and at
`v | T(u)` the class lattice of the pair of the class (2.9b): `L_𝒸 ⊂ L_𝒸^∨`, `T(u)L_𝒸^∨ ⊂ L_𝒸`, `[L_𝒸^∨ : L_𝒸] = T(u)^{2f}`; its
*self-dual lattice* is `L^{sd}_𝒸 := L_𝒸 + π̄_uL_𝒸^∨` (equal to `L_𝒸` away from `T(u)`); at the places of `E` above `π_u` one has
`L_𝒸^∨ = L^{sd}_𝒸`, at those above `π̄_u` one has `L_𝒸 = L^{sd}_𝒸`.

*Integral structures.* For a finite place `𝔭` of `𝕃` above `p` and a class `𝒸`, let `M_𝒸 ∈ GL_{N_j}(E ⊗ ℚ_p)` be a matrix whose
columns form a basis of `L_𝒸^∨ ⊗ ℤ_(p)` over `𝒪_E ⊗ ℤ_(p)`. An `𝕃`-rational form `φ` (values with
coefficients in `𝕃`) is *integral at `𝔭`* if, for every class, `φ(𝒸)((τ(M_𝒸)y_τ)_τ) = ∏_τ det(y_τ)^{Λ_{j,N_j}(τ)}·P(y)` with
`P ∈ ⊗_τ Δ_ℤ(Λ_j(τ) − Λ_{j,N_j}(τ)) ⊗ 𝒪_{𝕃,(𝔭)}`, the determinant factor being taken in the same coordinates. `ℳ_j^{int}` is the
set of forms integral at every `𝔭`. Above `π_u` this is the condition "values in the Weyl lattice of the unimodular `L^{sd}_𝒸`",
above `π̄_u` the condition for the larger lattice `L_𝒸^∨` (a smaller lattice of values). `ℳ_j^{∨}` is the set of forms satisfying
the same condition with `∇_ℤ` in place of `Δ_ℤ` at every `𝔭` not above `T(u)` (an `𝒪_𝕃[1/T(u)]`-lattice; at those places it is
the dual of `ℳ_j^{int}` for the class-set pairing up to the stabiliser orders, DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5a); the
dual of `ℳ_j^{int}` at the places above `T(u)` is not used. For forms on `U(X^h)` (3.9, 3.10) the same definitions apply
with the class lattices `L_𝒸`, which are self-dual at every place, the weight `σ` of 2.3 and the levels of 2.9a (the principal
level of stage `m`, on which `G₀` acts) and 2.9c (the row level of stage `m`), hyperspecial elsewhere; for `X^h`, `ℳ^∨` is defined
by the `∇`-condition at every `𝔭`.

*API.* `ℳ_j` is finite-dimensional, `ℳ_j(𝕃)` is an `𝕃`-structure, `ℳ_j^{int}` is an `𝒪_𝕃`-lattice of full rank in it and
`ℳ_j^{int} ⊗ 𝒪_𝕃[1/T(u)] ⊂ ℳ_j^{∨}`, and neither depends on the choice of `M_𝒸` (a change of basis is in `GL_{N_j}(𝒪_E ⊗ ℤ_(p))`, which preserves `Δ_ℤ ⊗ 𝒪` and `∇_ℤ ⊗ 𝒪`
and changes the determinant factor by a unit) nor on the representatives (an isometry carries `L_𝒸` to `γL_𝒸`). At every
`𝔭` not above `T(u)`, for `φ ∈ ℳ_j^{int}` and `φ′ ∈ ℳ_j^{∨}`, `⟨φ(𝒸), φ′(𝒸)⟩_{Λ_j} ∈ 𝒪_{𝕃,(𝔭)}` for every class (the Gram
matrix of a basis of the self-dual `L_𝒸` is a unit at `p`, and `Δ_ℤ`, `∇_ℤ` are dual), so the class terms of the Petersson
product have denominators dividing `|Γ_𝒸|`. The Petersson product is conjugate-linear in `φ`, so on rational
structures it is `c_𝕃`-semilinear in the first variable.

*Checks.* (i) Weight `0` and trivial characters: `ℳ_j` is the space of functions on `Cl_j` and `ℳ_j^{int}` the
`𝒪_𝕃`-valued ones. (ii) One class with `Γ_𝒸 = {±1}` and a weight on which `−1` acts by `−1`: `ℳ_j = 0`; a model that forgot
the invariants would give `W_{Λ_j}`. (iii) `N = 1`, `λ = (a)`: `W = {y ↦ y^a}` and `(γ·f)(y) = γ^{−a}y^a`, the dual of the
character `γ ↦ γ^a` of highest weight `a`: the coefficient module in Gross's model is the contragredient. (iv) `GL_2`,
`λ = (2, 0)`: `∇_ℤ(λ) = ℤx² + ℤxy + ℤy²` (`x, y` the entries of the first column), `⟨x², x²⟩ = 1`, `⟨xy, xy⟩ = 1/2`, so
`Δ_ℤ(λ) = ℤx² + 2ℤxy + ℤy² ⊊ ∇_ℤ(λ)`; two `∇_ℤ`-valued forms need not pair integrally, a `Δ_ℤ`-valued and a `∇_ℤ`-valued form do.

*KNOWN* (Gross 1999; Weyl modules). The chain forms and their integral structures: [FormalProof],
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/HermitianForms/TiedStep.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Forms.IntSpan`).
*Needs:* 2.3, 2.9, 2.9a, 2.9b, 2.9c; AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups §§7.4, 7.5 and DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5a;
HermitianFormsAndLattices §§5.1, 5.2, 5.5; ReductiveGroups 10B (integral Weyl and induced modules, contravariant form);
ArithmeticLocallySymmetricSpaces ALS.F.7 (coefficient lattices of integral dual Weyl modules).

### 3.1a Theta data, integral coordinates and axis ideals

*Definitions and lemma (theta data, integral coordinates, axis ideals).*

- *Theta data.* `Λ_K := δ_K·ι₀(𝒪_K) ⊂ ℂ` with `δ_K^{12} = Δ(ι₀(𝒪_K))`, so `Δ(Λ_K) = 1` (`Δ = g₂³ − 27g₃²`); `𝔞₀ = 𝔟^{−1}` is the
  ideal of 2.2(f). For self-dual `𝒪_E`-lattices `L ⊂ X_{j−1}`, `L′ ⊂ X_j` put
  `V_ℂ(j) := ℂ ⊗_{K, ι₀} 𝕎_j = ⊕_{τ ∈ Φ} M_{N_j × N_{j−1}}(ℂ)` (the complex structure of `Φ`),
  `𝔙(L, L′) := Λ_K ⊗_{𝒪_K} 𝔞₀ Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)`, `H_A(z, z′) := Σ_τ tr(z_τ z′_τ^*)/area(Λ_K)`, `Q_A(x) := (√d/2)·H_A(x, x)` and
  `α(x) := (−1)^{Q_A(x)}` on `𝔙(L, L′)`; `A(L, L′) := V_ℂ(j)/𝔙(L, L′)`. `Th(L, L′)` is the line of entire
  `Θ` with `Θ(z + x) = α(x)e^{πH_A(z, x) + (π/2)H_A(x, x)}Θ(z)` for `x ∈ 𝔙(L, L′)`; for `y ∈ V_ℂ(j)` the *Fock translation* is
  `(U_yΘ)(z) := Θ(z + y)e^{−πH_A(z, y) − (π/2)H_A(y, y)}` (the same definitions with `Λ_K` in place of `𝔙(L, L′)` on `ℂ`). A rational
  point `y ∈ 𝕎_j(F) = Hom_E(X_{j−1}, X_j)` gives the point `δ_K y := δ_K ⊗ y = (δ_K·τ(y))_{τ∈Φ}` of `V_ℂ(j)`; this map carries
  `𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)` onto the period lattice `𝔙(L, L′)`, and a Fock translation by a rational point always means `U_{δ_K y}`.
- *Integral coordinates.* `A(L, L′) ≅ (ℂ/Λ_K) ⊗_{𝒪_K} 𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)` (Serre's tensor construction), and `ℂ/Λ_K` is the
  `σ₀`-realisation of the model of `𝒞` of 2.6 rescaled by `Δ(𝒞)^{1/12}`, which has discriminant `1`; so `A(L, L′)` is defined
  over `𝕃`. Its `τ`-factor is the block `M_{N_j × N_{j−1}}(ℂ)` of `V_ℂ(j)`, and the matrix coordinates `z_τ` (for local
  `𝒪_E`-bases of `L, L′` at a prime `p`) are dual to the invariant differentials `dz ⊗ (basis)`. For a number field `M ⊂ ℂ`
  containing every `τ(E)`, the *axis ideal* of `τ ∈ Φ` is `𝔩_τ := {y ∈ M : y·e_τ ∈ 𝒪_M·Φ(𝔞₀)}`, where `e_τ` is the `τ`-th unit
  vector of `M^Φ` and `Φ(𝔞₀) := {(τ′(a))_{τ′∈Φ} : a ∈ 𝔞₀}`. `c_τ ∈ 𝕃^×` is a generator of `𝔩_τ𝒪_𝕃`, fixed once (principal by
  the lemma and 2.6 (a) (i)). So `c_τ·∂/∂z_{τ,a,b}` is a derivative along the `𝒪_𝕃`-span of `𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)` (acting through all `τ`); for
  `y ∈ 𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)` the *derivative along `y`* is `∂_y := Σ_{τ,a,b} τ(y)_{a,b}·∂/∂z_{τ,a,b}` (the direction `(τ(y))_τ` in the
  coordinates in which the period lattice is `Λ_K·Φ(𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′))`). `w := z/√area(Λ_K)` are the coordinates in which
  `H_A` is the standard form (the *Fock coordinates*).

*Lemma.* Let `d` be prime, `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)` (on the hosts of the chain `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)`, Layer 2), and both ranks
`N_{j−1}`, `N_j` even (2.1). Then `Im H_A` is integral and unimodular
on `𝔙(L, L′)`, `Q_A` is integer valued and `α` is a semicharacter for `Im H_A`; `A(L, L′)` is a principally polarised abelian
variety, isomorphic to a product of elliptic curves with complex multiplication by `𝒪_K`, on which `E` acts through `Φ`. The
axis ideal is `𝔩_τ = τ(𝔞₀𝔇_{E/K})𝒪_M = τ(𝔞̄₀)^{−1}𝒪_M`, which is the unit ideal at the primes not dividing `N_{E/ℚ}(𝔞₀)` and differs from
`τ(𝔞₀)𝒪_M` at each prime dividing it (there `𝔇_{E/K}` is not a unit).

Route. `Im H_A` is integral and unimodular on `𝔙(L, L′)` because `𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)` is self-dual for `Tr_{E/ℚ}(δ_T·h)`
(`h(x, y) = tr(x^†y)`): its dual is `δ_T^{−1}𝔞̄₀^{−1}𝔇_{E/ℚ}^{−1}·Hom(L, L′) = 𝔞₀Hom(L, L′)` by 2.2(f); `Q_A` is integral because
`d ≡ 3 (mod 4)` and the ranks are even; the polarisation is principal, and an `𝒪_E`-module decomposition gives the product of
CM elliptic curves (AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A5.3–A5.4 for the line). The integral coordinates:
AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A1.3a, A4.7a (the Serre tensor and its Lie realization), specialised in
ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §1.2 (Serre's construction), and the good model of `𝒞` (2.6). The axis ideal: for a
fractional ideal `𝔞` of `E` the dual of `𝒪_M·Φ(𝔞)` for `Σ_τ x_τy_τ` is `𝒪_M·Φ(𝔞^{−1}𝔇_{E/K}^{−1})` (the trace dual of `𝔞` for
`Tr_{E/K}`, base-changed to the flat `𝒪_M`), so `y·e_τ ∈ 𝒪_M·Φ(𝔞)` iff `y·τ(b) ∈ 𝒪_M` for all `b ∈ 𝔞^{−1}𝔇_{E/K}^{−1}`, iff
`y ∈ τ(𝔞𝔇_{E/K})𝒪_M`; and `𝔞₀𝔞̄₀𝔇_{E/K} = 𝒪_E` (2.2(f)).

*Checks.* (i) Rank one: `E = K`, `X_{j−1} = X_j = K`, `L = L′ = 𝒪_K`, `𝔞₀ = 𝒪_K` (`𝔇_{K/K} = 𝒪_K`): `𝔙 = Λ_K`,
`Q_A(δ_K x) = N_{K/ℚ}(x)`, `Im H_A(δ_K·1, δ_K·ω) = ±1` for `ω = (1 + √−d)/2`, so `Im H_A` is unimodular, and `α(x) = (−1)^{N(x)}`
satisfies `α(1 + ω)α(1)^{−1}α(ω)^{−1} = (−1)^{((9+d) − 4 − (1+d))/4} = −1 = (−1)^{Im H_A(1, ω)}`; the trivial function is not a
semicharacter. (ii) In the coordinates `z` the lattice `𝔙(L, L′)` is the period lattice of the algebraic model (`Δ(Λ_K) = 1`);
jets in the Fock coordinates `w` are the derivatives in `z` times powers of `area(Λ_K)^{1/2} = |δ_K|·area(ι₀𝒪_K)^{1/2}`, and the
archimedean size of the kernels at the conjugate embeddings is governed by the areas of the conjugate lattices, whose mean
is the `log Ω` of the Conventions. (iii) The axis ideal in relative degree `2`: locally `𝒪_E = 𝒪_K[ϑ]`, `𝔞 = a𝒪_E`,
`Φ = {τ₁, τ₂}`; `y·e_{τ₁} = u·Φ(a) + u′·Φ(aϑ)` forces `u = −u′τ₂(ϑ)` and `y = u′·τ₁(a)(τ₁(ϑ) − τ₂(ϑ))`, so the axis ideal is
`τ₁(a·g′(ϑ)) = τ₁(𝔞𝔇_{E/K})` (`g` the minimal polynomial of `ϑ`); a generator of `τ(𝔞₀)𝒪_𝕃` in place of `c_τ` would not give a
lattice direction at a prime where `𝔇_{E/K}` is not a unit.

*KNOWN* in kind (Shimura; Serre's construction). The theta data, the Fock translation, the axis ideals and `c_τ`:
[FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/GeneralLinearActions/IsDiscriminantTwelfthRootOfIdealLattice.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.TaylorLogMetric.Hf`), `Formalism/LocalToGlobal/KernelLattices/ThDistC.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Ker.IsDgen`).
*Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3, 2.1, 2.2, 2.6; AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A1.3a, A4.7a, A5.3–A5.4;
ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §1.2.

### 3.1b The character `χ_D`

*The character `χ_D`.* Let `I¹` be the group of fractional ideals `𝔞` of `E` with `𝔞𝔞̄ = 𝒪_E`. After
enlarging `𝕃` (2.6) there is a homomorphism `χ_D : I¹ → 𝕃^×` with `χ_D((z)) = ∏_{τ∈Φ} τ(z)^{−1}` for every `z ∈ E^×` with
`zz̄ = 1`, and `|σ(χ_D(𝔞))| = 1` at every embedding `σ`; it is fixed once. For `x ∈ 𝔸_{E,f}^×` write `χ_D(x/x̄)` for its value at
the ideal of `x/x̄`, and `𝔡(L, L₁) ∈ I¹` for the determinant ideal of a self-dual lattice `L₁` relative to `L` (the ideal of
`det g` for any `g` in the unitary group of the adeles with `gL = L₁`).

Route: on `P¹ := {(z) : zz̄ = 1}` the formula is well defined, because a unit `u` with `uū = 1` is a root of unity and
`∏_τ τ(u) = N_{E/K}(u) = 1` (2.3(g)); `I¹/P¹` is finite (it maps to the class group with kernel embedded in
`𝒪_F^{×}/N(𝒪_E^×)`); a homomorphism from `P¹` extends to `I¹` with values in `ℚ̄^×` (divisibility), with values whose conjugates
all have absolute value `1` (roots of such values).

*KNOWN* in kind (divisibility of `ℚ̄^×`). *Needs:* 2.3, 2.6.

### 3.1c The reduced theta function of `ℂ/Λ_K` and its integrality

*The reduced theta function of `ℂ/Λ_K` and its integrality.* Let `d` be a prime with
`d ≡ 7 (mod 8)` (on the hosts of the chain `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)`, Layer 2), `K = ℚ(√−d)` and `Λ_K` as in 3.1a.
Let `θ(z) := σ(z)e^{−s₂z²/2}` be the *reduced theta function* of `ℂ/Λ_K`
(`σ` the Weierstrass function, `s₂ = s₂(Λ_K)` the constant for which `|θ|²e^{−π|z|²/area(Λ_K)}` is periodic), and `θ₀` its Fock
translate by the point of order two outside the kernels of the two primes of `K` above `2`, divided by its value at `0` (the
even theta function with semicharacter `(−1)^{N(·)}`). For a prime `p` let `𝒪` be the integers of `ℂ_p` (through an embedding
of `ℚ̄`), `W` a Weierstrass model of `ℂ/Λ_K` over `𝒪` with unit discriminant and coefficients `a₁, b₂, …`, `x = ℘ − b₂/12`, `t` its
formal parameter, and `Ê(𝔪)` the points with non-integral `x`. Then:
(a) `s₂ + b₂/12 ∈ 𝒪` at every prime `p ≠ d` other than `3`, and at `p = 3` when `3` splits in `K`; at `p = d`, `√−d·s₂ ∈ 𝒪`,
so `v_d(s₂) ≥ −1/2`; and `s₂ = φ″(0)` for `φ := −log(θ/t)` and the
parameter `t = −2℘/℘′` of the model of discriminant `1`;
(b) for `p ≠ d` (for `p = 3` when `3` splits in `K`) and a torsion point `y ∉ Ê(𝔪)` of order `N`, `(log U_yθ)″ = −(x(z ⊕ y) + s₂ + b₂/12)` is a series with integral
coefficients, `(log U_yθ)′(0) ∈ a₁/2 + N^{−1}𝒪`, and `(U_yθ)(0)` is a unit; at `p = d` the same holds for
`(log U_yθ)″ + s₂ = −(x(z ⊕ y) + b₂/12)`, the Gaussian `e^{−s₂z²/2}` removed (`(log U_yθ)″` itself is not integral at `d`: Check);
(c) for odd `p ≠ d` (for `p = 3` when `3` splits in `K`), every product of Fock translates of `θ₀` by torsion points of order
prime to `p` has integral derivatives at `0`;
at `p = d` the same holds after the factor `e^{−(s₂/2)Σ_w z_w²}` is split off; at `p = 2` (which splits in `K`), with a model
whose `a₁` is a unit, the same holds with no factor.

Route: `φ″ = x(t) + (s₂ + b₂/12) + D²log t` (`D = d/dz`), a series with coefficients in `𝒪` up to the constant `s₂ + b₂/12`
(equivalently, the theorem of the cube for `σ`, AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A1.5); so all derivatives of `φ` of order `≥ 3`
at `0` are integral, at every `p`. For a
complex multiplication `α`, comparing quasi-periods gives `α(α − ᾱ)(s₂ + b₂/12) = Σ_{0≠P∈ker α}x(P) + (α² − 1)b₂/12`; when `α` and
`α − ᾱ` are units at the place considered, the kernel points lie outside `Ê(𝔪)`, and the last term is integral (`α² ≡ 1` modulo
`3`, resp. `8`, at a split `3`, resp. `2`), so `s₂ + b₂/12 ∈ 𝒪` at every `p ≠ d` with `p ≠ 3` or `3` split in `K` (with
`α = m + (1 + √−d)/2` or its conjugate) and `√−d·s₂ ∈ 𝒪` at `d` (short model, `α = (1 + √−d)/2`; for the short model `t = z + O(z⁵)`).
(b): `(log U_yθ)″ = −(℘(z + y) + s₂)` with `℘ = x + b₂/12`, as displayed (at `d`, `s₂` moved to the left); the first derivative is
the value at `y` of the periodic function `F(z) := ζ(z) − s₂z − (π/A)z̄` (`A` the covolume; periodic by the choice of `s₂`); the
value is a unit. (c): from (a), (b), the logarithmic derivatives of a product being the sums. At `2` the first derivative needs
one more step: for a translate of `θ₀` by a point `s` of odd order it is `F(s + T₀)` (`T₀` the point of order two defining `θ₀`), and
(b) alone gives only `a₁/2 + ½𝒪`. The addition formula `ζ(u + v) − ζ(u) − ζ(v) = ½(℘′(u) − ℘′(v))/(℘(u) − ℘(v))`, with
`℘′ = 2y + a₁x + a₃` in the coordinates of the model, gives `F(u + v) = F(u) + F(v) + λ(u, v) + a₁/2`, `λ(u, v)` the slope
`(y(u) − y(v))/(x(u) − x(v))` of the chord; `F(T₀) = 0` (`F` is odd and periodic, and `T₀ = −T₀`); for `s ≠ 0`, `F(s) ∈ a₁/2 + 𝒪` by
(b), and `λ(s, T₀)` is integral because neither point lies in `Ê(𝔪)` and their reductions are not `±` each other (orders odd and
`2`); so `F(s + T₀) ∈ a₁ + 𝒪 = 𝒪`. For `s = 0`, `θ₀` is even.

*Check.* The bound of (a) at `d` is attained: for the lattice of discriminant `1` with complex multiplication by the maximal
order of `ℚ(√−d)`, `√−d·s₂ = 1/4, 2/3, 2, 12, 38, 724` (up to a root of unity) for `d = 7, 11, 19, 43, 67, 163`, so `v_d(s₂) = −1/2`;
for `d = 7` neither `s₂` nor `℘(ω₀/2)` is `2`-integral, while `θ₀″(0) = −(℘(ω₀/2) + s₂) = ±2√−7/7` is.

*KNOWN* in kind (Weierstrass; Mumford, the theorem of the cube, AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A1.5; complex multiplication).
(a) and (c) follow from [FormalProof], `Formalism/LocalToGlobal/TorsionIntegrality/RamifiedCurveKernels/StatementR.lean`
(`Formalism.L2.Th1.G.HalfValuation`).
*Needs:* 2.6, 3.1a; AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A1.5; ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §1.2.

### 3.1d The CM theta family

*Definition (the CM theta family) and theorem.* Recall that `d` is prime and `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)` (on the hosts
of the chain `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)`, Layer 2), so `2` splits in `K` and `d ≥ 7`, and that both ranks `N_{j−1}`, `N_j` are even (2.1).

*Definition.* Fix a `K`-linear isometry `γ₀` of `E⁴` with the form `Σ_a Tr_{E/K}(x_aȳ_a)` onto `K^{4f}` with the standard form, and
for each prime `p` split in `K` a prime `𝔭 | p`, a generator `π_p` of `𝔭^{h_K}` and an isometry `γ_p` carrying `𝔞₀⁴ ⊗ ℤ_(p)` onto
`𝒪_K^{4f} ⊗ ℤ_(p)` (it exists by the classification of unimodular Hermitian lattices at a split prime); put
`κ_M := ∏_p (π_p/π̄_p)^{−ord_𝔭 det(γ_pγ₀^{−1})/(2h_K)}` (a finite product of fixed roots: `γ₀` is `p`-integral in both directions for
almost all `p`; the exponent does not depend on `γ_p`, two choices differing by a `p`-integral unitary matrix; `|κ_M| = 1` at every
embedding). `θ_M` is the theta function of `(ℂ/Λ_K) ⊗_{𝒪_K} 𝔞₀` with `θ_M^{⊗4} = κ_M·I_{γ₀}(θ₀^{⊗4f})` (`θ₀` of 3.1c; determined up to a
fourth root of unity, fixed once), `I_{γ₀}` the isogeny operator from the product lattice (restriction to the common
sublattice followed by the semicharacter-weighted trace, without the normalising factor of (b)). For the standard lattices
`Θ^{CM}_{L_{j−1},L_j} := ⊗θ_M` over the matrix entries; for the other pairs of self-dual lattices `L ⊂ X_{j−1}`, `L′ ⊂ X_j`
(`1 ≤ j ≤ m`), `Θ^{CM}_{L,L′} ∈ Th(L, L′)` is defined by the rules of (b) below.

*Theorem.* (o) The rules of (b) are consistent: the function they assign to a pair does not depend on the moves used to reach it.
(a) The Taylor coefficients of `Θ^{CM}_{L,L′}` at `0` in the coordinates `z` lie in a number field (contained in `𝕃` after
enlarging it, 2.6).
(b) `Θ^{CM}_{γL,γ′L′}(γ′zγ^{−1}) = Θ^{CM}_{L,L′}(z)` for `γ ∈ U(X_{j−1})(F)`, `γ′ ∈ U(X_j)(F)`; and for self-dual `L″ ⊂ X_j`,
`Θ^{CM}_{L,L″} = χ_D(𝔡(L′, L″))^{−N_{j−1}/2}·I(Θ^{CM}_{L,L′})`, for self-dual `L₁ ⊂ X_{j−1}`,
`Θ^{CM}_{L₁,L′} = χ_D(𝔡(L, L₁))^{N_j/2}·I(Θ^{CM}_{L,L′})` (`χ_D`, `𝔡` of 3.1b), where `I` is the *normalised isogeny operator* between the
two lines: for the period lattices `𝔙`, `𝔙′` of the source and the target line and `𝔙″ := 𝔙 ∩ 𝔙′`,
`I(Θ) := [𝔙′ : 𝔙″]^{−1/2}·Σ_{y∈𝔙′/𝔙″} α′(y)^{−1}·U_yΘ` (`α′` the semicharacter of 3.1a on `𝔙′`; the summand depends only on `y`
modulo `𝔙″`, and the sum lies in the target line; `[𝔙′ : 𝔙″] = [𝔙 : 𝔙″]`, both lattices being unimodular for `Im H_A`).
(c) The mean of `|Θ^{CM}_{L,L′}|²e^{−πH_A}` over `V_ℂ(j)/𝔙(L, L′)` is the same for all pairs of the genus, and so is the mean
for every Galois conjugate; it is invariant under the Fock translations, and its mean logarithm over the conjugates is at
most `C·f·N_{j−1}N_j·(La + |(L′/L)(1, χ_K)|)` (`La` of 2.1; so at most `2C·f·N_{j−1}N_j·La` when `|(L′/L)(1, χ_K)| ≤ La`).
(d) For every prime `p ≠ d` (including `2`, the primes dividing `N(𝔞₀)`, and `3` when `3` splits in `K`, that is when
`d ≡ 2 (mod 3)`; the case of `3` inert in `K` is not covered, and does not occur for the hosts of the chain, for which every
prime `≤ 12` splits in `K` by `SizeInert`) and every
`p`-integral finite combination `Θ′` of Fock translates of `Θ^{CM}_{L₁,L₁′}` by torsion points of order prime to `p`, with `L₁ = L`
and `L₁′ = L′` at `p`, all derivatives `(∂_{y_1}⋯∂_{y_n}Θ′)(0)` (`n ≥ 0`, `y_i ∈ 𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L, L′)`) are `p`-integral, that is,
integral at every place of `ℚ̄` above `p`.
(e) At `p = d`: such a `Θ′` is `exp(−s₂·Q_d)·Θ′₀` with `Θ′₀` as in (d), where
`Q_d := Σ_{{τ, cσ_dτ}} υ_τ Σ_{a,b} z_{τ,a,b} z_{cσ_dτ,a,b}`, the sum over the unordered pairs `{τ, cσ_dτ}` of elements of `Φ` (`σ_d`
the nontrivial element of the inertia group at `d`, `cσ_d ∈ Φ` an element of order `2`, FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3), in the coordinates of local bases
of `L`, `L′` at the places `w`, `c(w)` of `E` above `d` seen by `τ` and `cσ_dτ` (distinct by the second condition of
`HostSplitting E`, assumed in this layer) dual to each other, `υ_τ` units with `υ_{cσ_dτ} = υ_τ`, and `s₂ = s₂(Λ_K)` (3.1c), with
`v_d(s₂) ≥ −1/2`. Summed over all `τ ∈ Φ` (each pair occurs twice) the exponent reads
`−(s₂/2)·Σ_{τ∈Φ} υ_τ Σ_{a,b} z_{τ,a,b} z_{cσ_dτ,a,b}`.
(f) *Fourth powers.* For every prime `p` and every torsion point `y` of order prime to `p`, in the coordinates of an isometry of
four copies of `𝔙(L, L′)` with a power of `Λ_K` that is `p`-integral in both directions, `(U_yΘ^{CM}_{L,L′})^{⊗4}` is a `p`-integral
combination of products of Fock translates of `θ₀` by points of order prime to `p`, times a `p`-unit.

Route. The construction and (b) are definitions once (o) holds. (a) is the algebraicity of Taylor coefficients of torsion
translates of theta functions of CM abelian varieties. In (c), the equality of the means (given (o)): the normalised operator
of (b) preserves the mean, because the translates `U_yΘ`, `y ∈ 𝔙′/𝔙″`, have the mean of `Θ` and are pairwise orthogonal on
`V_ℂ(j)/𝔙″` (under `z ↦ z + x`, `x ∈ 𝔙`, the product `U_yΘ·conj(U_{y′}Θ)·e^{−πH_A}` acquires the factor `e^{2πi·Im H_A(y − y′, x)}`, a
character of `𝔙/𝔙″` that is trivial only for `y − y′ ∈ 𝔙`, `𝔙` being unimodular for `Im H_A`), so the trace multiplies the mean by
`[𝔙′ : 𝔙″]`; and `|χ_D| = 1`. Without the factor `[𝔙′ : 𝔙″]^{−1/2}` a move followed by its inverse would multiply the line by a
scalar of absolute value `[𝔙′ : 𝔙″]`, which exceeds `1` as soon as the lattices differ (for instance at a place split in `E`,
scaling one coordinate of `L′` by a uniformiser and the dual coordinate inversely). The bound on the mean logarithm is a
Chowla–Selberg computation for the standard pair, in which `(L′/L)(1, χ_K)` enters; it is not given here. (f): the fourth tensor power of a translate of `Θ^{CM}_{L,L′}`, in the coordinates of such an isometry, is by the
isogeny formula a `p`-integral combination of products of translates of `θ₀` by points of order prime to `p`, up to a normalising
constant. At `p`, `|κ_M|_v²·|det ι|_v = 1` for every isometry `ι` of the standard form carrying `γ₀(𝔞₀⁴)` to a lattice of index prime
to `p` in the standard one (the exponent of `κ_M` is `ord_𝔭 det ι`, and `v_𝔭(π_p/π̄_p) = h_K`); the ratio of the isogeny transforms
through `γ₀` and through `γ_p` is a constant `c_ι` whose eighth power is, up to a unit, `(det ι)^{4}` or `(det ι)^{−4}` (a determinant
factor of a Cayley transform times a normalised quadratic Gauss sum, an eighth root of unity); `κ_M` makes the constant a unit
exactly for the sign with `|c_ι|_v = |κ_M|_v^{−1}`, which is the one the normalisation needs; the normalisation passes to the
family by weak approximation for the unitary groups, equivariance, tensor powers and isogeny operators of index prime to `p`.
(d): if `T^{⊗4}` has integral derivatives, so has `T`; by (f) and 3.1c (c), the derivatives in the product coordinates are integral,
and derivatives along the lattice directions are integral combinations of them. (e): at `d` the bilinear Gram matrix of a
`d`-integral frame is the Hermitian one modulo `√−d`, which turns `Σ_w z_w²` into twice the sum over the pairs `{τ, cσ_dτ}` of the
pairing of `τ` with `cσ_dτ` (up to units), so the factor `e^{−(s₂/2)Σ_w z_w²}` of 3.1c (c) is `exp(−s₂·Q_d)`. The following are not
given here: (o) and the equivariance on which the normalisation of the family at every prime rests; (a); the bound of (c); the
eighth-power law for `c_ι` used in (f) and its sign; the integrality of the series of `θ₀` and its translates in the formal parameter at `2` (only their
derivatives at `0` are used); the eigenframe at `d` and the separation statement it uses; a `d`-integral isometry of four copies
of `𝔞₀` with the standard lattice.

*Checks.* (i) By (e) the jets at `d` are not bounded below when `v_d(s₂) < 0`, as in the Check of 3.1c: a jet of order `2n` of
`exp(−s₂·Q_d)` is a sum over matchings of the derivatives, each matched pair costing `v_d(s₂)`, so the derivatives of `Θ^{CM}` at `0`
do not generate an ideal integral at `d`, whatever constant they are divided by. (ii) Clauses (a), (b), (d)–(f) do not determine
the family: `N·Θ^{CM}` satisfies them for every integer `N ≠ 0` ((d) is an integrality statement; in (f) the coefficients are
multiplied by `N⁴`), and (c) up to `2 log|N|` in the mean logarithm; the defining equality fixes the normalisation, and a
characterisation by (f) alone would need a primitivity condition.

*KNOWN* in kind (Shimura; Mumford, the theorem of the cube, AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A1.5; Serre's construction), *NEW*
(the family with (b)–(f)). [FormalProof] establishes the family, with (b)–(f), for the hosts of FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums
§0.4 (`q ≡ 3 (mod 8)`, `d ≡ 7 (mod 16)`, layer `n ≥ 2`); for the other hosts of Layer 2 (a prime `d ≡ 7 (mod 8)`) the
theorem is this roadmap's wider form, which rests on the steps that the route lists as not given. The definition and
(o), (a), (b), (e): [FormalProof],
`Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Proofs/IwasawaDecomposition/ExistsThetaVectorFamilyOfCurveDatumStatement.lean`
(`GlobalTheory.T.exists_thetaVectorFamily_of_curveDatum`), `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/TaylorLogMetric.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.TaylorLogMetric.vec22`),
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/TorsionIntegrality/GaussianEigenFrames/StatementJ.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Th1.GD.ShapeEigenLoc`); (c)
follows from [FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/TaylorLogMetric.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.TaylorLogMetric.th22`), `Formalism/LocalToGlobal/KernelLattices/LateralTheory.lean`
(`Formalism.L2.Ker.Base.ThP`). *Needs:* FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums §0.3,
2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.6, 3.1a, 3.1b, 3.1c; AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A5.3–A5.4; ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §1.2.

### 3.2 The step of the chain

*Definitions and lemma.*

- *The pair.* Step `j` is the homomorphism pair of `(X_{j−1}, X_j)` in the normal form of 2.2a with skew element `δ_T`
  (2.2(f); `Im τ(δ_T) > 0` for `τ ∈ Φ`): the symplectic space is `𝕎_j` (restricted to `F`), on which `(g, h) ∈ U(X_{j−1}) × U(X_j)` acts by
  `(g, h)·x := h∘x∘g^{−1}`, with the Hermitian form `h_j(x, y) := tr(x^†y)` (`x^†` the adjoint, conjugate-linear in `x`), the
  alternating form `B_j(x, y) := Tr_{E/F}(δ_T·h_j(x, y))` and the standard additive character `ψ_F` of `𝔸_F/F` (`ψ_{F,∞}(x) = e^{2πix}`);
  equivalently the form `Tr_{E/F}(δ_T·tr(y^†x))` with the character `ψ̄_F`, since `ψ̄_F(Tr(δ_T·tr(y^†x))) = ψ_F(Tr(δ_T·tr(x^†y)))`.
  The splitting characters of step `j` for a tuple `ξ` are `(μ_j(ξ), μ_j(ξ))`: the
  twists are `μ̃_j(ξ)(det g)^{−1}` on `U(X_{j−1})` and `μ̃_j(ξ)(det h)` on `U(X_j)` (`μ̃(x/x̄) := μ(x)`). The self-dual lattice of
  step `j` is `Â := (𝔞₀Hom_{𝒪_E}(L_{j−1}, L_j))^∧` (3.1a), with the lift `q_Â` of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §0.3 for which
  the semicharacter of its 1.8 is `α^{−1}` (lifts form a torsor under the characters of `Â`, and changing the lift moves the
  semicharacter through all semicharacters of the form, its 1.8 Check); `φ_Â` is its lattice vector. At the places above `d`,
  where `δ_T` is not a unit and `ψ_F` is ramified, `Â_v` is self-dual for `ψ_{F,v}∘B_j` but is not the tensor product of a self-dual
  lattice of `X_{j−1}^∨` with a lattice of `X_j` unimodular for the skew form `δ_T⟨·,·⟩`; only the lattice vectors of 0.3 of that
  roadmap in their general form (any compact open self-dual subgroup, any conductor) are used there. At a place `v` split in
  `E` of odd residue characteristic (those above `d` included, 2.2(g)), `Â_v` is `M_{N_{j−1}×N_j}(𝒪_w)` in bases of `L_{j−1,w}` and
  `(𝔞₀)_wL_{j,w}` together with its dual in the complementary Lagrangian (the component at `w̄`), and `q_Â` is trivial on it (at an odd
  place the trivial map is a lift; `q_Â` takes the values `±1` on the dense global lattice and its components at the dyadic
  places take values in `2`-power roots of unity, so its components at the odd places, characters of pro-odd groups, are
  trivial); so `φ_{Â_v}` is the type-II lattice vector of 0.3 of that roadmap.
- *Comparison with ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups.* Step `j` is the homomorphism pair `(X_{j−1}^∨, δ_TX_j)` of that roadmap's
  0.1 (`X_{j−1}^∨` its `X₁^*`: the dual space with the induced Hermitian form, on which `g ∈ U(X_{j−1})` acts by `(g^{−1})^∨`;
  `δ′ = δ_T`), with symplectic space `𝕎_j`, form `B_j`, action `(g, h)·x = hxg^{−1}` and the standard character; its CM type
  `{τ : Im τ(δ_T) > 0}` is `Φ`. ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5 (with `δ = δ′`) is the dictionary of the step, as one statement (its letters:
  `V = X_{j−1}^∨`, `W = δ_TX_j`): (a) at the
  real place below `τ ∈ Φ`, `τ_v = c∘τ`, `ε_v = −1`, and the splitting characters `χ_V = χ_W = μ_j(ξ)`, of infinity type
  `−2w_j(τ)` at `τ` (2.4(b)), give `a_v = (N_j − 2w_j(τ))/2 = −s♯_j(τ)` and `b_v = (N_{j−1} − 2w_j(τ))/2 = −s_div,j(τ)`
  (`s♯_j := w_j − N_j/2`, `s_div,j := w_j − N_{j−1}/2`); (b) a weight `λ` on its `U(V)` is the weight `λ` of `U(X_{j−1})` at `τ`, a weight
  `λ` on its `U(W)` the weight `λ^*` of `U(X_j)` at `τ`, and the polynomial Fock vectors of degree `k` are
  `⊕_μ (V_{μ − s♯_j})^∨ ⊗ V_{(μ,0) − s_div,j}` of `U(X_{j−1}) × U(X_j)` through `τ`; for `μ = ν_j(τ)` this is
  `V_{Λ_{j−1}(τ)}^∨ ⊗ V_{Λ_j(τ)}` (2.3(b)–(c)), the type of `ℱ_j` below, and forms of weight `Λ_{j−1}` on `U(X_{j−1})` go to forms
  of weight `Λ_j` on `U(X_j)`, the occurrence condition being that `ν_j(τ)` is a partition; (c) its Bargmann readings `ϑ` are
  holomorphic for `cΦ`, and the theta functions of this layer are their complex conjugates, holomorphic for `Φ`: for `g, h`
  finite adelic and a finite datum `φ_f`, `Θ := conj∘ϑ_{ω_f(g,h)φ_f}` satisfies `θ(g, h; B^{−1}P ⊗ φ_f) = (P(π^{−1}∂_w)Θ)(0)`, where `w`
  are the Fock coordinates of 3.1a (orthonormal for `H_A`) and `P` is read in `w` with the coefficients it has in the
  coordinates `w̄` of that roadmap's 1.7 (that is, `π^{−1}∂/∂w_i` is substituted for `w̄_i`); for a lattice vector `Θ` has Riemann
  form `Im H_A` of 3.1a and semicharacter `α` (the conjugate of the semicharacter `α^{−1}` of `ϑ`); (d) its Satake relation is
  `c_w·α` with no inversion (3.3(iv)); (e) its Weil representation is the one characterised by the five properties of
  ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §4.4a (3.3).
- *Fock summand.* `ℱ_j` is the summand of the algebra `ℂ[∂_z]` of constant-coefficient differential operators on `V_ℂ(j)` which,
  under `(GL_{N_{j−1}}, GL_{N_j})`-duality at every `τ` (TensorInductionAndFiniteGroupInputs §7.1) for the action
  `D ↦ D∘(z ↦ hzg^{−1})`, is `⊗_τ (V_{ν_j(τ)}^{(N_{j−1})})^∨ ⊗ V_{(ν_j(τ), 0^{t+1})}^{(N_j)}`; the vacuum transforms by
  `det(g)^{s♯_j}det(h)^{−s_div,j}`, so the summand of the Fock space is
  `(V_{ν_j} ⊗ det^{−s♯_j})^∨ ⊗ (V_{(ν_j,0^{t+1})} ⊗ det^{−s_div,j}) = V_{Λ_{j−1}}^∨ ⊗ V_{Λ_j}`. The kernel of the step at a pair of classes
  is a map `V_{Λ_{j−1}}^∨ → V_{Λ_j}^∨` between the coefficient modules of 3.1 (Gross's model). The step below substitutes the
  polynomial `P_𝒸` itself, so no identification of `ℱ_j` with a space of matrix coefficients enters its definition; where
  `ℱ_j` is identified with `(V_{Λ_{j−1}} ⊗ V_{Λ_j}^∨)^∨`, it is through the matrix coefficients `v ⊗ v′ ↦ (y ↦ ⟨ρ(y)v, v′⟩)`
  of the invariant forms of 3.1, not through the identification that is isometric for the Fischer form.
- *Finite data.* An *admissible finite datum* of step `j` for the tuple `ξ` is a vector `φ_f = φ_{f,j}(ξ) = ⊗_v φ_{f,v}` of the Weil
  representation of step `j` at the finite places, each `φ_{f,v}` a finite combination `Σ_k r_{k,v}·ρ(y_{k,v})φ_{Â_v}` of Heisenberg
  translates of the lattice vector (`r_{k,v} ∈ 𝕃`, `y_{k,v} ∈ 𝕎_j(F_v)`), such that:
  (A1) `φ_{f,v} = φ_{Â_v}` at every `v ∉ S(ξ)` not above `T(u)`;
  (A2) at `v | T(u)`, `φ_{f,v} = φ_{T,v}`, the indicator, in the model on `Hom(X_{j−1,w}, X_{j,w})` of 2.2a(a) (`w | v` above `π_u`),
  of the set `Maps(𝔡₀^{(j−1)}, 𝔡₀^{(j)})` of maps between the standard pairs (2.9b);
  (A3) at `v ∈ S(ξ)` (split in `E` and prime to `2f_E`: 2.4(b), 2.5, 2.7): the `y_{k,v}` have order a power of the residue
  characteristic modulo `Â_v`, and `ω_v(κ, κ′)φ_{f,v} = χ_{j−1}(κ)^{−1}χ_j(κ′)·φ_{f,v}` for `κ` in the column level of `X_{j−1}` and `κ′` in
  that of `X_j` at `v` (2.9a, 2.9c; at `j − 1 = 0` the level of the word), `χ_{j−1}`, `χ_j` their characters; at the type primes,
  moreover, `φ_{f,v}` is, in the model on `Hom(X_{j−1,w_v}, X_{j,w_v})` of 2.2a(a), a function of `x` modulo `g` supported on the
  integral matrices.
  Every statement of this layer holds for every admissible finite datum. The finite data of the chain come in finite *systems* of
  admissible data: at a twisted tuple the data of the steps `1, …, m − 2` are indexed by pairs of charts at the twist places
  (the charts of the sub-roadmap SeesawForThetaChains, which constructs these data: finitely many double cosets at each twist place, 2.9c
  Check), and the data of the last two steps by a chart and a coset of the line
  variable (`g^{−1}L_{m−1,w_v}` modulo `L_{m−1,w_v}` at the type primes, `p^{−m}L_{m−1,w_v}` modulo `p^mL_{m−1,w_v}` at the twist places);
  for the base tuple only the last two steps carry an index; each member of such a system is admissible for the column levels
  of 2.9a, 2.9c.
- *Theta functions of a step.* For classes `𝒸 ∈ Cl_{j−1}`, `𝒸′ ∈ Cl_j`, with the self-dual lattices `L^{sd}_𝒸`, `L′^{sd}_{𝒸′}` of their class
  lattices (3.1; they are the class lattices away from `T(u)`), write
  `ω_f(t_𝒸, t′_{𝒸′})φ_f = Σ_k r_k·ρ(y_k)·ω_f(t_𝒸, t′_{𝒸′})φ_Â` (so the `r_k` are the coefficients of `φ_f`, and `y_k ∈ 𝕎_j(F)` is congruent
  to the moved translation point modulo `(t_𝒸, t′_{𝒸′})Â`: every point of `𝕎_j(𝔸_f)` is congruent to a rational one modulo a compact
  open subgroup), and put `Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ) := Σ_k r_k·e_k·U_{δ_K y_k}Θ^{CM}_{L^{sd}_𝒸,L′^{sd}_{𝒸′}}` (`δ_K y_k` the point of `V_ℂ(j)` of the rational
  point `y_k`, 3.1a), `e_k ∈ ℂ^1` the phase of the Heisenberg lift of `y_k`
  (the complex conjugate of the factor of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §1.8, translates); it is well defined modulo
  the period lattice by the functional equation of `Θ^{CM}`. (By 3.3 (i), the reading of `ω_f(t_𝒸, t′_{𝒸′})φ_f` is
  `c₀(j)ρ^ξ_j(t_𝒸)ρ′^ξ_j(t′_{𝒸′})·Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ)`: the class factor occurs once.)
- *The step operator.* Let `ρ^ξ_j(t) := μ̃_j(ξ)(det t)^{−1}χ_D(det t)^{−N_j/2}` for `t ∈ U(X_{j−1})(𝔸_f)` and
  `ρ′^ξ_j(t′) := μ̃_j(ξ)(det t′)χ_D(det t′)^{N_{j−1}/2}` for `t′ ∈ U(X_j)(𝔸_f)` (3.1b). For `φ ∈ ℳ_{j−1}(u, ξ)` write
  `φ(𝒸)(y) = P_𝒸(y)·∏_τ det(y_τ)^{−s♯_j(τ)}`, `P_𝒸` a polynomial of column degrees `ν_j(τ)` (2.3(c)), and define

  `(K_j(ξ)φ)(𝒸′)(y′) := Σ_{𝒸 ∈ Cl_{j−1}} |Γ_𝒸|^{−1}·ρ^ξ_j(t_𝒸)ρ′^ξ_j(t′_{𝒸′})·[P_𝒸((c_τ·ᵗ∂_{z_τ}·y′_τ|_{N_{j−1}})_τ)Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ)](0)·∏_τ det(y′_τ)^{−s_div,j(τ)}`,

  where `y′_τ|_{N_{j−1}}` is the `N_j × N_{j−1}` matrix of the first `N_{j−1}` columns of `y′_τ` and `c_τ` is the constant of 3.1a.
  The chain is defined by this formula; 3.3 identifies it with a class-set theta lift and 3.4 shows that `K_j(ξ)` maps
  `ℳ_{j−1}(u, ξ)` into `ℳ_j(u, ξ)`, and `ℳ_{j−1}(𝕃)` into `ℳ_j(𝕃)` (the jets are `𝕃`-rational by 3.1d(a)).

*Lemma (the data at the bad places).* (i) `φ_{T,v}` is a finite combination of Heisenberg translates of `φ_{Â_v}` by points of
order `T(u)` modulo `Â_v`; it is a function on `Hom(X_{j−1,w}, X_{j,w})` for the place `w` above `π_u` only (at the conjugate place
nothing but integrality is asked); it is fixed by `Stab(𝔡₀^{(j−1)}) × Stab(𝔡₀^{(j)})` and by the simultaneous diamond operators
`(⟨a⟩_v, ⟨a⟩_v)`, and moved by `(t, t′)` it is the indicator of `Maps(t𝔡₀^{(j−1)}, t′𝔡₀^{(j)})` times the values of the splitting
characters at `det t`, `det t′` (unramified at `T(u)`, 2.5) and the positive factor `|det t|_w^{N_j/2}|det t′|_w^{−N_{j−1}/2}` (2.2a(a)).
(ii) At a type prime `v | g`, a datum with (A3) is a combination of Heisenberg translates
by points of `𝕎_j(F_v)` whose component at `w_v` lies in `Â_v` and whose component at `w̄_v` is killed by `π̄_g` (the prime of `K`
below `w̄_v`, a uniformiser there and a unit at `w_v`): its translation points are `π̄_g`-torsion modulo `Â_v`, and the datum, read on
the period lattice, is a function on `𝔙/π_g𝔙` (it is *taken modulo `π_g`*, the prime of `K` below the places `w_v` of 2.4(b)). The
prime `π_g` is the same in its roles: below the places `w_v` (2.4(b)), of the level structures (2.9a) and of the datum (here);
the type-prime cost of the integrality estimates of the chain is read at the same prime, so the prime modulo which the
type-prime datum is taken is `ϖ_g = π_g`.

Route: (i) `Maps(𝔡, 𝔡′)` is a coset, in `Hom(Λ, Λ′)`, of the subgroup `Hom(Λ, ker fn′) ⊃ ϖ·Hom(Λ, Λ′)`; its indicator is a function on
`Hom(Λ, Λ′)/ϖHom(Λ, Λ′)` supported on `Hom(Λ, Λ′)`, the support of `φ_{Â_v}` (`𝔞₀` is a unit above `T(u)`, which splits completely
in `E`), hence a combination of the characters of that quotient times `φ_{Â_v}`, that is, of Heisenberg translates by points of the
complementary Lagrangian of order `T(u)`; `x ↦ h∘x∘g^{−1}` maps `Maps(𝔡, 𝔡′)` onto `Maps(g𝔡, h𝔡′)`, and `(a·fn′)∘x = a·fn` on `Λ`; the factor
for general `(t, t′)` is that of 2.2a(a) with `n_S = N_{j−1}`, `n_T = N_j`, and on the stabilisers it is `1` (unramified characters at
units, `|det| = 1`). (ii) The same
expansion in the characters of the integral matrices modulo `g` at `w_v`, which are translations by points of the component at
`w̄_v` of order dividing the uniformiser `π̄_g`; the components of `𝔙 ⊗ ℤ_g` at the places of `E` above `g` are those at the `w_v`
(the places above `π_g`, where `π_g` is a uniformiser) and at their conjugates, and a function of the components at the `w_v`
modulo `g` is a function on `𝔙/π_g𝔙`.

*API.* (a) For two tuples whose finite data agree at the places of a pair of classes, the summands at `(𝒸, 𝒸′)` differ by the
factor `(ρ^ξ_jρ′^ξ_j/ρ^{ξ′}_jρ′^{ξ′}_j)(t_𝒸, t′_{𝒸′}) = ξ̃(det t_𝒸)^{−s}ξ̃(det t′_{𝒸′})^{s}·(ξ′ likewise)^{−1}`, a root of unity
(`ξ` of finite order; `s` the sign of 2.5). (b) Changing `c_τ` by a unit of `𝒪_𝕃` changes `K_j(ξ)` by a unit on each polynomial
degree. (c) Let `𝔭` be a place of `𝕃` above a prime `p ∉ {d, g₀, g₁, T(u)}` that is not the residue characteristic of a place of
`Q(ξ)` (and `p ≠ 3` unless `3` splits in `K`).
Then `Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ)` is a combination of Fock translates of `Θ^{CM}_{L^{sd}_𝒸,L′^{sd}_{𝒸′}}` by points of order prime to `p` (by
(A1)–(A3) the translation points have order supported at the places of `S(ξ)` and above `T(u)`); if its coefficients `r_ke_k` are
`𝔭`-integral, its derivatives at `0` along `𝔞₀Hom(L^{sd}_𝒸, L′^{sd}_{𝒸′})` are `𝔭`-integral (3.1d(d)), and so are the coefficients of
`[P_𝒸((c_τ·ᵗ∂_{z_τ}·y′_τ|_{N_{j−1}})_τ)Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ)](0)` for every `P_𝒸` with `𝔭`-integral coefficients. The `𝔭`-integrality of the
coefficients `r_{k,v}` of the finite data used at the representatives, and the choice of representatives with `t_𝒸`, `t′_{𝒸′}`
trivial at `p` (for which `ρ^ξ_jρ′^ξ_j` is a `𝔭`-unit; for an arbitrary representative it is not), are not proved here; they are
the input that the integrality estimates of the chain take together with (c).

*Checks.* (i) Rank one (the dictionary): `E = K`, `X_{j−1} = X_j = K`, a splitting character of infinity type `(z/z̄)^{−w}` at
`τ` (`w ∈ ½ + ℤ`): in that roadmap, with `ε_v = −1` and `a_v = b_v = ½ − w`, a form of weight `λ` on `U(X_{j−1})` (weight `λ` at
`τ_v` on its `U(V)`) occurs iff `λ − a_v = λ + w − ½ ≥ 0` and lifts to weight `−(λ − a_v + b_v) = −λ` at `τ_v`, that is `λ` at `τ` on
`U(X_j)`; the formula of the step for `N_{j−1} = N_j = 1` (`s♯ = s_div = w − ½`) gives the same condition `ν = λ + w − ½ ≥ 0` and the
weight `ν − s_div = λ`. With `V := X_{j−1}` (the tensor model) the same dictionary sends `λ` to `−λ`: in the homomorphism model the
Harish-Chandra parameters persist. (ii) The formula of the step for `N_{j−1} = 2`, `N_j = 4` and a weight `λ` of `GL_2` on
`U(X_{j−1})`: `s♯ = w − 2`, `s_div = w − 1`, `ν = λ + (w − 2)·(1, 1)`, and the output weight is
`(ν, 0, 0) − (w − 1)·(1, 1, 1, 1) = (λ − (1, 1), (1 − w)²)`: the old entries drop by `(N_j − N_{j−1})/2 = 1` and the new entries are `−w + N_{j−1}/2`, as in 2.3(b). (A step
from rank `1` to rank `2` does not occur: the splitting characters of the two members would need exponents of different parity.)

*NEW* (definition). The pair, the finite data, the theta functions of a step and the step by jets, with the lemma and
the API, follow from [FormalProof], `Formalism/LocalToGlobal/KernelLattices/ThDistC.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Ker.Amb.jetK`).
