1.7, 1.8, 2.1, 2.5; TensorInductionAndFiniteGroupInputs §7.1.

### 3.3 The step as a class-set theta lift

Let `ω_ξ` be the Weil representation of step `j` with the splitting characters
`(μ_j(ξ), μ_j(ξ))`: the representation `ω_{ψ, μ_j(ξ), μ_j(ξ)}` of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5(f) for the pair of 3.2,
the one characterised by the five properties of ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs §4.4a (among them continuity of the orbit maps
`(g, h) ↦ ω(g, h)φ` into `L²` for the adelic topology, and, at a place split in `E`, the law `r·μ̃_j(ξ)(det g)^{−1}μ̃_j(ξ)(det h)`,
`r > 0`, of the functional `φ ↦ φ(0)` of the type-II model, `det g` taken on `X_{j−1}`; 2.2a(a)). Let `φ⁰` be the vector of norm one
fixed by the Heisenberg translations of the standard lattice `Â` (3.2) and killed by the annihilation operators at the real
places (the vacuum), and `n_Â` the scalar with `φ_Â ⊗ (archimedean vacuum) = n_Â·φ⁰`. The *reading* of a finite vector is the
complex conjugate of its Bargmann reading (ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §§2.5(c), 1.7 for the pair of 3.2).
(i) *Vacuum.* There is `c₀(j) ∈ ℂ^×` such that for every tuple `ξ ∈ Ξ` and all `g ∈ U(X_{j−1})(𝔸_f)`, `h ∈ U(X_j)(𝔸_f)` with
`gL_{j−1} = L`, `hL_j = L′`, `det g = x/x̄`, `det h = y/ȳ`: the reading of `ω_ξ(g, h)φ⁰` is `c₀(j)·μ_j(ξ)(x)^{−1}μ_j(ξ)(y)·Θ⁰_{L,L′}`,
where `Θ⁰_{L,L′}` is the image of `Θ^{CM}_{L_{j−1},L_j}` under the isogeny operators of 3.1d(b) without the factors `χ_D`; so the
reading is `c₀(j)·ρ^ξ_j(g)ρ′^ξ_j(h)·Θ^{CM}_{L,L′}`.
(ii) *Split places.* Let `S_sp` be a finite set of places of `F` split in `E` and prime to `2f_E`, and `φ_f` a finite vector equal to
`φ⁰` outside `S_sp` and, at `S_sp`, a finite combination of Heisenberg translates of the lattice vector by points of odd order
modulo the lattice. Then for every polynomial Fock vector `P` and all finite adelic `(g, h)`: `θ(g, h; B^{−1}P ⊗ φ_f) = (P(π^{−1}∂_w)Θ)(0)`
(the normalisation of 3.2), where `Θ` is the corresponding combination of Fock translates of the reading of `ω_ξ(g, h)φ⁰` (the
coefficients being the values of the moved datum).
(iii) *Kernel identity.* For every word `u`, tuple `ξ` and admissible finite datum `φ_f`, with `c_K(j) := vol(K_∞K)·c₀(j)·n_Â` (`K` the
level), `vol(K_∞K)·θ(t_𝒸, t′_{𝒸′}; B^{−1}P ⊗ φ_f) = c_K(j)·ρ^ξ_j(t_𝒸)ρ′^ξ_j(t′_{𝒸′})·(P(π^{−1}∂_w)Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ))(0)` for all classes and all
polynomial Fock vectors `P`, read in the normalisation of 3.2. In the coordinates `z` of 3.1a, `π^{−1}∂_w = (area(Λ_K)^{1/2}/π)·∂_z = π^{−1/2}|s_Λ|·∂_z`
with `s_Λ := −δ_K/√(2π/√d)`, `|s_Λ|² = area(Λ_K)/π`; writing `P(w̄) = P_1(√π·(s_Λ/|s_Λ|)·w̄)` (`P_1` the same Fock vector as a polynomial in
the normalised creation operators, rotated by the phase of `s_Λ`), the right side is `(P_1(s_Λ·∂_z)Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ))(0)`, and for `P`
homogeneous of degree `k`, `(P(s_Λ·∂_z)Θ)(0) = π^{k/2}(s_Λ/|s_Λ|)^k·(P(π^{−1}∂_w)Θ)(0)`. Equivalently, the kernel of `K_j(ξ)` at a polynomial
`Q` is `vol(K_∞K)/c_K(j)` times the theta kernel at the Fock polynomial `Q((√π·c_τ/|s_Λ|)·)` (`c_τ∂_z = (√π·c_τ/|s_Λ|)·π^{−1}∂_w` at `τ`); on
`ℱ_j`, of degree `|ν_j(τ)|` at each `τ`, this rescaling is the scalar `∏_τ(√π·c_τ/|s_Λ|)^{|ν_j(τ)|}`. So, up to a constant, `K_j(ξ)` is
the class-set theta lift of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §1.2 for the pair of 3.2 and the test vectors `B^{−1}P ⊗ φ_f`,
`P ∈ ℱ_j`. Two tuples with the same levels have the same constant `c_K(j)`; through `vol(K_∞K)` the constant depends on the column
levels chosen (2.9–2.9c), and for a level `K′ ⊂ K` of index `ι` it is `ι^{−1}` times the constant for `K`.
(iv) *Hecke operators.* At a place `v` of `F` split in `E`, of odd residue characteristic, outside `S(ξ)` and not above `T(u)`,
with `μ_j(ξ)` unramified above `v` (the places above `d` and above the odd primes dividing the different of `F` included),
`K_j(ξ)` intertwines the Hecke operators of the hyperspecial levels with the Satake relation of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups
§2.5(d) for the pair of 3.2: an eigenform with Satake parameter `α` at `w | v` has image with the parameter `c_w·α` together
with the segment of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.2 (`c_w = 1` because `χ_V = χ_W`): no inversion (on `V = X_{j−1}^∨`, through `(g^{−1})^∨`,
the form has the parameter `α^{−1}`, 0.1 of that roadmap). At the dyadic places no Hecke relation is used.
(v) *The conjugate datum.* The complex conjugate of `ω_ξ` is the Weil representation of the same pair for the form `−B_j` and the
splitting characters `μ_j(ξ)^{−1}` (2.2a(b)); its theta kernel at a vector `φ` is the complex conjugate of the kernel of `ω_ξ` at
`conj φ`, and (i)–(iv) hold for it with every
scalar conjugated, `ρ^ξ_j`, `ρ′^ξ_j` replaced by their inverses (they have absolute value `1`), the readings holomorphic for `cΦ`, and
the Satake parameters conjugated; in particular the constant of (iii) for the conjugate datum is `conj c_K(j)`.

Route: (i) Inputs besides 3.1d: the characterisation of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5(f) (ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs
§4.4a) for every tuple, and strong approximation for the special unitary groups. At `g = h = 1` the reading of `φ⁰` is a
nonzero element of `Th(L_{j−1}, L_j)` (the functional equation is the Heisenberg invariance of the lattice vector, and the line
is one-dimensional because the polarisation is principal); every finite adelic element factors as `r·s·k` with `r`
rational, `s` a product of elements supported at single split places and `k` in the stabiliser of the standard lattice
(strong approximation for `SU`, AdelicAlgebraicGroups §4.2, and determinant classes), and the statement is carried along
each factor: rational elements by the equivariance of 3.1d(b), split places one at a time by the split-place law,
stabilisers by the character of 0.3 of that roadmap; two splitting data differ on the line by the character values of
its 0.2, so one `c₀(j)` serves all tuples. (ii) Move the lattice model at `S_sp` and the vacuum by `(g, h)`; the reading of the
moved datum is the classical combination of translates (that roadmap's 1.8, translates), and finite adelic operators
commute with the Fock operators, so the jet identity of ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5 (c) (its 1.7, conjugated) applies: on the reading, which is
entire, each creation operator `w̄_i` acts as `π^{−1}∂/∂w_i` (each normalised creation operator `√π·(s_Λ/|s_Λ|)·w̄_i` as `s_Λ·∂/∂z_i`), and
the identity for a polynomial `P` follows by induction on the word of creation operators. (iii) From (ii), the definition of
`Θ_{𝒸,𝒸′}(ξ)` (3.2: the coefficients `r_k` of the datum, the moved lattice vector) and (i) for the moved lattice vector, which
contributes `c₀(j)ρ^ξ_j(t_𝒸)ρ′^ξ_j(t′_{𝒸′})` once; the class-set formula is 1.2 of that roadmap, whose normalisation is the volume
`vol(K_∞K)`; `n_Â` compares the lattice vector with the vacuum. (iv) (iii) and ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §2.5 (d) (its §2.2,
for any additive character and residue characteristic, with the translation rule of its 0.1); at an odd split place the
finite datum `φ_{Â_v}` is the type-II lattice vector of its 0.3 (3.2), fixed by the stabilisers of `L_{j−1,v}` and `L_{j,v}`. (v) Apply
complex conjugation to (i)–(iv), with 2.2a(b) for the conjugate representation; `|ρ^ξ_j| = |ρ′^ξ_j| = 1` because `μ_j(ξ)` is unitary and
`|χ_D| = 1` (3.1b). The reading of the moved datum: ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §1.8 (*Moving the lattice*, *Translates*)
makes the reading of a moved lattice vector the complex conjugate of a section, of the same norm, of the line of the moved lattice
with the moved lift, and reads the moved Heisenberg translates; the identification of that section, for an element supported at a
single split place, with the image under the isogeny operator of 3.1d (b) times the scalar of (i) is not given here.

### 3.4 Weights of the step

`K_j(ξ)` maps `ℳ_{j−1}` into `ℳ_j`: forms of weight `Λ_{j−1}` to forms of weight `Λ_j` (multiplying by
`det^{s♯_j}`, substituting and dividing by `det^{s_div,j}` sends highest weight `λ` to `(λ − ((t+1)/2)·1, (−s_div,j)^{t+1})`; the new
Harish-Chandra entries form `S(−w_j, t+1)`), and the summand `ℱ_j` is nonzero because `ν_j(τ)` is a partition with at most
`N_{j−1}` rows (2.3(c)). The coefficient modules of `ℳ_{j−1}` and `ℳ_j` are of the same kind (3.1), so `K_{j+1}(ξ)∘K_j(ξ)` is
defined on `ℳ_{j−1}`.

Route: for any entire `Θ`, `P ↦ [y′ ↦ P(ᵗ∂_z·y′|_{N_{j−1}})Θ(0)]` maps the polynomials of column degrees `ν` that are invariant
under adding earlier columns to later ones to polynomials in `y′` of column degrees `(ν, 0^{t+1})` with the same invariance:
the substitution `X ↦ ᵗ∂_z·y′|_{N_{j−1}}` followed by a linear functional in `z` preserves column degrees and the invariance under
column operations, with zeros appended; multiplication by `det^{s♯_j}` and division by `det^{s_div,j}` shift the column weights
to those of 2.3(b) (`Λ_{j−1,N_{j−1}} + s♯_j ≥ 0` by 2.3(c)). Equivariance under the representatives and the levels: 3.1d(b) and the
admissibility (A1)–(A3) of the finite datum. No dictionary of `K_∞`-types is needed; the comparison of 3.2 gives the same weights.

*Check.* `s_div,j − s♯_j = (t + 1)/2`: the old highest weights drop by exactly the half-rank jump, which is the shift
`ρ_{N_j} − ρ_{N_{j−1}}` on the first `N_{j−1}` coordinates, so the Harish-Chandra parameters of the earlier levels persist.

*KNOWN* in kind (Kashiwara–Vergne). *Needs:* 2.3, 3.1, 3.1d, 3.2.

### 3.5 The chain lift

*Definitions.* A *system of data* of the chain for the tuple `ξ` consists of finite index sets
`A_1, …, A_{m−1}` (with `A_0 = A_m = {∗}`) and, for `1 ≤ j ≤ m` and `(α, α′) ∈ A_{j−1} × A_j`, an admissible finite datum
`φ^{(α,α′)}_j` of step `j` (3.2), the zero datum allowed; `K_j(ξ; φ)` denotes the step operator of 3.2 for the datum `φ`. The *chain
lift* of the system is

`col_ξ := Σ_{(α_1, …, α_{m−1}) ∈ A_1 × ⋯ × A_{m−1}} K_m(ξ; φ^{(α_{m−1},∗)}_m) ∘ ⋯ ∘ K_2(ξ; φ^{(α_1,α_2)}_2) ∘ K_1(ξ; φ^{(∗,α_1)}_1) : ℳ_0(u, ξ) → ℳ_m(u, ξ)`,

the class sums of each `K_j` taken for the column levels of 2.9–2.9c; a single datum per step is the case `A_j = {∗}`, where
`col_ξ = K_m(ξ) ∘ ⋯ ∘ K_1(ξ)`. `col := col_1`. (The data of the chain form such a system, 3.2: for `1 ≤ j ≤ m − 2`, `A_j` is the set
of choices of a chart of stage `j` (3.2) at each twist place (a point for the base tuple), and `A_{m−1}` the set of cosets of the line
variable, stage `m − 1` having one chart.)

*API.* (a) `col_ξ` is a class-set kernel operator whose kernel is the sum over the index paths of the composite kernels (the
sum over intermediate classes `𝒸_1, …, 𝒸_{m−1}` of the products of the kernels of the steps, with weights
`∏_{i=1}^{m−1}|Γ_{𝒸_i}|^{−1}`, and weights `|Γ_{𝒸_0}|^{−1}`). (b) The adjoint of `K_j(ξ; φ)` for the Petersson products of 3.1 is the
class-set operator with the adjoint kernels and weights `|Γ_{𝒸′}|^{−1}`; `col_ξ^*` is the sum over the index paths of the reversed
composites of the adjoints. (c) At the places of 3.3(iv) for every step, `col_ξ` intertwines the Hecke operators with the
composite of the Satake relations of the steps: the parameter of the image of an eigenform is the union of `α` (up to the
scalars `c_w`) and the `m` segments. (d) Let `ξ` be unramified everywhere and the two tuples have the same system of data, and
let `m^{(i)}` be the multiplication by `ξ̃(det t_𝒸)` on level `i`. Then `K_j(ξ; φ) = (m^{(j)})^{s} ∘ K_j(1; φ) ∘ (m^{(j−1)})^{−s}` for every
datum (`s` the sign of 2.5, `+1` for the chain), hence `col_ξ = (m^{(m)})^{s}∘col∘(m^{(0)})^{−s}`. (e) *Levels.* Replacing
the level of an intermediate
stage `j` (`1 ≤ j ≤ m − 1`) by an open subgroup of index `ι` with the restricted character multiplies each composite through
that stage by `ι` on forms of the larger level. So integrality statements for `col_ξ` hold for every choice of
intermediate levels under which the data are admissible, while the constants of a composite depend on the levels: the
constants `c_K(j)` of 3.3 (iii) and the index factors `ι`.

Route: DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5b (composition, adjoint) and the class-set form of
ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §1.4 (the adjoint kernel `k_φ(t, t′)^*`; only this form is used, not its description of the
adjoint as a lift for another pair), applied to each composite and summed; (c) from 3.3(iv), for each composite; (d) from
3.2 API (a): `ρ^ξ_j/ρ^1_j = ξ̃(det)^{−s}` and `ρ′^ξ_j/ρ′^1_j = ξ̃(det)^{s}`, and the intermediate factors cancel in each composite
because the sign is the same at every step. (e) The class sum `Σ_𝒸 |Γ_𝒸|^{−1}F(t_𝒸)` over the classes of a level `K` is the integral of
`F` over `U(F)\U(𝔸_f)` for the Haar measure giving `K` volume `1`; for `K′ ⊂ K` of index `ι` and a `K`-invariant `F` the sum over the
classes of `K′` is `ι` times the sum over those of `K`. Not given here: the values of the constants of the chain lift for the
levels of 2.9–2.9c (the constants `c_K(j)` of 3.3 (iii), the index factors `ι` of (e) and the chart weights of the sub-roadmap SeesawForThetaChains), and
which charts of a twist place carry a nonzero datum.

*NEW* (object), elementary API. The chain lift: [FormalProof], `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataKernels.lean`
(`Formalism.BasicNotions.ColumnChain.col`). *Needs:* 3.2, 3.3, 3.4; DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5b;
ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups §1.4.

### 3.6 Integrality of the chain lift

Let `D_Γ` be the least common multiple of the orders of the stabilisers `Γ_𝒸` of all classes of
all levels `0 ≤ j ≤ m` and of `X^h`. Then `D_Γ` divides `minkowskiBound(2f N_m)`, and `log D_Γ ≤ C f N_m log(f N_m)`. Let `𝔭` be a
finite place of `𝕃`, and `col_ξ` the chain lift of a system of data (3.5). If, for every step `j`, every datum `φ^{(α,α′)}_j` of the
system and all classes, the kernel of `K_j(ξ; φ^{(α,α′)}_j)` at `(𝒸, 𝒸′)` (the summand of 3.2 without the factor `|Γ_𝒸|^{−1}`) maps the integral values at `𝒸` (3.1) to integral values at `𝒸′` locally at `𝔭`, then `D_Γ^m·col_ξ` maps
`ℳ_0^{int}` into `ℳ_m^{int}` locally at `𝔭`; and if the kernel of step `j` maps into `𝔭^{o_j}` times the integral values, then
`col_ξ` maps `ℳ_0^{int}` into `𝔭^{Σ_j o_j − m·v_𝔭(D_Γ)}ℳ_m^{int}` locally at `𝔭`.

Route: each class weight `|Γ_𝒸|^{−1}` lies in `D_Γ^{−1}ℤ`; composition (DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5b), for each composite
of the sum of 3.5, and the sum of integral maps is integral. A stabiliser `Γ_𝒸`
acts faithfully on `L_𝒸 ≅ ℤ^{2fN_j}`, so `|Γ_𝒸|` divides `minkowskiBound(2fN_j)` (HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2(c),
FiniteGroupInvariants §10.2), and `minkowskiBound` is monotone for divisibility; the bound on `log D_Γ` is the explicit formula
for `minkowskiBound`. The stabilisers of classes of `X^h` act faithfully on lattices of rank `2f(N_m − 1)`. Every prime
dividing `D_Γ` is at most `2fN_m + 1`, so the level primes (2.8) do not divide it.

*Check.* The denominator is attained: one class with `Γ_𝒸 = {±1}`, trivial weight and a kernel equal to `1` give
`K_j 1 = 1/2`.

*KNOWN* (Minkowski; composition). [FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/DesignExistsOfChoices.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.DGamDvdF`), `Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/DataMink.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.minkM`),
`Formalism/LocalRingCardinality/RhoDetPrimeEqMuTildeMulChiPow.lean` (`Formalism.BasicNotions.FactDGam`).
*Needs:* 3.1, 3.2, 3.5; HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2; FiniteGroupInvariants §10.2;
DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5b.

### 3.7 Operators at the level prime

*Definitions and API* at a place `v | T(u)` (residue field `𝔽_v ≅ 𝔽_{T(u)}`, `q_v = T(u)`), for
forms on pairs at level `j` (2.9b), with a nontrivial additive character `χ_v` of `𝔽_v` (with values in `μ_{T(u)} ⊂ 𝕃`):

- the *diamond operators* `⟨a⟩_v`, `a ∈ 𝔽_v^×` (2.9b), and the *trace* `Tr_v := Σ_{a ∈ 𝔽_v^×} ⟨a⟩_v`; the *new part* at `v` is `ker Tr_v`,
  and `ℳ_j^{new} := ⋂_{v | T(u)} ker Tr_v`;
- the *Gauss operators* `Gs_v := Σ_{a∈𝔽_v^×} χ_v(a)·⟨a⟩_v` and `Gs_v^D := Σ_{a∈𝔽_v^×} χ_v(−a)·⟨a^{−1}⟩_v`;
- the *finite Fourier transform* `(FF_vφ)(𝔡) := Σ_{fn₂} χ_v(⟨fn₂, ϖ_v y⟩)·φ(ker fn, fn₂)` for a pair `𝔡 = (Λ, fn)`, the sum over the
  nonzero functionals `fn₂` of the lattice `ker fn`, where `y ∈ Λ` is any vector with `fn(y) = 1` (so `ϖ_v y ∈ ker fn`; the inputs have
  as lattice the kernel of the output), and its adjoint `(FF_v^*φ)(𝔡) := Σ_{𝔡₁ : ker fn₁ = Λ} χ_v(−⟨fn, ϖ_v y₁⟩)·φ(𝔡₁)`, where `y₁ ∈ Λ₁` is any
  vector with `fn₁(y₁) = 1` (so `ϖ_v y₁ ∈ Λ`; the inputs have as kernel the lattice of the output);
- the operator `(U_vφ)(𝔡) := Σ_{𝔡₁ : ker fn₁ = Λ, ⟨fn, ϖ_v y₁⟩ = 1} φ(𝔡₁)` with its transpose `D_v`.

*API.* `⟨·⟩_v` is an action of `𝔽_v^×` commuting with the Hecke operators away from `T(u)`; `Tr_v/(q_v − 1)` is the orthogonal
projector onto the invariants, so `ker Tr_v` is the sum of the nontrivial eigenspaces. `Gs_v∘Gs_v^D = q_v − Tr_v` on all forms;
on an eigenspace with nontrivial character `Gs_v` acts by a Gauss sum, of absolute value `q_v^{1/2}`. On new forms
`FF_v = D_v∘Gs_v` and `FF_v^* = U_v∘Gs_v^D`; `FF_v` and `FF_v^*` are adjoint for the counting measures on pairs, commute with every
`⟨a⟩_v`, preserve new forms, and on new forms `‖FF_vφ‖² = ‖FF_v^*φ‖² = q_v^{N_j}‖φ‖²` (*Plancherel*: over a fixed lattice they are the
Fourier transforms of `𝔽_v^{N_j}` between the functions of the nonzero functionals and of the nonzero vectors, which on new forms
lose nothing). `K_j(ξ)` commutes with the diamond operators and preserves new forms. `U_v` and `D_v` commute with every
`⟨a⟩_v` and preserve new forms, and on new forms `D_v∘U_v = U_v∘D_v = q_v^{N_j−1}·id`.

Route: finite Fourier analysis on `Λ/ϖΛ`. For `FF_v^*` fix the lattice `Λ` of the output: a pair `𝔡₁` with `ker fn₁ = Λ` is determined by
the vector `y₁ ∈ ϖ^{−1}Λ/Λ` with `fn₁(y₁) = 1`, the outputs by the functionals `fn` of `Λ/ϖΛ`, and `⟨fn, ϖy₁⟩` is the natural pairing;
for `FF_v` fix `Λ₂ := ker fn`: the outputs with this kernel are determined by the nonzero vectors `ϖy ∈ Λ₂/ϖΛ₂` (`Λ = Λ₂ + 𝒪_w y`), the
inputs by the functionals of `Λ₂/ϖΛ₂`; exchanging the two sums shows that they are adjoint. Replacing a functional by `a` times
it and the vector by `a^{−1}` times it leaves the pairing unchanged, whence the commutation with the diamond operators. On new
forms the value of the full Fourier transform at zero, a sum of `φ` over the nonzero points (sum over lines of the sums over
`𝔽_v^×`), vanishes, so the restriction loses nothing. `Gs_v∘Gs_v^D = Σ_{a,a′}χ_v(a − a′)⟨a/a′⟩_v = Σ_b ⟨b⟩_v Σ_{a′}χ_v(a′(b − 1))`, which is `q_v − 1` at `b = 1`
and `−1` otherwise; on a `⟨·⟩_v`-eigenspace of character `η ≠ 1`, `Gs_v` acts by `Σ_a χ_v(a)η(a)`. Sorting by the value of the
pairing: `(D_vGs_vφ)(Λ, fn) = Σ_{fn₂ : ⟨fn₂, ϖy⟩ ≠ 0} χ_v(⟨fn₂, ϖy⟩)φ(ker fn, fn₂)` (the inputs of `D_v` have as lattice the kernel of
the output, a diamond keeps the lattice) and `(U_vGs_v^Dφ)(Λ, fn) = Σ_{𝔡₁ : ker fn₁ = Λ, ⟨fn, ϖy₁⟩ ≠ 0} χ_v(−⟨fn, ϖy₁⟩)φ(𝔡₁)`; the terms
with pairing `0` form unions of diamond orbits, whose sums vanish on new forms. The commutation of `K_j(ξ)` with the diamond operators is the invariance
of the datum `φ_{T,v}` under the simultaneous diamond operators (3.2, (A2) and Lemma (i)); it then preserves the eigenspaces of
`⟨·⟩_v`, hence new forms. `U_v∘D_v`: for a pair `(Λ, fn)` it is `Σ_{fn′} n(fn′)·φ(Λ, fn′)`, `n(fn′)` the number of `y₁ ∈ ϖ^{−1}Λ/Λ` with
`fn(ϖy₁) = fn′(ϖy₁) = 1`, which is `q_v^{N_j−1}` for `fn′ = fn`, `0` for `fn′ = a·fn` with `a ≠ 1`, and `q_v^{N_j−2}` otherwise; on new
forms the sums over the lines `𝔽_v^×fn′` vanish, which leaves `q_v^{N_j−1}φ(Λ, fn)`. `D_v∘U_v` likewise, with the roles of the vectors
`y₁` and the functionals exchanged; replacing `fn` by `a·fn` and `y₁` by `a^{−1}y₁` gives the commutation with `⟨a⟩_v`.

*Check.* `Tr_v` kills a form on which some `⟨a⟩_v` acts by a nontrivial character, and on the invariants
`Tr_v = (q_v − 1)·id`: for `q_v = 3`, `𝔽_v^× = {±1}` and `ker Tr_v` is the `−1` eigenspace of `⟨−1⟩_v`, on which `Gs_v` acts by
`χ_v(1) − χ_v(−1) = ζ₃ − ζ₃^{−1}`, of absolute value `√3` (with a multiplicative character in place of `χ_v` the sum would be
`q_v − 1 = 2`). For `N_j = 1` and `q_v = 3` the new functions of the nonzero vectors `y ∈ {1, −1}` of `𝔽_3` are the multiples of
`φ(y) = y`, and `(FF_v^*φ)(f) = χ_v(−f) − χ_v(f)`, of squared absolute value `3` at `f = ±1`; so `Σ_f |FF_v^*φ(f)|² = 6 = 3·Σ_y |φ(y)|²`.
The two transforms have different incidences: in rank one, for `q_v = 3`, a new function supported on the pairs with lattice
`ϖ^{−1}Λ` (values `1`, `−1` on its two functionals) has, at an output with lattice `Λ`, `FF_v^*φ = ±(ζ₃ − ζ₃^{−1})` (the inputs of `FF_v^*`
have the kernel `Λ`, hence the lattice `ϖ^{−1}Λ`), while `FF_vφ = D_vGs_vφ = 0` there (its inputs have the lattice `ϖΛ`); so
`FF_v^* = D_v∘Gs_v` is false.

*NEW* (definitions; KNOWN finite Fourier analysis). *Needs:* 2.9b, 3.1, 3.2.

### 3.8 Source lattices

*Definitions.* For a word `u`: `ℳ_0(u) := ℳ_0(u, 1)`, the space of forms on `U(X_0)` of
weight `λ_u` and level `K_u` (2.9b) with the character induced by `χ₂` (at `v | T(u)`: forms on pairs); its *new part*
`ℳ_0^{new}(u) := ⋂_{v|T(u)} ker Tr_v` (3.7). The *full source lattice* is `M_u^{all} := ℳ_0(u)^{int}` (3.1: values in the Weyl
lattices) and the *source lattice* `M_u := M_u^{all} ∩ ℳ_0^{new}(u)`, a saturated `𝒪_𝕃`-sublattice; both are Hermitian
`𝒪_𝕃`-lattices with the Petersson metric of 3.1 at every embedding `σ` of `𝕃`. `d_u^{all} := rk M_u^{all}`, `d_u := rk M_u`.

*Theorem.* Assume `2fk + 1 < g₀`. (a) *Neatness:* `U(X_0)(F) ∩ γK_uγ^{−1} = 1` for every `γ ∈ U(X_0)(𝔸_f)`, so every class of
level `K_u` has trivial stabiliser. (b) *Rank formulas:* `d_u^{all} = |Cl_0(u)|·dim V_{λ_u}`,
`|Cl_0(u)| = mass(𝒪_E^k)·i_col·(T(u)^k − 1)^f`, so `d_u^{all} = ď_u^{all} = d*_u·(T(u)^k − 1)^f` (2.8); the diamond group
`∏_{v|T(u)} 𝔽_v^×` acts freely on the classes, and `d_u·(T(u) − 1)^f = d_u^{all}·(T(u) − 2)^f`, so `d_u = ď_u`. In particular
`d_u > 0`, and the rank window of 2.8 holds for `d_u^{all}` and `d_u`. (c) *Slopes at the level prime:*
`μ(M_u) = μ(M_u^{all}) − f·log(T(u) − 1)/(T(u) − 2)` and `μ_max(M_u) ≤ μ_max(M_u^{all})`.

Route: (a) `K_u` is sufficiently small (HermitianFormsAndLattices §5.2 (d)(ii); 2.9a (d)): an element of `U(X_0)(F) ∩ γK_uγ^{−1}` has
finite order and is unipotent modulo the places above `g₀`, with `g₀ > k[E:ℚ] + 1`. (b) Mass times index counts the classes of a
sufficiently small level (HermitianFormsAndLattices §§5.6(a), 5.7(c)); at each `v | T(u)` the pairs over a fixed lattice are the
`T(u)^k − 1` nonzero functionals; on a slice of the free diamond action, forms are functions on `∏_v 𝔽_v^×` with values in a
fixed space, and the new forms are those with zero sum along every coordinate. (c) On such a slice the full lattice is
`𝒪_𝕃[∏_v 𝔽_v^×] ⊗ (fixed lattice)` with the orthogonal product metric, and the zero-sum sublattice of `ℤ^{q−1}` has Gram
determinant `q − 1` and rank `q − 2`; with `μ = 2μ̂` (Conventions) and additivity of slopes on tensor products
(SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles §1.7), each of the `f` places contributes `−log(T(u) − 1)/(T(u) − 2)`; `μ_max` decreases on
saturated sublattices (SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles §2.1).

*Check.* For `T(u) = 3`, one place and one class orbit, the new forms are the functions `φ` on `{±1}` with
`φ(1) + φ(−1) = 0`: rank `1 = 2·(3 − 2)/(3 − 1)`, and the lattice `ℤ·(1, −1)` has squared covolume `2`, slope
`μ = −log 2 = −log(T − 1)/(T − 2)`.

(`Formalism.BasicNotions.ColumnChain.StabZero`); (b): [FormalProof],

(`Formalism.MainNotions.LocalQuadraticParts.adm26`), `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/LinkMin.lean`

### 3.9 The top period

*Definitions.* For a word `u`: the *top weight* `σ(τ)` of 2.3 on `U(X^h)` and, at each `τ`,
`n_τ := |Λ_m(τ)| − |σ(τ)|` (`|λ| = Σ_i λ_i`; entries indexed from `0`, as in 2.3).

- *Degree.* By interlacing (2.3(e)), `n_τ = Λ_{m,0}(τ) − D₀(τ)` with `D₀(τ) := Σ_{i=0}^{N_m−2} (σ_i(τ) − Λ_{m,i+1}(τ)) ≥ 0`; `n_τ` is large
  at the tops and may be negative at the other places.
- *Branching map.* For each `τ`, let `ξ_τ` be the product over `i` of the leading minor of size `i + 1` to the power
  `σ_i(τ) − Λ_{m,i+1}(τ)` and of the minor on the rows `0, …, i − 1, N_m − 1` and the columns `0, …, i` to the power
  `Λ_{m,i}(τ) − σ_i(τ)`, a highest-weight vector of weight `σ(τ)` for `GL_{N_m−1}` in `∇_ℤ(Λ_m(τ))`,
  and `c_{Br,τ}` the integer `∏_i ∏_{v∈R_i∖{σ_i(τ)}} ∏_j ((v − i) − (σ_j(τ) − j))`, `R_i := [Λ_{m,i+1}(τ), min(Λ_{m,i}(τ), Λ_{m,i+1}(τ) + D₀(τ))] ∩ ℤ`
  (every factor is nonzero: for `v ∈ R_i` and `j ≠ i` the numbers `σ_j − j` lie outside `[Λ_{m,i+1} − i, Λ_{m,i} − i]` by interlacing).
  `io_τ : V_{σ(τ)} → V_{Λ_m(τ)}` is the `GL_{N_m−1}`-equivariant map (on the polynomial models of 3.1) with `io_τ(v_{σ(τ)}) = c_{Br,τ}·ξ_τ`; it
  maps `∇_ℤ(σ(τ))` into `∇_ℤ(Λ_m(τ))` (integral branching for `GL_N ↓ GL_{N−1}`, IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups §1.1 (a), for
  the interlacing pair `(Λ_m(τ), σ(τ))`; IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups §1.1 (f) gives `io_τ` explicitly on the bideterminants). The *branching map* `br_τ : V_{Λ_m(τ)} → V_{σ(τ)}` is the adjoint of `io_τ` for the forms `⟨·,·⟩` of 3.1.
  It is `GL_{N_m−1}`-equivariant, nonzero (so the projection onto the unique constituent `V_{σ(τ)}`, 2.3(e), up to a scalar), homogeneous of
  degree `n_τ` in the last coordinate, and maps `Δ_ℤ(Λ_m(τ))` into `Δ_ℤ(σ(τ))` (by duality; IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups §1.1 (b), (c)). With `D₁(τ) := Σ_i #(R_i ∖ {σ_i(τ)})` (so
  `c_{Br,τ}` is a product of `(N_m − 1)·D₁(τ)` factors) and the *spread* `spread(λ) := λ_0 − λ_{N−1} + N` of a dominant weight `λ` of
  `GL_N`, `|c_{Br,τ}| ≤ spread(Λ_m(τ))^{(N_m−1)D₁(τ)} ≤ (2·spread(Λ_m(τ)))^{N_m·D₁(τ)}`, each factor having absolute value at most
  `Λ_{m,0}(τ) − Λ_{m,N_m−1}(τ) + N_m − 2` (IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups §1.1 (d)). Exactly, `br_τ∘io_τ = c_{Br,τ}²·⟨ξ_τ, ξ_τ⟩_{Λ_m(τ)}·id` on `V_{σ(τ)}`, with
  `⟨ξ_τ, ξ_τ⟩_{Λ_m(τ)} ≤ dim V_{Λ_m(τ)}` (IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups §1.1 (e)). The integer `c_{Br,τ}` is not in general the least one for which the scaled
  embedding is integral (Check).
- *The class of a class of `U(X^h)`.* For a class `𝒸` of `U(X^h)` (class lattice `L_𝒸`, self-dual; levels of 2.9a–2.9c), `𝒸̃` is the class
  of level `m` of its representative `t_𝒸 ∈ U(X^h)(𝔸_f) ⊂ U(X_m)(𝔸_f)` (the stabiliser of `z_u`). Its class lattice is `L_𝒸 ⊕ 𝒪_E z_u`
  (`z_u = π_u e_{N_m}`, `⟨z_u, z_u⟩ = T(u)`, 2.8), with, at each `v | T(u)`, the pair `t_𝒸·𝔡₀^{(m)}`, whose functional on the dual lattice is
  `y ↦ ⟨z_u, y⟩` modulo the place `w` above `π_u` (2.9b). So at `v | T(u)` the unimodular lattice of `𝒸̃` is `L_𝒸 ⊕ 𝒪_E e_{N_m}`, the
  class lattice `L_𝒸 ⊕ π_u𝒪_E e_{N_m}` is the kernel of the functionals, the dual class lattice is `L_𝒸 ⊕ π̄_u^{−1}𝒪_E e_{N_m}`, and
  `⟨z_u, ·⟩ = π̄_u·⟨e_{N_m}, ·⟩` is the coordinate dual to `π̄_u^{−1}e_{N_m}`: the element `π̄_u` is a unit at the places above `π_u` (those
  carrying the functionals) and is divisible exactly by the places above `π̄_u`. The places of `E` above `π_u𝒪_K` are `f` places of
  degree one, no two of them conjugate (`T(u)` splits completely in `E`, 2.8); `z_u` is divisible by them, and `π̄_u𝒪_E` is the product
  of their conjugates. At the type primes and twist places, `U(X^h)(F_v) ∩ K^X_m(v)` is the level of the forms on `U(X^h)`, with the
  restricted character (2.9a(d), 2.9c).
- *Restriction and period.* For a form `φ′` of level `m`, `(res φ′)(𝒸) := (⊗_τ br_τ)(φ′(𝒸̃))`, the value `φ′(𝒸̃)` being written in the
  coordinates of 3.1 at `𝒸̃` (a basis of the dual class lattice whose last coordinate is `⟨z_u, ·⟩`); in the standard coordinates of
  `e_{N_m}` this is `⊗_τ τ(π̄_u)^{−n_τ}·br_τ`, because the last coordinate is `⟨z_u, ·⟩ = τ(π̄_u)·⟨e_{N_m}, ·⟩` at `τ`. For a form `Υ` on
  `U(X^h)` of weight `σ`,

  `P̃(φ′, Υ) := ⟨Υ, res φ′⟩_{X^h} = Σ_𝒸 |Γ_𝒸|^{−1}⟨Υ(𝒸), (res φ′)(𝒸)⟩_σ`,  `P̃_ξ(φ, Υ) := P̃(col_ξ φ, Υ)`.

`P̃` is linear in the level-`m` form, hence (the step operators being linear, 3.2) linear in the column `φ`: a scalar of `𝕃` comes out
of the column slot unchanged, so a bound for the period at a place `𝔭` uses the column at the same place `𝔭`. It is conjugate-linear
in `Υ` (the Hermitian product is conjugate-linear in its first variable). This is the only definition of the top period.
`P̃` is a definite, vector-valued form of the adjacent (`r = 0`) period of GanGrossPrasadUnitaryGroups §4.1 for the pair
`X^h ⊂ X_m`: the integral over `[U(X^h)]` is the class sum with the weights `|Γ_𝒸|^{−1}`, and the two forms are paired through
`br_τ` and the Hermitian product.

- *The bilinear form of the period.* Let `β_λ` be the `ℚ`-bilinear contravariant form on `𝒫_λ` whose Hermitian form is `⟨·,·⟩_λ`
  (3.1): `⟨P, Q⟩_λ = β_λ(P^c, Q)`, `P^c` the polynomial with complex conjugate coefficients; `β_σ := ⊗_τ β_{σ(τ)}`. For a form `Υ` on
  `U(X^h)` its *coefficient conjugate* is `Υ^c(t, y) := (Υ(t, ·))^c(y)` (for `𝕃`-rational `Υ`: `c_𝕃` applied to the coefficients). It
  satisfies `Υ^c(γtκ, γ̄_∞y) = χ̄(κ)Υ^c(t, y)`: it is a form of weight `σ` for the conjugate embeddings `c∘τ` (CM type `cΦ`) with the
  conjugate character. For a level-`m` form `φ′` and a form `Υ′` for `cΦ` put `P^{bil}(φ′, Υ′) := Σ_𝒸 |Γ_𝒸|^{−1}β_σ(Υ′(𝒸), (res φ′)(𝒸))`;
  it is well defined (`β_λ(ḡP, gQ) = β_λ(P, Q)` for unitary `g`), `𝕃`-bilinear, and `P̃(φ′, Υ) = P^{bil}(φ′, Υ^c)`. So a bilinear period
  whose row argument is a form for `cΦ` is `P̃` read through `Υ ↦ Υ^c`: the conjugation is that of the coefficients of the row form
  (equivalently, of its CM type), never of the column or of the place at which the column is bounded; it is not the pointwise
  complex conjugation of the function (`Ῡ(t, y) = Υ^c(t, ȳ)` is antiholomorphic in the frame). `Υ^c` is integral at `𝔭` exactly when
  `Υ` is integral at `c_𝕃(𝔭)`.

*API.* (a) `𝒸 ↦ 𝒸̃` is injective on classes, `Γ_𝒸 → Γ_{𝒸̃}` is a bijection, and `res φ′` is a form on `U(X^h)` of the level of 2.9a–2.9c. (b)
`res` maps rational forms to rational forms, and `ℳ_m^{int}` (word `u`, base tuple) into the forms on `U(X^h)` with values in the Weyl
lattices (`ℳ^{int}` of 3.1 for `X^h`), at every finite place, the places above `T(u)` included; for a tuple `ξ` the same holds locally
at every place `𝔭` whose residue characteristic is not below `Q(ξ)`. (c) For every embedding `σ_𝕃 : 𝕃 → ℂ`,
`‖res φ′‖²_{σ_𝕃} ≤ (∏_τ T(u)^{−n_τ}β_τ)·‖φ′‖²_{σ_𝕃}`, `β_τ` the squared operator norm of `br_τ` for the forms of 3.1 (standard
coordinates), `β_τ = c_{Br,τ}²·⟨ξ_τ, ξ_τ⟩_{Λ_m(τ)}` (it contains the square of the branching integer); `β_τ` does not depend on `𝒸`, and
`|σ_𝕃(π_u)|² = T(u)` at every embedding. The factor `T(u)^{−n_τ}` is the archimedean side of the coordinate `⟨z_u, ·⟩` of (b) (product
formula); it is not a cost: the restriction is integral at every finite place, and the cost of the level prime lies in the column
steps. (d) For rational `φ′` and `Υ`, `P̃(φ′, Υ) ∈ σ₀(𝕃)`, and `σ₀^{−1}P̃` is `𝕃`-linear in `φ′` and `c_𝕃`-semilinear in `Υ`.

Route: (a) The class lattice `L_𝒸 ⊕ 𝒪_E z_u` of `𝒸̃` determines `L_𝒸`. If `t_{𝒸′} = γt_𝒸κ` with `γ ∈ U(X_m)(F)` and `κ` in the level of `X_m`,
then `γ` carries the class lattice of `𝒸̃` to that of `𝒸̃′` and the functional of the pair to the functional; the vectors of norm `T(u)` of
`L ∩ T(u)L^∨` are the `ζz_u`, `ζ ∈ μ_E` (positivity of the form and `T(u) > 1`), so `γz_u = ζz_u` with `ζ ≡ 1` modulo the place above `π_u`,
hence `ζ = 1` because `T(u) ∤ |μ_E|`; so `γ ∈ U(X^h)(F)`, and `κ = t_𝒸^{−1}γ^{−1}t_{𝒸′} ∈ U(X^h)(𝔸_f)` lies in the level of `U(X^h)` because
`U(X^h) ∩ K^X_m(v)` is that level at the type primes and twist places (2.9a(d), 2.9c) and the stabiliser of `L_𝒸 ⊕ 𝒪_Ez_u` meets `U(X^h)` in
that of `L_𝒸` elsewhere; the same argument for `𝒸′ = 𝒸` gives `Γ_{𝒸̃} = Γ_𝒸`. `res φ′` has the level of `U(X^h)` because the characters of the
levels of `X_m` restrict to those of `U(X^h)`, and `br_τ` is `GL_{N_m−1}`-equivariant. (b) In the integral coordinates of 3.1 `res` is the
same polynomial map `⊗_τ br_τ` at every place, and `br_τ` maps `Δ_ℤ` into `Δ_ℤ` (integral branching, by duality); integrality holds at the
places above `T(u)` because the coordinates at `𝒸̃` are taken in the dual class lattice, whose last coordinate is `⟨z_u, ·⟩`. (c)
Cauchy–Schwarz over the classes with the weights `1/|Γ_𝒸| = 1/|Γ_{𝒸̃}|` and injectivity of `𝒸 ↦ 𝒸̃`; the price of the coordinate `⟨z_u, ·⟩`
against the orthonormal coordinate is `T(u)^{−n_τ}` in each archimedean norm (`br_τ` is homogeneous of degree `n_τ` in the last coordinate
and `|τ(π̄_u)|² = T(u)`); `br_τ∘br_τ^* = io_τ^*∘io_τ` is the scalar `⟨io_τv_σ, io_τv_σ⟩ = c_{Br,τ}²⟨ξ_τ, ξ_τ⟩` on the irreducible `V_{σ(τ)}`
(Schur; `⟨v_σ, v_σ⟩_σ = 1`), which is `β_τ`. (d) The Hermitian product is conjugate-linear in its first variable, here `Υ`, and the
contravariant form is `ℚ`-rational on the polynomial modules. The bound on `|c_{Br,τ}|`: there are `(N_m − 1)D₁(τ)` factors, each of absolute
value at most `Λ_{m,0} − Λ_{m,N_m−1} + N_m − 2 < spread(Λ_m(τ))`.

*Checks.* (i) `GL₂ ↓ GL₁` (`N_m = 2`): `D₀ = σ_0 − Λ_{m,1}`, `R_0 ∖ {σ_0} = [Λ_{m,1}, σ_0 − 1]`, `D₁ = D₀` and `c_{Br} = (−1)^{D₀}D₀!`; each
subspace of fixed degree in the last coordinate is a single type, and the equivariant map with `v_σ ↦ ξ` is already integral,
so `c_{Br}` exceeds the least admissible constant `1` by the factor `D₀!`. (ii) The `+N` in the spread is needed: for `N = 6`,
`λ = (1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0)`, `σ = (1, 1, 1, 1, 1)` one has `D₀ = D₁ = 1`, `R_i ∖ {σ_i} = ∅` for `i < 4`, `R_4 ∖ {σ_4} = {0}` and
`c_{Br} = ∏_{j=0}^{4}(j − 5) = −120`, which exceeds `(2(λ_0 − λ_5))^{N·D₁} = 64` but not `spread(λ)^{5} = 7⁵`.

*NEW* (object). [FormalProof], `Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalSchurBounds/ProductFirstZerothParts.lean`
(`Formalism.MainNotions.product_first_zeroth_parts.Conclusion6`),
`Formalism/LocalToGlobal/Columns/FiniteSumNonzeroLargeDepth.lean` (`Formalism.L2.Col.Br.cBr`),
`Formalism/PeriodIntegrals/UnitaryGroupMassFormula/GelfandTsetlinTopCoefficient/NumMore.lean` (`PI.GTNum.abs_cBr_le_cBd`).
*Needs:* 2.3, 2.8, 2.9, 2.9a, 2.9b, 2.9c, 3.1, 3.5; ClassicalGroups §6.1 (branching
`GL_N ↓ GL_{N−1}`); IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups §1.1 (integral branching).

### 3.10 Integrality of the top period

For `φ′ ∈ ℳ_m^{int}` (word `u`, base tuple; values in the Weyl lattices) and a form `Υ` on `U(X^h)` of
weight `σ` with values in the dual Weyl lattices at every class (`ℳ^{∨}` of 3.1 for `X^h`):
`v_𝔭(σ₀^{−1}P̃(φ′, Υ)) ≥ −e_𝔭·v_{p(𝔭)}(D_Γ)` at every finite place `𝔭` of `𝕃`. Locally: if `φ′` is integral at `𝔭` and `Υ` at `c_𝕃(𝔭)`,
the same bound holds at `𝔭`; in the bilinear form of 3.9, `v_𝔭(P^{bil}(φ′, Υ^c)) ≥ −e_𝔭·v_{p(𝔭)}(D_Γ)` for `φ′` and `Υ^c` integral at
`𝔭`. For a tuple `ξ` the same holds at every `𝔭` whose residue characteristic is not below `Q(ξ)`.

Route: 3.9(b) puts `res φ′` in the Weyl lattices; the class-set pairing of a Weyl-lattice-valued and a dual-Weyl-lattice-valued form
on `U(X^h)` takes values in `D^{−1}𝒪_𝕃`, `D` the least common multiple of the stabiliser orders (DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics
§7.5a, perfect pairing, with the lattices of 3.1, which are dual for `⟨·,·⟩_σ` because the class lattices `L_𝒸` are self-dual);
the stabilisers of classes of `X^h` inject into those of `X_m` (3.9(a)), whose orders divide `D_Γ`; conjugate-linearity in `Υ`
moves the integrality of `Υ` from `c_𝕃(𝔭)` to `𝔭`, while `φ′` enters linearly.

*Check.* The roles of the two lattices cannot be exchanged: for `GL_2` and `σ = (2, 0)` (3.1 Check (iv)) two `∇_ℤ`-valued values
`xy`, `xy` pair to `1/2`, while `Δ_ℤ` pairs integrally with `∇_ℤ`.

*KNOWN* (stabiliser denominators). *Needs:* 3.1, 3.6, 3.9; DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §7.5a.

### Examples

- *Weyl lattices* (3.1, Check (iv); 3.10): for `GL_2` and `λ = (2, 0)`, `∇_ℤ(λ) = ℤx² + ℤxy + ℤy²` and `Δ_ℤ(λ) = ℤx² + 2ℤxy + ℤy²`; two `∇_ℤ`-valued values `xy`, `xy` pair to `1/2`, while `Δ_ℤ` pairs integrally with `∇_ℤ`.
- *The constant `s₂`* (3.1c): for the lattice of discriminant `1` with complex multiplication by the maximal order of `ℚ(√−d)`, `√−d·s₂ = 1/4, 2/3, 2, 12, 38, 724` (up to a root of unity) for `d = 7, 11, 19, 43, 67, 163`, so `v_d(s₂) = −1/2`.
- *A denominator* (3.6): one class with `Γ_𝒸 = {±1}`, trivial weight and a kernel equal to `1` give `K_j 1 = 1/2`.
- *Gauss operators* (3.7): for `q_v = 3`, `ker Tr_v` is the `−1` eigenspace of `⟨−1⟩_v`, on which `Gs_v` acts by `ζ₃ − ζ₃^{−1}`, of absolute value `√3`.
- *A source lattice* (3.8): for `T(u) = 3`, one place and one class orbit, the new forms have rank `1` and the lattice `ℤ·(1, −1)` has slope `−log 2 = −log(T − 1)/(T − 2)`.
- *A branching constant* (3.9, Check (ii)): for `N = 6`, `λ = (1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0)`, `σ = (1, 1, 1, 1, 1)`, `c_{Br} = −120`.

## Downstream consumers

The later roadmaps of the chain in the family use this roadmap as follows.

- ThetaChainsAndFrobeniusPrimeSums asks `HostSplitting E` of the hosts of its chain data (§10.0 (H2)); its §10.6 fixes the order of the constants, the numbers `A_n`, `J` and `c_H` that the size predicates of 2.1 take as arguments, and the values of `δ_top`, of the orbit lengths and of `ε₀`, and states the constraints of the exponents written in the size predicates of 2.1; its §10.0 (D4) collects the conditions on the twisting datum ((T1), (T2) of 2.5, (T3) of GluedTargetAndDivisionInequality §8.20, the good twisting datum of CongruenceModulesOfThetaLifts §7.10a and the wild-kernel budget of CongruenceModulesOfThetaLifts §7.11), which ThetaChainsAndFrobeniusPrimeSums §§10.7 (b), 10.9 (c), (d) verify for the construction; ThetaChainsAndFrobeniusPrimeSums §9.4 uses the measure of the Conventions on the indefinite groups, for which `vol([U(Y_0)]) = mass(𝒪_E^r)`.
- UniformWildKernelBounds §7.6 gives the window constant `J` at the parameters of the chain.
- RowTowerAndRallisIdentities forms the row forms on `U(Y_0)` at principal level at the type primes, where the finite group `G₀` of 2.9a acts (its §5.3): the rows `ℋ^♮` at principal level and the good ones `𝔼^♮ = Π_good·ℋ^♮`, their parts `ℋ` and `𝔼 = Π_good·ℋ` of the row type level `(P_1, χ_{P_1})` of 2.9a, on which the Poincaré series, the seesaw and the target rows live, and the part `𝔼_0` of the base level `(P_0, χ_{P_0})`. Its Petersson product for forms on indefinite groups (§5.1) sums over an orthonormal basis of the lowest `K`-type; the arrows of its grid (§5.8a) are homomorphism pairs in the normal form of 2.2a with a positive rational multiple of `δ_T`; the steps of the descent of a tuple are normalised by the volume of its finite level group (§5.4).
- SeesawForThetaChains: the period pairing `B_u(φ)(x)` of its §6.1 is linear in the column `φ` and conjugate-linear in the row `x ∈ 𝔼` (§6.14 (d)); for a tuple `ξ` and a row form `Υ` of the relevant part `ℋ^{rt}_ξ` of the row type level (RowTowerAndRallisIdentities §5.4), `P̃_ξ(φ, Υ) = ζ(u, ξ)·B_u(φ)(ι_ξΥ)` with a root of unity `ζ(u, ξ)` (SeesawForThetaChains §6.14).
- LocalGainOfThetaChains §4.16: the cost of the level prime `T(u) = N(π_u)` sits at the places above `π_u` and is `0` at those above `π̄_u`.
- ArithmeticVolumesAndIntegralFormsOnUnitaryShimuraVarieties defines the cusp forms on the Shimura varieties of the steps, their lattices of integral forms and their deep covers over the coefficient field `𝕂` of 2.6 and uses its size bound; the targets of its Layer 8 that use Koecher's principle on the unitary Shimura varieties over `F` ask `f ≥ 2`, which `HostSplitting E` gives (the size conditions of 2.1 give much more, `f = 2|A| ≥ 2x^{A_n}` in the construction).

## Scope and ownership

This sub-roadmap of [RiemannHypothesisForDirichletLFunctions](../README.md) is independently definitive.

This roadmap owns Layers 2 and 3 above: the ranks, scales and size predicates of the chain; its column, row and model spaces and the normal form of the pairs they form, with the conjugate datum, the conventions for lifts and descents and the reading of a step at a real place; weight data, words, adapted and separated data and their existence; the existence criterion for Hecke characters of a CM field with prescribed restriction, local data and infinity type, and the Hecke characters of the chain; anticyclotomic twisting characters and twisting data; the coefficient field `𝕂` with its size bound and the field of places `𝕃`; the type, auxiliary and level primes; the level structures at the unramified places, the type primes, the level primes and the twist places; chain forms and their integral structures; the theta data, the character `χ_D`, the reduced theta function of `ℂ/Λ_K` with its integrality and the CM theta family; the step operator, its identification with a class-set theta lift and its weights; the chain lift and its integrality; the operators at the level prime; the source lattices; the top period and its integrality.

Not owned here. Abelian CM hosts, their predicates, the cyclic hosts, tops and thick sets are [FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums](../FrobeniusRestrictedRieszSums/README.md) Layer 0. Unitary groups and Hermitian forms over quadratic étale algebras, their local and global classification, unimodular lattices, class sets, stabilisers and masses are [HermitianFormsAndLattices](../HermitianFormsAndLattices/README.md) Layers 2–6. The dictionary of the theta lift of a definite homomorphism pair, its lattice vectors, theta kernels, class-set lifts and Hecke relations are [ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups](../ThetaLiftsForDefiniteUnitaryGroups/README.md) Layers 0–2; the global Weil representation of a unitary pair and the archimedean correspondence of discrete series are [ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs](../ThetaKernelsForUnitaryDualPairs/README.md) §§4.4a, 3.46a. Integral branching for `GL_N ↓ GL_{N−1}` is [IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups](../IntegralWeylModulesOfGeneralLinearGroups/README.md) §1.1; slopes of Hermitian vector bundles and their base change are [SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles](../SlopesOfHermitianVectorBundles/README.md); prime ideals of a ray class in short intervals and Linnik's theorem are [RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros](../RieszSumsOverPrimesAndExceptionalZeros/README.md) §§1.7b, 2.9b; class-set integration, the perfect pairing of lattices of forms and kernel operators are [AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups](../../AutomorphicFormsOnReductiveGroups/README.md) Layer 7 with [DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics](../DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics/README.md) §§7.5a, 7.5b, and the formal degree of a discrete series is DiscreteSeriesCoefficientsAndAutomorphicMetrics §8.3; the Gelfand–Graev and Bessel-type pairs of `GL_n(𝔽_q)` and the tame labelled form are [FiniteReductiveGroups](../FiniteReductiveGroups/README.md); global class field theory is [ClassFieldTheory](../../ClassFieldTheory/README.md); Hecke characters are [GlobalNumberFields](../../../Completed/GlobalNumberFields/README.md); the Serre tensor construction and canonical Gross characters are ComplexMultiplicationAndExplicitReciprocity §§1.2, 4.3. The route of 2.6 takes the bound for the different from NumberFieldArithmetic §6.4, Minkowski's bound from Mathlib (`NumberField.exists_ideal_in_class_of_norm_le`), and the action of the automorphisms fixing `K(n)` on `ℂ/𝒪_K` with its level structure of level `n` from ShimuraVarieties §5.9. The root discriminants of composita and of Kummer steps, and the values of algebraic Hecke characters in a Kummer field, are used by that route and are stated by no roadmap on main.

The roadmaps of the family named under Downstream consumers above use these statements; the theta lifts along rows, the model steps, the periods and the slope argument are theirs.
